# Complete bounded story spines — current checkpoint

13 newly completed this continuation; 82 total retained complete spines (75 Gaṇeśa/Mudgala). Only explicit COMPLETE stories appear here; PARTIAL stories remain in graph.json and story_spines.json.

Source narration order, including recalled events and advance summaries, is preserved. Unstated family, teacher, austerity or boon fields are not guessed. Exact relationships and field-specific boon evidence remain in the linked graph tables.

## Suratha and Samādhi: loss, inquiry, worship and boons

ID: `dm-story-frame`. Scope: DM 1 frame + DM 13; all directly narrated actions and conversations of the two devotees. Newly completed this continuation: False.

All narrative scenes and conversations within this bounded arc read and source-order transitions recorded. COMPLETE denotes bounded narrative chronology, not every atomic hymn epithet or exhaustive chapter graph.

No universal corpus or critical-edition completeness. Chapter extraction remains PARTIAL; liturgical adjuncts and unexpanded hymn predicates remain queued. Future appearances are predictions and retain future modality. Narrative order is source order; primordial stories are retrospective within the frame.

1. **dm-event-001** — Mārkaṇḍeya introduces the future eighth Manu. Suratha of Caitra’s line loses to hostile kings, returns to his own city and resumes ruling his own territory, then suffers renewed enemy attack. His powerful corrupt ministers seize treasury and army even in his city. Riding away under a hunting pretext, he reaches Medhas’s hermitage yet worries about kingdom, elephant, servants and treasure. Outcome: Suratha remains attached after dispossession.. [DM 1 / Pargiter canto 81, web lines 57–61](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117155.html)

2. **dm-event-002** — Suratha asks the distressed merchant his identity. Samādhi says wife, sons and kinsmen expelled him for wealth; he still worries about their welfare. Suratha questions this affection; Samādhi admits knowledge has not made him unloving. Outcome: Their shared attachment becomes the question.. [DM 1 / Pargiter canto 81, web lines 62–69](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117155.html)

3. **dm-event-003** — They approach Medhas respectfully. Suratha asks why discrimination does not end attachment. Medhas cites hungry birds feeding young and explains Mahāmāyā’s power over even knowers; propitiated, she also grants liberating knowledge. Suratha asks her origin, nature and activity. Outcome: Medhas begins Devī’s narratives.. [DM 1 / Pargiter canto 81, web lines 72–85](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117155.html)

62. **dm-event-062** — Medhas urges refuge in Devī, giver of enjoyment, heaven and release. Suratha bows and joins Samādhi on a riverbank; they make an earthen image, recite, offer flowers, incense, fire and water, restrict food and give blood-sprinkled offerings for three years. Outcome: Their worship pleases Devī.. [DM 13 / Pargiter canto 93, web lines 53–57](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117175.html); [DM 13.1–13](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

63. **dm-event-063** — Devī appears and offers their desired gifts. Suratha requests regained rule with enemies defeated now and lasting kingship in another birth; disillusioned Samādhi requests knowledge removing attachment to I and mine. Outcome: The two devotees request different goals.. [DM 13 / Pargiter canto 93, web lines 58–63](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117175.html); [DM 13.14–18](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

64. **dm-event-064** — Devī promises Suratha his kingdom within days after killing enemies, then birth from Vivasvat as Manu Sāvarṇi; she grants Samādhi perfected knowledge. Praised by both, she disappears. Mārkaṇḍeya reiterates Suratha’s future birth as the eighth Manu. Outcome: Both boons are granted; the frame closes.. [DM 13 / Pargiter canto 93, web lines 64–71](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117175.html); [DM 13.19–29](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

## Madhu and Kaiṭabha

ID: `dm-story-madhu-kaitabha`. Scope: DM 1 nested narrative, cosmic sleep through slaying. Newly completed this continuation: False.

All narrative scenes and conversations within this bounded arc read and source-order transitions recorded. COMPLETE denotes bounded narrative chronology, not every atomic hymn epithet or exhaustive chapter graph.

No universal corpus or critical-edition completeness. Chapter extraction remains PARTIAL; liturgical adjuncts and unexpanded hymn predicates remain queued. Future appearances are predictions and retain future modality. Narrative order is source order; primordial stories are retrospective within the frame.

4. **dm-event-004** — Medhas distinguishes eternal Devī from births described when she appears for divine purposes. At cosmic dissolution Viṣṇu sleeps on Śeṣa; Madhu and Kaiṭabha arise from his ear-region and threaten lotus-seated Brahmā. Outcome: Brahmā seeks awakening of Viṣṇu.. [DM 1 / Pargiter canto 81, web lines 87–89](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117155.html)

5. **dm-event-005** — Brahmā praises the goddess as sacrificial utterance, cosmic creation, preservation and dissolution, knowledge, illusion, nature and armed power. He asks her to delude the demons and awaken Viṣṇu to kill them. Outcome: Brahmā’s invocation addresses cosmic sleep.. [DM 1 / Pargiter canto 81, web lines 90–96](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117155.html); [DM 1.73–87](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

6. **dm-event-006** — Yoganidrā leaves Viṣṇu’s eyes, mouth, nose, arms, heart and breast and appears before Brahmā. Viṣṇu awakens and fights the demons with his arms for five thousand years; deluded and intoxicated with strength, they offer him a boon. Outcome: Demons become boon-offerers.. [DM 1 / Pargiter canto 81, web lines 98–99](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117155.html); [DM 1.89–95](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

7. **dm-event-007** — Viṣṇu chooses their death at his hands. They stipulate a place where earth is not flooded; he accepts and kills them with his discus. The Sanskrit says jaghane, while Pargiter renders the bodily locus ambiguously; a precise thigh-position is reserved for review. Outcome: Madhu and Kaiṭabha are slain under their stated condition.. [DM 1 / Pargiter canto 81, web lines 100–109](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117155.html); [DM 1.96–104](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

## Mahiṣāsura battle cycle

ID: `dm-story-mahisha`. Scope: DM 2–4, divine displacement, manifestation, gifts, army combat, chiefs, transformations, death, thanksgiving and boon. Newly completed this continuation: False.

All narrative scenes and conversations within this bounded arc read and source-order transitions recorded. COMPLETE denotes bounded narrative chronology, not every atomic hymn epithet or exhaustive chapter graph.

No universal corpus or critical-edition completeness. Chapter extraction remains PARTIAL; liturgical adjuncts and unexpanded hymn predicates remain queued. Future appearances are predictions and retain future modality. Narrative order is source order; primordial stories are retrospective within the frame.

8. **dm-event-008** — After a hundred-year war Mahiṣa wins Indra’s office and divine jurisdictions; displaced gods roam earth. Led by Brahmā, they tell Śiva and Viṣṇu their defeat and seek a plan to kill him. Outcome: Displaced gods seek deliverance.. [DM 2 / Pargiter canto 82, web lines 55–57](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117157.html); [DM 2.2–8](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

9. **dm-event-009** — Divine energies converge as Devī: Śiva’s face, Yama’s hair, Viṣṇu’s arms, Moon’s breasts, Indra’s waist, Varuṇa’s legs/thighs, Earth’s hips, Brahmā’s feet, Sun’s toes, Vasus’ fingers, Kubera’s nose, Prajāpati’s teeth, Agni’s eyes, twilights’ brows and wind’s ears. Outcome: Gods’ energies manifest as a woman.. [DM 2 / Pargiter canto 82, web lines 58–60](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117157.html); [DM 2.9–19](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

10. **dm-event-010** — Śiva gives a trident; Viṣṇu a discus; Varuṇa a conch and noose; Agni a spear; wind a bow and full quivers; Indra a vajra and Airāvata’s bell; Yama a rod; Prajāpati a rosary; Brahmā a water-pot; Sun rays; Kāla sword and shield. Outcome: Devī receives divine equipment.. [DM 2 / Pargiter canto 82, web lines 61–62](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117157.html); [DM 2.20–24](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

11. **dm-event-011** — Milk Ocean supplies pearl necklace and imperishable garments; ornaments adorn Devī. Viśvakarman gives axe, diverse weapons and impenetrable armor; Ocean lotus garlands and lotus; Himavat lion and gems; Kubera a wine cup; Śeṣa a jeweled serpent necklace. Outcome: Devī is adorned and mounted.. [DM 2 / Pargiter canto 82, web lines 62–64](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117157.html); [DM 2.25–31](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

12. **dm-event-012** — Devī’s roar shakes worlds, sea, earth and mountains; gods and sages praise her. Mahiṣa musters armies and approaches its source. Her light, crown, bow sound and thousand arms fill the worlds; Cikṣura and Cāmara enter battle. Outcome: The battle begins.. [DM 2 / Pargiter canto 82, web lines 64–68](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117157.html); [DM 2.32–41](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

13. **dm-event-013** — Udagra, Mahāhanu, Asiloman, Bāṣkala, Biḍāla and huge contingents fight with missile and close weapons; Pargiter inserts Ugradarśana from Bombay’s reading where the displayed Sanskrit has no proper name. Mahiṣa leads enormous chariot, elephant and horse forces. Outcome: Named and collective contingents engage.. [DM 2 / Pargiter canto 82, web lines 69–71](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117157.html); [DM 2.41–48](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

14. **dm-event-014** — Devī cuts enemy missiles effortlessly. Her lion attacks; her breaths create energized troops carrying axes, javelins, swords and halberds, with drums and conchs. Her weapons kill and bind enemies; bell sound confounds them. Outcome: Devī’s breath-born army joins combat.. [DM 2 / Pargiter canto 82, web lines 72–75](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117157.html)

15. **dm-event-015** — Mutilated demons and armed headless bodies continue fighting or dance to martial music. Fallen forces obstruct the field and blood forms rivers. Devī, lion and emanated troops destroy the host; gods shower flowers. Outcome: Mahiṣa’s army is destroyed.. [DM 2 / Pargiter canto 82, web lines 76–79](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117157.html)

16. **dm-event-016** — Cikṣura shoots arrows; Devī cuts them, kills horses and driver, breaks bow and banner and wounds him. His sword strikes lion and Devī’s arm, then breaks. His thrown śūla and his body are shattered by hers. Outcome: Cikṣura is slain.. [DM 3 / Pargiter canto 83, web lines 55–58](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117158.html); [DM 3.2–10](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

17. **dm-event-017** — Elephant-mounted Cāmara hurls a spear; Devī’s huṃ renders it powerless, and arrows cut his next śūla. Lion leaps onto elephant, wrestles Cāmara on the ground, then springs and severs his head with a paw-blow. Outcome: Cāmara is slain.. [DM 3 / Pargiter canto 83, web lines 59–60](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117158.html); [DM 3.11–16](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

18. **dm-event-018** — Devī kills Udagra with stones/trees, Karāla with bodily blows, Uddhata with club, Bāṣkala with bhindipāla, Tāmra/Andhaka with arrows, Ugrāsya/Ugravīrya/Mahāhanu with trident, Biḍāla with sword and Durdhara/Durmukha with arrows. Outcome: Named chiefs are slain.. [DM 3 / Pargiter canto 83, web lines 61–62](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117158.html); [DM 3.17–20](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

19. **dm-event-019** — Buffalo-shaped Mahiṣa attacks troops with muzzle, hooves, tail, horns, rush, roar, circling and breath, then attacks lion. His movements shake earth, sea, clouds and mountains. Outcome: Mahiṣa confronts Devī directly.. [DM 3 / Pargiter canto 83, web lines 63–65](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117158.html); [DM 3.21–23](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

20. **dm-event-020** — Devī binds Mahiṣa. He changes buffalo to lion, then armed man as she cuts at him; arrows strike man, sword and shield. Elephant-form seizes lion; she cuts its trunk. He becomes buffalo again. Outcome: Mahiṣa’s successive forms are countered.. [DM 3 / Pargiter canto 83, web lines 66–67](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117158.html)

21. **dm-event-021** — Devī drinks and laughs; arrows break hurled mountains. She tells Mahiṣa his roaring is brief, leaps, presses his neck and strikes with śūla. As he emerges halfway from the buffalo mouth, she beheads him. Gods rejoice; celestial singers and dancers celebrate. Outcome: Mahiṣāsura is slain.. [DM 3 / Pargiter canto 83, web lines 67–73](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117158.html)

22. **dm-event-022** — Gods praise Devī’s cosmic power, knowledge, prosperity, protection and mercy in battle, including the claim that slain enemies reach heaven. They ask her gentle and fearsome forms and her weapons to protect every direction. Outcome: Gods worship the victorious Devī.. [DM 4 / Pargiter canto 84, web lines 56–152](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117161.html)

23. **dm-event-023** — Gods offer flowers, perfumes and incense. Invited to choose a boon, they ask relief whenever they remember her in calamity and prosperity for mortals singing this hymn. She agrees and disappears; Medhas introduces her later emergence from Gaurī. Outcome: Devī grants remembrance-based protection.. [DM 4 / Pargiter canto 84, web lines 155–167](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117161.html)

## Śumbha–Niśumbha battle cycle and concluding promises

ID: `dm-story-shumbha`. Scope: DM 5–12, invocation, messenger, commanders, Kālī, Mothers, Raktabīja, brothers, future manifestations and ritual conclusion. Newly completed this continuation: False.

All narrative scenes and conversations within this bounded arc read and source-order transitions recorded. COMPLETE denotes bounded narrative chronology, not every atomic hymn epithet or exhaustive chapter graph.

No universal corpus or critical-edition completeness. Chapter extraction remains PARTIAL; liturgical adjuncts and unexpanded hymn predicates remain queued. Future appearances are predictions and retain future modality. Narrative order is source order; primordial stories are retrospective within the frame.

24. **dm-event-024** — Śumbha and Niśumbha seize the three worlds, divine jurisdictions and sacrificial portions. Dispossessed gods recall Devī’s earlier promise, go to Himavat and invoke her as Viṣṇu’s māyā. Outcome: Gods invoke the previous boon.. [DM 5 / Pargiter canto 85, web lines 54–55](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117163.html)

25. **dm-event-025** — Their hymn salutes Devī in auspicious and fierce modes and as consciousness, intellect, sleep, hunger, shadow, power, thirst, patience, class, modesty, peace, faith, beauty, fortune, activity, memory, mercy, contentment, mother, error, sense-governance and pervading awareness. Outcome: The hymn asks immediate deliverance.. [DM 5 / Pargiter canto 85, web lines 57–176](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117163.html)

26. **dm-event-026** — Pārvatī arrives to bathe and asks whom gods praise. Ambikā emerges from her bodily sheath and answers that the hymn addresses her; she is named Kauśikī. Pārvatī turns dark and is called Kālikā. Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa see Ambikā. Outcome: Kauśikī manifests and is seen by the scouts.. [DM 5 / Pargiter canto 85, web lines 178–181](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117163.html); [DM 5.84–89](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

27. **dm-event-027** — Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa commend her beauty and urge seizure alongside Śumbha’s treasures: Airāvata, Pārijāta, Uccaiḥśravas, Brahmā’s vehicle, Mahāpadma, Ocean’s garland, Varuṇa’s umbrella, Prajāpati’s chariot, Utkrāntidā, Varuṇa’s noose held by Niśumbha, sea-gems and Agni’s garments. Outcome: Scouts urge Śumbha to acquire her.. [DM 5 / Pargiter canto 85, web lines 182–185](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117163.html); [DM 5.90–99](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

28. **dm-event-028** — Śumbha sends Sugrīva to secure willing arrival. The envoy claims Śumbha’s rule, sacrifices and treasures, offers marriage to him or younger Niśumbha and promises incomparable sovereignty. Outcome: The envoy delivers the marriage demand.. [DM 5 / Pargiter canto 85, web lines 188–192](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117163.html)

29. **dm-event-029** — Devī replies that her former vow requires a husband who defeats her in battle, removes her pride and equals her strength. Sugrīva mocks a lone woman’s chance and threatens dragging her by her hair; she repeats the vow and asks him to report it. Outcome: Devī makes battle her marriage condition.. [DM 5 / Pargiter canto 85, web lines 195–203](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117163.html)

30. **dm-event-030** — Sugrīva reports her response. Angry Śumbha orders Dhūmralocana to drag her by the hair and kill any rescuer, god, Yakṣa or Gandharva. He arrives with sixty thousand Asuras and threatens forcible capture. Outcome: Dhūmralocana attempts seizure.. [DM 6 / Pargiter canto 86, web lines 56–60](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117165.html)

31. **dm-event-031** — Devī asks what she can do if one sent by the Asura king, strong and with an army, takes her by force. When he charges, her huṃ reduces him to ashes. His army showers arrows, śakti spears and axes; her lion kills with paws, jaws and claws, tears bodies and drinks blood, destroying the host. Outcome: Dhūmralocana and his force are destroyed.. [DM 6 / Pargiter canto 86, web lines 61–67](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117165.html); [DM 6.7–14](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

32. **dm-event-032** — Śumbha orders Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa to bring her bound or dragged by hair; if capture is doubtful, all troops should kill her and the lion, then seize her. Outcome: Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa are dispatched.. [DM 6 / Pargiter canto 86, web lines 68–69](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117165.html)

33. **dm-event-033** — Fourfold forces approach smiling lion-seated Devī on a golden peak. Her angry face darkens; Kālī emerges from her frowning forehead with sword, noose, khaṭvāṅga, skull garland, tiger skin, gaunt body, wide mouth, tongue and sunken red eyes. Outcome: Kālī manifests against the attackers.. [DM 7 / Pargiter canto 87, web lines 56–58](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117166.html); [DM 7.2–8](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

34. **dm-event-034** — Kālī devours elephants with crews and bells, horses with fighters, chariots with drivers and enemy weapons; she crushes foes bodily and kills with sword, staff and teeth. Outcome: Kālī destroys the army.. [DM 7 / Pargiter canto 87, web lines 59–61](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117166.html); [DM 7.9–15](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

35. **dm-event-035** — Caṇḍa charges, and Muṇḍa showers arrows and discuses. Kālī laughs, seizes Caṇḍa’s hair and cuts his head, then kills Muṇḍa. Survivors flee. Presenting their heads to Caṇḍikā, she reserves Śumbha/Niśumbha for her; Caṇḍikā names her Cāmuṇḍā. Outcome: Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa die; Kālī receives a name.. [DM 7 / Pargiter canto 87, web lines 62–68](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117166.html)

36. **dm-event-036** — Śumbha musters eighty-six Daityas, eighty-four Kambūs, fifty valiant families, one hundred Dhaumra families, Kālakas, Daurhṛdas, Mauryas and Kālakeyas, and marches. Devī’s bow, bell, lion and Kālī’s cries resound; armies surround them. Outcome: Śumbha’s whole force mobilizes.. [DM 8 / Pargiter canto 88, web lines 55–58](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117167.html); [DM 8.2–11](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

37. **dm-event-037** — Divine Śaktis arrive: Brahmāṇī with swans, rosary and pot; Māheśvarī on bull with trident, snakes and moon; Kaumārī on peacock with spear; Vaiṣṇavī on Garuḍa with conch, discus, club, Śārṅga and sword; boar Vārāhī, lion-form Nārasiṃhī and thousand-eyed elephant-riding Aindrī with vajra. Outcome: Divine Śaktis join Caṇḍikā.. [DM 8 / Pargiter canto 88, web lines 59–62](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117167.html); [DM 8.12–22](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

38. **dm-event-038** — Caṇḍikā’s fierce Śakti emerges with a hundred-jackal cry and appoints Śiva envoy: restore Indra’s worlds and divine sacrifice portions, retreat to Pātāla to live, or fight and feed her jackals. Hence Śivadūtī’s name. The Asuras reject the ultimatum and attack. Outcome: Śivadūtī’s ultimatum is rejected.. [DM 8 / Pargiter canto 88, web lines 63–65](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117167.html); [DM 8.23–29](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

39. **dm-event-039** — Devī cuts missiles. Kālī strikes with śūla and staff; Brahmāṇī’s water weakens courage and force. Māheśvarī uses trident, Vaiṣṇavī discus, Kaumārī spear, Aindrī vajra; Vārāhī snout, tusks and discus; Nārasiṃhī claws/devouring; Śivadūtī laughter/devouring. Asuras flee the Mothers. Outcome: Mātṛkās rout the enemy.. [DM 8 / Pargiter canto 88, web lines 65–68](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117167.html); [DM 8.30–39](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

40. **dm-event-040** — Raktabīja advances with club. Each blood-drop falling to earth produces an equal in size and strength. Aindrī’s vajra and club, Vaiṣṇavī’s discus and other Mothers’ weapons cause multiplying blood-born fighters; he clubs them individually. Gods fear the proliferating army. Outcome: Blood-replication defeats ordinary attacks.. [DM 8 / Pargiter canto 88, web lines 69–72](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117167.html); [DM 8.40–52](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

41. **dm-event-041** — Caṇḍikā directs Cāmuṇḍā to catch the blood and devour generated Asuras. As Devī wounds Raktabīja, Kālī drinks every flow, including fighters generated in her mouth. His club causes Devī no pain; multi-weapon blows drain him bloodless. Gods rejoice and Mothers dance. Outcome: Raktabīja dies when replication is intercepted.. [DM 8 / Pargiter canto 88, web lines 73–76](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117167.html); [DM 8.53–63](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

42. **dm-event-042** — Suratha asks what followed Raktabīja’s death. Medhas narrates the brothers’ fury: Niśumbha and Śumbha advance with their armies and shower arrows; Caṇḍikā cuts their arrows and wounds them. Outcome: Brothers enter the battle.. [DM 9 / Pargiter canto 89, web lines 56–59](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117169.html); [DM 9.1–10](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

43. **dm-event-043** — Niśumbha strikes lion with sword. Devī’s kṣurapra cuts sword and eight-moon shield; discus cuts spear, fist shatters śūla, trident burns his club to ash, and arrows fell his axe-bearing advance. Outcome: Niśumbha first falls unconscious.. [DM 9 / Pargiter canto 89, web lines 60–61](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117169.html); [DM 9.11–16](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

44. **dm-event-044** — Eight-armed Śumbha advances. Conch, bow, bell, lion, Kālī’s hand-strikes and Śivadūtī’s laugh terrify forces; Devī challenges him. A great firebrand counters his flaming spear. After reciprocal arrow-cutting, Caṇḍikā’s śūla fells him unconscious. Outcome: Śumbha is temporarily incapacitated.. [DM 9 / Pargiter canto 89, web lines 62–66](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117169.html); [DM 9.17–28](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

45. **dm-event-045** — Niśumbha revives and shoots Devī, Kālī and lion. Ten thousand arms throw discuses; Devī cuts these and arrows. She cuts his club with sword and pierces his heart with śūla; another strong man emerges challenging her and is beheaded. Outcome: Niśumbha and the heart-born warrior are slain.. [DM 9 / Pargiter canto 89, web lines 67–69](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117169.html); [DM 9.29–36](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

46. **dm-event-046** — Lion, Kālī and Śivadūtī devour troops; Kaumārī’s spear, Brahmāṇī’s mantra-purified water, Māheśvarī’s trident, Vārāhī’s snout, Vaiṣṇavī’s discus and Aindrī’s vajra destroy or repel them. Some die, some flee, some are eaten. Outcome: Remaining forces are routed.. [DM 9 / Pargiter canto 89, web lines 70–72](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117169.html); [DM 9.37–41](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

47. **dm-event-047** — Śumbha grieving his brother accuses Durgā of relying on other goddesses. She states that they are her manifestations, absorbs them into herself and stands alone; the apparent plurality is withdrawn before the duel. Outcome: Devī accepts solitary combat.. [DM 10 / Pargiter canto 90, web lines 56–64](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117170.html); [DM 10.2–8](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

48. **dm-event-048** — Each counters divine missiles; Devī breaks his with huṃ. Arrows break Śumbha’s bow, discus his spear, arrows his sword, hundred-moon shield and mace. Without horse or driver he punches her breast; her palm-blow fells him. Outcome: Śumbha loses arms and chariot.. [DM 10 / Pargiter canto 90, web lines 66–69](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117170.html); [DM 10.10–27](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

49. **dm-event-049** — Śumbha rises, seizes Devī and ascends. Unsupported she wrestles him in the sky, whirls and throws him down. He charges again with fist raised; her śūla pierces his chest and kills him, shaking earth and seas. Outcome: Śumbha is slain.. [DM 10 / Pargiter canto 90, web lines 69–71](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117170.html)

50. **dm-event-050** — The universe clears: fiery portents cease, rivers return to channels, winds and sunlight become auspicious, sacrificial fires burn peacefully, and gods, Gandharvas and Apsarases celebrate. Outcome: Cosmic order is restored.. [DM 10 / Pargiter canto 90, web lines 72–73](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117170.html)

51. **dm-event-051** — Agni-led gods sing the Nārāyaṇī hymn: Devī sustains earth and water, deludes and liberates, appears as divine Śaktis, shields suppliants, and guards against enemies and disasters. They ask unceasing protection and pacification for the worlds. Outcome: The gods request continuing protection.. [DM 11 / Pargiter canto 91, web lines 56–184](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117171.html); [DM 11.19–23](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

52. **dm-event-052** — Invited to choose a boon, gods request removal of troubles and foes. Devī predicts other Śumbha and Niśumbha in the twenty-eighth age of Vaivasvata’s manvantara; born of Yaśodā in Nanda’s house and residing at Vindhya, she will kill them. Outcome: A future Vindhya manifestation is promised.. [DM 11 / Pargiter canto 91, web lines 185–193](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117171.html); [DM 11.36–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

53. **dm-event-053** — Devī predicts a fierce form devouring Vaipracitta Dānavas; their consumption reddens her teeth like pomegranate flowers, producing the name Raktadantikā. Outcome: Raktadantikā is foretold.. [DM 11 / Pargiter canto 91, web lines 194–194](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117171.html); [DM 11.43–45](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

54. **dm-event-054** — After a hundred-year drought, remembered by sages, she will appear without womb-birth as hundred-eyed Śatākṣī, nourish the world with vegetables from her body until rain, become Śākambharī, kill Durgama and be named Durgādevī. Sanskrit clarifies wording omitted or mistranscribed in English. Outcome: Śatākṣī, Śākambharī and Durgādevī are foretold.. [DM 11 / Pargiter canto 91, web lines 195–196](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117171.html); [DM 11.46–50](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

55. **dm-event-055** — Devī predicts taking a terrible form in Himalaya and devouring Rākṣasas to protect sages; their praise establishes Bhīmādevī’s name. Outcome: Bhīmādevī is foretold.. [DM 11 / Pargiter canto 91, web lines 197–197](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117171.html); [DM 11.50–52](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

56. **dm-event-056** — When Aruṇa troubles the three worlds, she will become innumerable bees and kill him, gaining the name Bhrāmarī. She promises repeated manifestations whenever Dānava oppression arises. Pargiter’s main text reads Aruṇākṣa, with Aruṇa noted as variant. Outcome: Bhrāmarī and recurring deliverance are promised.. [DM 11 / Pargiter canto 91, web lines 198–200](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117171.html); [DM 11.52–55](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

57. **dm-event-057** — Devī promises relief to attentive hymn-praisers and faithful listeners on eighth, ninth and fourteenth lunar days: no sin-born troubles, poverty or separation, and no fear of foes, robbers, rulers, weapons, fire or floods. Regular recitation secures presence in her shrine. Outcome: Hearing and recitation receive stated benefits.. [DM 12 / Pargiter canto 92, web lines 55–57](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117173.html); [DM 12.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

58. **dm-event-058** — Her exploits should be heard at bali, worship, fire rites and festivals; she accepts knowing or unknowing offerings. Autumn hearing grants freedom from trouble, wealth, grain, children and courage; recitation pacifies portents, bad dreams, planetary afflictions and child-seizure, reconciles broken fellowship and repels harmful beings. Outcome: Ritual settings and further benefits are stated.. [DM 12 / Pargiter canto 92, web lines 58–61](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117173.html)

59. **dm-event-059** — One hearing wins favor comparable to sustained offerings and gives purification and health; battle narratives remove fear and rebirth, hymns improve understanding. Remembering her exploits delivers from forest fire, isolated roads, robbers, enemies, wild beasts, angry rulers, death sentence, bonds, sea storms, battle and pain. Outcome: Remembrance is promised as rescue.. [DM 12 / Pargiter canto 92, web lines 62–65](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117173.html)

60. **dm-event-060** — Devī disappears before the watching gods. They regain offices and sacrificial shares; surviving Daityas go to Pātāla after the brothers’ deaths. Outcome: The third battle cycle concludes.. [DM 12 / Pargiter canto 92, web lines 68–69](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117173.html); [DM 12.32–35](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

61. **dm-event-061** — Medhas explains eternal Devī’s repeated world-protecting manifestations: creator, deluder, giver of knowledge and prosperity, cosmic destruction, stability, good fortune and ill fortune; worship brings wealth, sons and righteous understanding. Outcome: Medhas explains the story’s theological consequence.. [DM 12 / Pargiter canto 92, web lines 70–72](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/the-markandeya-purana/d/doc117173.html); [DM 12.36–41](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_devii/durga700.html)

## Matsarāsura–Vakratunda: origin, boon, conquest, rescue, surrender and restoration

ID: `gp-story-matsara`. Scope: Mudgala1.23–39; campaign closes at1.39.46, with1.39.47–54 transition previews separately recorded. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Major narrative transitions directly read: old1.23–24 origin/boon;1.25–31 conquests and inner Matsara counsel;1.32–36 manifestation, renewed battle, revival counter and sons’ death;1.37–38 grief, final confrontation, impartiality dialogue, vision, surrender and restricted duties;1.39 restoration. Whole-chapter completeness is independently declared in chapter_coverage; original author extraction still pending independent Sanskrit review.

COMPLETE means bounded major-transition story spine, not independent language/edition validation or every chapter COMPLETE.1.27.29 grammatical mechanism and1.31.2 Vijayapriya identity unresolved. No final physical duel or killing of Matsara inferred; his immunity is limited by Vakratunda’s self-explanation and surrender replaces destruction. No sons’ revival or later Śukra release inferred. Chapter32.18 puṣkara object/action also unresolved. Hymn39.20 says nāśita, but explicit living return39.43 controls outcome: defeat/pacification, no death asserted.

1. **gp-event-matsara-1** — Dakṣa requests origins and battles of eight incarnations; Mudgala begins Vakratunda. Indra’s annual accession festival assembles the named gods, sages, attendants and musicians recorded in mud-e-festival-roster. Rambhā’s performance and ball play enthrall Indra, who closes it prematurely. After forest play with Rambhā, seeing Durvāsas makes him leave her and bow; his seed falls on earth. He returns home, repeatedly condemns his distraction and resumes his own dharma. Matsara arises from his seed; Earth nurtures, names and initiates him at five. He studies Vedas with an unnamed teacher, and marries Durbuddhi’s daughter Madakarā. Outcome: Matsara reaches adulthood and marriage.. [Vakratunda chapter23, verses18–39; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html); [Mudgala1.23.1–21](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **gp-event-matsara-2** — Matsara obtains the five-syllable formula from Sukra and propitiates Sambhu. He stays in a secluded forest, observes Pasupata initiation, fasts for a thousand divine years, repeats the formula and meditates on Sadasiva. His austerity disturbs gods and beings. Gods and sages seek Sankara’s protection; Sankara comes with Parvati and his attendants. They see Matsara reduced to skin and bones, covered by an anthill. Outcome: Siva reaches the ascetic.. [Vakratunda chapter23, verses40–49; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **gp-event-matsara-3** — Siva awakens Matsara and invites his wishes. Matsara regains strength on seeing him, bows, worships and offers a hymn. After Siva renews the offer, Matsara asks for universal fearlessness, devotion, immunity from death caused by the five-element and three-guna domain, health with lasting lordship, and sovereignty over the cosmic egg. Although the request astonishes the assembly, Siva grants all of it because of the fierce austerity. Outcome: The requested compound boon is granted.. [Vakratunda chapter24, verses1–11,20,23–28; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html); [Vakratunda chapter24, verses29–33; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html); [Mudgala1.24.1–22](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **gp-event-matsara-4** — Matsara returns home, making friends, his mother and wife glad, and enjoys pleasures. Madakara bears him two sons: the elder Sundarapriya and younger Visayapriya. Outcome: The narrator introduces Matsara’s sons.. [Vakratunda chapter24, verses29–33; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **gp-event-matsara-5** — The assembled demons urge Matsara to conquer the gods. He says that he wants this too, but Earth restrains him because Indra is his father and gods deserve honor. The demons counter with their own account of Jalandhara, argue that gods are enemies of demons, and tell Matsara to leave Earth. He accepts this advice, which the narrator calls harmful, abandons Earth and stays with them. Outcome: Matsara abandons Earth’s counsel and resides with the demons; the campaign follows in1.25.. [Vakratunda chapter24, verses34–48; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

6. **mud-event-coronation** — Mudgala tells Dakṣa the campaign’s purpose. Matsara and demons approach Śukra, request consecration as their king and receive his agreement. Named chiefs assemble; Veda-knowing Brahmins consecrate Matsara with Ṛg/Yajus/Sāman formulas, Uśanas directing the rites. The assembly rejoices and returns home. Outcome: Matsara is consecrated demon king.. [Mudgala1.25.1–16](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

7. **mud-event-army** — Matsara summons the Asuras to conquer the three worlds. Fourfold forces muster with arms, trees, rocks and mountains: infantry, decorated elephants, swift horsemen, chariots and other animal mounts. His decorated chariot has one hundred asses; both sons ride horses ahead, Virocana and other chiefs around him. Outcome: Army marches to conquest.. [Mudgala1.25.17–44](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

8. **mud-event-earth-conquest** — Demon forces defeat terrestrial rulers: some flee, some surrender, warriors die fighting. Sons of dead rulers are installed as tributaries; surrendered kings retain office. Demons govern rulerless regions and are stationed beside local kings. Matsara rejoices after conquering the islanded earth. Outcome: Earth is subordinated under tribute and surveillance.. [Mudgala1.26.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

9. **mud-event-naga-battle** — Demons destroy Nāga gardens in Pātāla. Vāsuki, Takṣaka, Kambala and Aśvatara lead resistance: arms and missiles first repel demons; returning chiefs repel Nāgas. Serpent poison-flames burn demons, weapons and mounts; Śukra’s vidyā removes poison. A Garuḍa missile produces bird assailants, burnt by Vāsuki’s poison-fire. Virocana and chiefs themselves become Garuḍas by māyā and overwhelm Nāgas. Outcome: Māyā transformations overcome the first failed bird missile.. [Mudgala1.26.10–32](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

10. **mud-event-naga-settlement** — Śeṣa comes conciliating, asks why fellow Pātāla residents wage war, and addresses demons as brothers. Virocana demands submission to Matsara’s universal authority or destruction. Śeṣa agrees, offers annual tribute and jewels, withdraws with Vāsuki and others. Victorious demons march against Varuṇa. Outcome: Pātāla settles through annual tribute.. [Mudgala1.26.33–43](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

11. **mud-event-varuna-conquest** — Matsara sends Varuṇa an ultimatum: submit in office or be killed. Makara-mounted Varuṇa advances with noose and aquatic forces. Virocana, Mahiṣāsura, Śumbha and Andhaka fight them. After three days demons fall; Kāvya restores their awareness, battle resumes and Virocana captures Varuṇa. Kubera hears the news and flees to Śiva on Kailāsa; demons occupy Alakā and march with captive Varuṇa against Yama. Outcome: Varuṇa captured; Alakā occupied after Kubera’s flight.. [Mudgala1.27.1–21](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

12. **mud-event-yama-conquest** — Buffalo-mounted Yama advances with staff, Death, diseases and attendants. Five days of fighting defeat demons until Viṣayapriya fights Death and afflicts diseases. He disables Yama’s buffalo and fells Yama. Rising, Yama strikes him with Kāladanda; the staff instead falls to earth, and Viṣayapriya captures him. Demons praise the son’s feat. Outcome: Viṣayapriya captures Yama despite the Death-staff attack.. [Mudgala1.27.22–33](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

13. **mud-event-indra-conquest** — Demons proceed with captive Yama to Amarāvatī. Agni, Vāyu, Viśvedevas, Sādhyas, Rudras, Vasus, Gandharvas, Yakṣas, Vidyādharas, planets, moon, sun and Ādityas join Indra on Airāvata with vajra. Seven nights end in demon flight and fainting chiefs. Sundarapriya attacks the gods; Indra’s vajra does not move him. He disables Airāvata, seizes Indra by the foot and captures him. Other gods die, fall, flee or are captured; Matsara gains heaven. Outcome: Sundarapriya captures Indra; Matsara rules heaven.. [Mudgala1.27.34–52](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

14. **mud-event-divine-refuge** — Fleeing gods ask Kaśyapa to protect them and release Indra. With Vasiṣṭha, gods and sages, Kaśyapa petitions Brahmā to restore proper stations. Brahmā admits Matsara’s Śiva-boon makes him unconquerable and takes them to Viṣṇu, who also admits inability. They seek Śiva, who explains his own fierce-austerity grant and inability to destroy the demon. Viṣṇu chooses refuge at Kailāsa rather than submission, predicts Matsara will take Vaikuṇṭha and Satyaloka but spare Kailāsa out of devotion. Śiva remains anxious. Outcome: Gods cluster at Kailāsa; their expectation will be challenged.. [Mudgala1.28.1–29](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

15. **mud-event-andhaka-envoy** — Matsara occupies Indra’s throne, uses Airāvata, receives frightened celestial entertainers and redistributes divine offices to demon chiefs. He resents Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Rudra not paying homage. Andhaka is sent to summon them under threat, finds Satyaloka and Vaikuṇṭha empty, then enters Kailāsa and receives a seat. He delivers the demand. Śiva asserts that Matsara wins through his boon and threatens to burn him; he releases Andhaka because he is an envoy and orders him to report. Outcome: Andhaka returns with Śiva’s refusal.. [Mudgala1.29.1–28](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

16. **mud-event-matsara-karma-argument** — Andhaka praises Kailāsa’s unmatched splendour. Chiefs urge attack; Matsara credits his own austerity and karma, rejecting Śiva’s boast as giver. He argues desire-driven action yields gains and Brahman-dedicated action liberation, and claims world creation, preservation and destruction depend on karma rather than the three gods. Demons march to Kailāsa, wound Śiva’s guards, who flee and report. Outcome: Matsara attacks his former boon-grantor’s domain.. [Mudgala1.29.29–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

17. **mud-event-kailasa-battle** — Śiva arms with Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Kāma, Vasus, Kubera, moon, sun, Agni, Vāyu, Kālāgni Rudra, Bhairavas, Vīrabhadra, Nandin, Bhṛṅgin, Puṣpadanta, Mahākāla, Mahākālī and Kārttikeya. Bull-mounted Śiva advances. Confused armies counter weapons and wrestle; chiefs enter named duels while Matsara watches. Seven days/nights end with gods overpowering and felling the demon chiefs and sons. Outcome: Gods gain a temporary victory.. [Mudgala1.30.1–35](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

18. **mud-event-matsara-captures-siva** — Matsara enters roaring and showers arrows: Senānī, Agni, Nandin, Vīrabhadra, sun, moon, Vāyu, Kālāgni Rudra, Mahākālī, Bhṛṅgin’s Gaṇas, Kubera and Brahmā fall. He counters Viṣṇu’s thousand-arrow shower and fells him with a hundred. Śiva’s trident is ineffective and falls; Matsara nooses his neck and draws him like a bound animal. He taunts Śiva that both owe capacity to karma, captures other gods and enjoys Kailāsa. Outcome: Śiva and divine defenders are captured.. [Mudgala1.30.36–52](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

19. **mud-event-world-allocation** — Matsara assigns Kailāsa to Sundarapriya, Vaikuṇṭha to the son here written Vijayapriya, Satyaloka to Prahlāda, and Indra’s region to Virocana. He creates Matsarāvāsaka on earth. He releases Śiva and gods with permission to go wherever they wish provided they obey his orders. They shelter anxiously in mountain valleys and caves. Outcome: Divine captives are released under Matsara’s command.. [Mudgala1.31.1–10](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

20. **mud-event-forced-worship** — Matsara orders gods’ images broken and cast into water, and installs his images in regions, settlements and houses. He coerces Brahmins to perform sacrifice, Gāyatrī and Agnihotra in his name and promises wealth and heaven; feeding demons pleases him. Some sages keep rites in wild forests, while others abandon duties from fear. Sacrificial exclamations cease; gods suffer starvation and cannot devise his destruction. Outcome: Suppressed worship produces divine deprivation.. [Mudgala1.31.11–27](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

21. **mud-event-datta-counsel** — Wandering Dattātreya arrives, a fearless naked yogin and Gaṇarāja devotee. Gods honor him, massage his feet and offer fruit. Śiva asks a destruction strategy, describing displacement and starvation. Dattātreya says Śiva’s own claim to unique lordship is Matsara in his heart; conquer envy through yoga and Gaṇeśa worship. He recounts his own one-syllable worship and non-separation from Gaṇeśa, prescribes that worship, tells Śiva first to destroy inner Matsara then the demon, and leaves. Outcome: Dattātreya prescribes inner reform and one-syllable worship.. [Mudgala1.31.28–52](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

22. **mud-event-vak-manifest** — Śiva repeats Dattātreya’s counsel; gods and sages worship Gaṇeśa with the one-syllable mantra for a thousand divine years. They use diverse seats, fasting or leaves/air/water, breath control, toe-standing, mental worship, flowers and circumambulation. Vakratunda appears as radiant Brahman, lion-mounted, elephant-faced, four-armed with noose/goad and boon/fearlessness gestures, Siddhi left and Buddhi right. The alarmed petitioners ask gentleness; he identifies himself and softens his radiance. They worship with sixteen services and begin a hymn. Outcome: Vakratunda manifests and accepts worship.. [Mudgala1.32.1–29](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

23. **mud-event-vak-promises-help** — Gods and sages hymn Vakratunda’s forms and yoga unity, ask lasting devotion and destruction of Matsara for the afflicted world. Vakratunda promises the hymn will confer siddhis and desired objects and that he will remove Matsara. He explains that gods, humans, Nāgas and the world belong to the five-element/three-guṇa/eight-envelope domain from which Matsara has no fear, making destruction difficult, yet promises their welfare. Outcome: Vakratunda accepts the rescue request and states its difficulty.. [Mudgala1.32.30–58](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

24. **mud-event-vak-army** — Vakratunda remembers his army; Pramoda, Āmodaka and elephant-faced, four-armed Gaṇas manifest, variously lion-, mouse-, peacock-, Śeṣa- or horse-mounted. They offer obedience and say one could destroy Matsara. Vakratunda explains that he created the demon through māyā for play: immediate annihilation would end the play. He orders battle in his presence and says a form without the eight envelopes can kill him instantly. Gods and sages rejoice; the whole host goes to the city named Īrṣyā. Outcome: Vakratunda deliberately permits the campaign instead of immediate annihilation.. [Mudgala1.33.1–22](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

25. **mud-event-sky-warning** — Matsara enjoys celestial company and all mentally wished objects through austerity and boon. A sky-voice announces Vakratunda’s arrival to kill him; he faints. Demons search the sky unsuccessfully and restore him. He invokes immunity against eight-envelope beings. Prahlāda and chiefs argue the defeated gods may have produced an illusion, dismiss the voice, and obtain permission to find and kill them. Outcome: The warning triggers a demon reconnaissance rather than surrender.. [Mudgala1.33.23–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

26. **mud-event-reconnaissance-muster** — Searching chiefs see elephant-human Vakratunda praised by gods, fear the warning is true and return to report rather than risk dying before reporting. Vakratunda is encamped five yojanas from Īrṣyāpura. Matsara first pales, then orders his army to attack. Frightened gods petition immediate protection; lion-mounted Vakratunda with noose/goad promises rescue and roars, terrifying all worlds and demons. Chiefs offer to fight while Matsara watches. Outcome: Matsara’s fourfold host advances; chiefs begin battle.. [Mudgala1.34.1–25](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

27. **mud-event-five-day-stalemate** — Prahlāda-led demons rain weapons; Viṣṇu-led gods respond. Countered missiles, swords, maces, nooses and wrestling generate casualties and dust that hides friend from foe. Blood settles the dust; matching fourfold units fight one another. Wounded troops and alternating advances/falls continue without night rest for five days, with neither side prevailing. Outcome: Five-day stalemate opens the chief-led reversals.. [Mudgala1.34.26–45](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

28. **mud-event-chief-reversals** — Prahlāda’s Anala missile burns gods and mounts; Indra prepares Megha missile, but Prahlāda cuts his bow before release. Indra’s vajra fells Prahlāda; Mahiṣa maces Indra unconscious. Agni flames Mahiṣa, who becomes water and floods him, cuts Agni’s śakti and becomes a buffalo to fell him with horns. Yama staffs Mahiṣa down; Virocana swords Yama down. Varuṇa nooses Virocana, but Andhaka cuts the noose and maces Varuṇa. Vāyu swords Andhaka down; Śambara clubs Vāyu down. Virocana’s arrows fell Kubera. Outcome: Successive interventions reverse both sides’ advantages.. [Mudgala1.35.1–23](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

29. **mud-event-narayana-counter** — Viṣṇu’s mace splits Virocana’s chest leaving him dead-like; discus fells Andhaka, arrows fell Śambara, and discus/mace kill or disable demons. Prahlāda recovers, wounds gods, fights Viṣṇu with mace and fells Garuḍa with sword. As Viṣṇu’s discus approaches, Prahlāda remembers Janārdana and releases a Nārāyaṇa missile. It repels the discus, burns gods, hits Keśava’s heart and leaves him unconscious, dead-like. Outcome: Nārāyaṇa missile defeats the discus and disables Viṣṇu.. [Mudgala1.35.24–38](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

30. **mud-event-siva-pasupata** — Seeing gods and sages burnt, Śiva uses Pināka to release Pāśupata. It fights Nārāyaṇa missile in the sky; the gods rejoice. Śiva then throws trident, kills many demons, strikes Prahlāda’s heart and fells him. Other chiefs die or faint; Matsara’s two sons Sundarapriya and Viṣayapriya approach Śiva angrily. Outcome: Śiva temporarily defeats the chiefs; sons enter.. [Mudgala1.35.39–48](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

31. **mud-event-sons-fell-siva** — Dakṣa asks for more detail; Mudgala answers that even great gods cannot recount all Vakratunda’s power. The sons challenge Śiva, promising to kill him, Vakratunda, gods and sages. Śiva answers that Vakratunda came to reduce earth’s burden, reversed their former time through changed karma, and will enable him to destroy them. Sons counter his arrows; their Pāśupata missile nullifies his trident, hits his heart and fells Śiva unconscious. Outcome: Even the newly confident Śiva falls to the sons’ missile.. [Mudgala1.36.1–21](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

32. **mud-event-gana-intervention** — Vakratunda sends eight chief Gaṇas with their troops: Pramoda, Āmoda, Brahmapriya, Yogapriya, Svānandabhoktṛ, Jñānamaya, Sarvaga and Sarvavit. Their look restores gods from fainting, fatigue and wounds, with doubled energy. Kavi restores demon forces to life and health. Gaṇas’ weapons sever demons; Brahmapriya seizes Śukra and throws him in a distant cave. Demons resolve to die fighting or rescue their guru, disable gods again, but the armed Gaṇas fell the chiefs and kill or disable the army. Outcome: Śukra’s capture removes the rescuing guru from the active battle.. [Mudgala1.36.22–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

33. **mud-event-sons-killed** — Both sons challenge Pramoda’s Gaṇas and rain arrows. Sarvaga and Sarvavit counter the arrows and destroy demon troops. When the frightened sons prepare Agni missiles, two axes arrive before release and sever their heads. Survivors report both sons certainly dead, while they cannot tell if Prahlāda and others are dead or dead-like; they urge Matsara to abandon pride and surrender to protect the remaining demons. Outcome: Both sons die; survivors counsel surrender.. [Mudgala1.36.43–58](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

34. **mud-event-matsara-grief** — News of his sons’ death makes Matsara faint; restored after two muhūrtas, he laments lost children, supposedly failed immortality/fearlessness boon, and fate’s victory over effort. Elders comfort him: bodies die, self does not; relatives must perform funerary rites. They urge ritual duty, attack or surrender, and avoiding giving enemies joy by grief. Matsara instead takes the surviving army to battle. Outcome: Matsara chooses battle after consolation and alternative counsel.. [Mudgala1.37.1–19](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

35. **mud-event-matsara-final-challenge** — Gods warn Vakratunda about Matsara’s sovereignty, timely rain, fruit, suppressed disease/theft, monopolized worship and sacrifices, displaced gods and disabled sages, seized women and immunity from eight-envelope beings. Vakratunda reassures them and advances lion-mounted with one tusk, noose and goad. Matsara threatens a one-arrow victory, invokes claimed earth-splitting and mountain-crushing strength, and offers Vakratunda life under his command in the forest while threatening the instigating gods and sages. Outcome: Final confrontation begins with threats and invocation of immunity.. [Mudgala1.37.20–47](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

36. **mud-event-vak-boon-limit** — Vakratunda says he is neither god, human, Asura, Nāga, Gandharva nor mountain, nor a creature from varied birth-origins or the eight envelopes, but joy-giving Brahman. He came to restore gods and sages; Matsara’s boon-merit has loosened through accumulated sin. He offers survival if Matsara surrenders and abandons hatred of dharma and gods. Astonished, Matsara respectfully asks how attribute-free Brahman can have form and favor gods over demons. Outcome: Matsara moves from threats to a doctrinal question, without a physical final duel.. [Mudgala1.38.1–15](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

37. **mud-event-vak-impartiality-explanation** — Vakratunda explains creation as play with each being’s own dharma, his indwelling control through Puruṣa and Prakṛti, and conditional impartiality. When gods abandon duty to uproot demons or obstruct austerity, he inspires and grants demons boons against them. When demons abandon duty to uproot gods, he reveals their vulnerable means through the gods or takes form against demons the gods cannot conquer. He remains Brahman in both when they follow duty. Matsara oppressed gods through abandoning his own dharma, so he came against him. Outcome: The apparent favoritism is explained as restoring disrupted dharma.. [Mudgala1.38.16–30](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

38. **mud-event-matsara-vision-surrender** — Vakratunda shows his Brahman form in a point; by the grace and fruit of that vision Matsara receives divine sight. He sees the form beyond body/embodied, guṇas and māyā, with attributed body, attribute-free elephant head and their yoga unity. He bows, hymns the deity’s Brahman/yogic form, four arms, lion mount, Siddhi and Buddhi lordship, and seeks refuge and passage beyond worldly existence. He repeatedly falls at the feet and stands with folded hands. Outcome: Divine sight leads to praise and surrender.. [Mudgala1.38.31–50](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

39. **mud-event-matsara-new-duties** — Vakratunda declines to destroy a surrendered devotee, offers a boon, and promises Matsara’s hymn sovereignty, health, wealth, family and four aims. Matsara requests firm devotion, affection for Vakratunda’s devotees, and a place/livelihood with welfare. Vakratunda grants unwavering devotion and directs him to protect beings following their dharma and devotees. He must cause no obstruction wherever the deity’s image is worshipped or remembered at beginnings. Elsewhere he may act as an Asura, take fruits from karma/knowledge actors and rule in their hearts; toward devotion he must act as servant. Matsara accepts joyfully. Outcome: Matsara survives under restricted protective/obstructive duties.. [Mudgala1.38.51–62](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

40. **mud-event-gods-final-hymn** — Seeing Matsara at peace, gods and sages rejoice and hymn Vakratunda as Brahman encompassing and transcending forms, cosmic elements, deities, senses, creation/preservation/destruction, karma fruits, family and natural forms. They praise the demon’s transformation to peace and world’s joy, admit the deity exceeds Vedas, scriptures and yoga comprehension, and stand silent with folded hands. Vakratunda welcomes the praise. Outcome: Peace prompts final hymn and boon offer.. [Mudgala1.39.1–24](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

41. **mud-event-gods-devotion-boon** — Vakratunda promises the hymn desired enjoyments, descendants, enjoyment/liberation, siddhis and insight. A thousand repetitions free a prisoner; recitation forgives offences, and twenty-one repetitions on twenty-one days grant what is contemplated. The praising gods/sages will be free of obstruction and devoted to their own dharma. Offered a boon, they request unwavering devotion as sufficient for all siddhis; he grants it. Outcome: Gods and sages receive unwavering devotion.. [Mudgala1.39.25–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

42. **mud-event-campaign-aftermath** — Vakratunda vanishes before gods and sages and dwells in Svānanda. Saddened gods and sages return to their own stations contemplating him. Matsara returns to his station with demons, abiding peacefully; demons then return to their separate homes. Mudgala explains Vakratunda creates, preserves and destroys and through incarnations restores gods, demons and humans to their respective duties. Outcome: Divine offices and ordinary homes are restored; Matsara lives peacefully.. [Mudgala1.39.41–46](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## The speech-impaired sage hears Brahma’s recitation and gains speech

ID: `gp-story-muka`. Scope: Krida155.24–32; self-contained illustrative narrative. Newly completed this continuation: False.

The question introducing the example, narration request, hearing, transformation, subsequent recitation, descendants and final abode are all read and extracted; verse33 explicitly introduces another ancient account.

Completeness is limited to this nine-verse narrative, not chapter155 or wholeGanesa Purana. Muka is not conflated with any independently named sage.

1. **gp-event-muka-1** — The sages ask Suta for a past example of the merit produced by hearing the Purana. He introduces a sage who could not speak. Outcome: A concrete hearing narrative begins.. [Krīḍā chapter155, verses24–28; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

2. **gp-event-muka-2** — Lomasa reaches Brahmaloka, bows to Brahma and sits with permission. He requests the Ganesa Purana that Brahma had narrated to Vyasa; Brahma invites him to listen carefully. Outcome: Brahma agrees to narrate.. [Krīḍā chapter155, verses24–28; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

3. **gp-event-muka-3** — Brahma tells the Purana to Lomasa. The speech-impaired sage listens devotedly to the complete recitation. He subsequently speaks fluently, studies and recites the text for others. Outcome: The listener gains speech and becomes a reciter.. [Krīḍā chapter155, verses29–32; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

4. **gp-event-muka-4** — The sage enjoys desired pleasures and gains sons and grandsons. At life’s end he reaches Ganesa’s auspicious abode. Outcome: The bounded example reaches its final outcome.. [Krīḍā chapter155, verses29–32; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

## Saṃvaraṇa and his sister obtain offspring after hearing Gaṇeśa Purāṇa

ID: `gp-story-samvarana`. Scope: Krida155.33–45ab. Newly completed this continuation: False.

King’s prior efforts, arrival of reciter, hearing, rewards, offspring and final state; sister’s parallel response and final outcome all read. Verse45cd introduces Sagara’s son, a different example.

Source presents devotional efficacy, not a literal individually spoken boon. Do not convert these results into an explicit divine boon grant.

1. **gp-event-samvarana-1** — The narrator describes Saṃvaraṇa as an Ikṣvāku-line king devoted to sacrifice, giving, study and protecting his subjects. Childless, he performs a putriya sacrifice complete with its prescribed elements, gifts and food distribution. He then hears Harivamsa and rewards the reader and Brahmins; neither brings a son. Outcome: His wish remains unfulfilled.. [Krīḍā chapter155, verses33–41; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

2. **gp-event-samvarana-2** — Saṃvaraṇa asks the sage to stay, joyfully hears the Purana from him and rewards him with jewels, pearls, garments and golden ornaments. The king gains a son, becomes devoted to Ganesa, enjoys many pleasures and finally attains Ganesa’s highest state. Outcome: The king’s hearing episode concludes.. [Krīḍā chapter155, verses33–41; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

3. **gp-event-samvarana-3** — The king’s unnamed sister summons the same sage and listens to the Purana. She gains a brave son, becomes devoted to Ganesa, has sons and grandsons and enjoys pleasures. At life’s end she reaches Ganesa’s own abode. Outcome: The sister’s hearing episode reaches its final outcome.. [Krīḍā chapter155, verses42–45ab; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

## Bhaṇḍāsura origin-to-restoration narrative transition chain

ID: `lal-story-bhanda`. Scope: Source-ordered major narrative transitions in11–30, with necessary10.79–87 preview; not exhaustive atomic extraction of all descriptive names and ritual promises.. Newly completed this continuation: False.

All numbered narrative text of11–30 directly read, origins, campaign decisions, battles, final destruction, restoration and return at30.107 extracted in bounded records. Complete applies to transition chain only.

Every chapter remains PARTIAL for exhaustive extraction. Main sequence follows source presentation including previews and flashbacks; no universal Purāṇic chronology is inferred.

1. **lal-event-020** — Before the origin account, Hayagrīva previews Bhaṇḍa’s domination: Viśukra emerges from his right shoulder, Viṣaṅga from his left; he also creates sister Dūminī. The three oppose gods, who flee their offices and families. Outcome: Next chapter supplies origin and boons.. [Chapter 10, verses 79–87](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362945.html)

2. **lal-event-001** — After Dākṣāyaṇī’s death, Śiva withdraws; her rebirth serves him. Gods oppressed by Tāraka seek a son of Śiva and send Kāma, recalling Viṣṇu’s earlier gifts. Kāma shoots Śiva, whose recovered composure leads him to burn Kāma with his third eye; Gaurī leaves for penance. Outcome: Kāma is burned; Gaurī begins penance.. [Chapter 11, verses 2–29](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362946.html)

3. **lal-event-002** — Citrakarmā shapes Kāma’s ashes into a boy; Rudra’s gaze animates him. Addressing him as son, Citrakarmā teaches Śatarudriya worship. Śiva accepts the boy’s requests for half his opponent’s strength and arrow-based neutralization of hostile weapons, additionally granting sixty thousand years of rule. Brahmā’s utterance supplies the name Bhaṇḍa. Outcome: Animated Bhaṇḍa obtains combat powers and a fixed royal term.. [Chapter 11, verses 30–38](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362946.html)

4. **lal-event-003** — Śukra crowns Bhaṇḍa; Maya rebuilds Śoṇitapura. Royal gifts include Vijaya bow, protective umbrella, disease-averting chowries and indestructible crown. Eight assistants and four wives are listed. The asuras maintain Vedic rites and Śiva worship during sixty thousand years. Outcome: Bhaṇḍa rules with assistants, wives, protections and religious observances.. [Chapter 12, verses 1–19](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362947.html)

5. **lal-event-004** — Viṣṇu sends his deluding Māyā with Viśvācī’s companions; Bhaṇḍa neglects worship. Nārada urges Indra to worship Parāśakti. Śukra warns Bhaṇḍa; Śrutavarmā notes the expired reign, while Bhīmakarmā trusts his combat boon and urges disruption. Outcome: Indra worships Parāśakti; Bhaṇḍa chooses to disrupt worship despite warning about his royal term.. [Chapter 12, verses 20–58](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362947.html)

6. **lal-event-005** — Repeated attacks, including Vāyavya, fail against the regenerated divine wall. Indra’s gods offer their own flesh in a great sacrifice. Lalitā emerges with noose, goad, sugarcane bow and five arrows; her glance restores the mutilated gods. Outcome: Sacrificing gods receive Lalitā’s manifestation and bodily restoration.. [Chapter 12, verses 59–75](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362947.html)

7. **lal-event-006** — The gods praise Lalitā’s cosmic identity; Indra requests rescue. She promises personally to defeat Bhaṇḍa and restore the worlds. Devout recitation of this hymn receives blessings of virtue, learning, longevity, health and family prosperity. Outcome: Lalitā promises victory over Bhaṇḍa and conditional hymn benefits.. [Chapter 13, verses 1–36](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362948.html)

8. **lal-event-007** — Gods and attendants arrive; Viśvakarmā builds a city at Brahmā’s command. Considering a suitable coronation and spouse, Brahmā sees Śiva assume a beautiful form and names him Kāmeśvara. Lalitā and Kāmeśvara mutually desire each other; Brahmā requests their joint enthronement. Outcome: Śiva manifests as Kāmeśvara; mutual attraction prepares marriage.. [Chapter 14, verses 1–29](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362949.html)

9. **lal-event-008** — Lalitā insists on independence and a compatible husband; her thrown garland selects Kāmeśvara. Brahmā calls Viṣṇu her brother; Viṣṇu performs the marriage. Gods provide weapons and regalia. Brahmā’s Kusumākara chariot resists weapons, travels freely and removes afflictions through its fragrance. Outcome: She selects Kāmeśvara and receives ceremonial gifts at marriage.. [Chapter 15, verses 1–28](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362950.html)

10. **lal-event-009** — After the procession, Lalitā fulfills the assembly’s desires by her glance; Brahmā names her Kāmākṣī and Kāmeśvarī. Following ten thousand years of imperial life, Nārada recalls Bhaṇḍa’s threat. Gods return to their homes while continuing service through partial manifestations. Outcome: Gods return home while continuing partial service; the campaign follows.. [Chapter 15, verses 29–54](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362950.html)

11. **lal-event-010** — Lalitā’s campaign begins. Sampatkarī emerges from her goad and leads elephants on Kaṇakolāhala. Atitvaritavikrānti emerges from the noose, rides Aparājita and leads cavalry; her four hands hold noose, goad, cane and bridle. Daṇḍanāthā’s war preparation follows. Outcome: Elephant and cavalry divisions emerge and set out.. [Chapter 16, verses 1–36](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362951.html)

12. **lal-event-011** — Daṇḍanāthā rides Vajraghoṣa with boar-faced troops. The text gives her twelve names, including Vārāhī, then Śyāmalā’s names, including Mantriṇī. Dhanurveda emerges from Śyāmalā’s parrot and gives Citrajīva and inexhaustible quivers. Yantriṇī and Tantriṇī attend her. Outcome: Divine names, vehicles and Śyāmalā’s bow establish their campaign roles.. [Chapter 17, verses 1–51](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362952.html)

13. **lal-event-012** — Lalitā proceeds on Śrīcakra, armed with goad, noose, sugarcane bow and five arrows, attended by Kāmeśvarī and other eternal powers. Hayagrīva supplies twenty-five epithets and benefits of their recitation. Outcome: Lalitā advances with armed eternal attendants.. [Chapter 18, verses 1–19](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362953.html)

14. **lal-event-013** — Hayagrīva describes Lalitā’s Cakrarāja tiers, including Siddhis, eight mothers, Mudrās, successive yoginī groups and fifteen Nityās. Mantriṇī’s seven-tier Geyacakra houses allied powers, Bhairavas, protective deities and guardians; petitions reach Lalitā through Mantriṇī. Alternative maternal iconography is explicitly reported. Outcome: The narrator enumerates deployment and Mantriṇī’s access role.. [Chapter 19, verses 3–90a](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362954.html)

15. **lal-event-014** — Kiricakra houses Daṇḍanāthā and supporting powers, including Jṛmbhiṇī, Mohinī, Stambhinī, Dhātunāthās, personified plough and pestle, guardians and vehicles. Surāsindhu’s prospective role is to refresh thirsty combatants. The witness calls this a five-step chariot but later mentions a sixth step. Outcome: Kiricakra powers and support roles are introduced.. [Chapter 20, verses 1–83](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362957.html)

16. **lal-event-015** — Omens disturb Śūnyaka. Bhaṇḍa’s younger brothers Viśukra and Viṣaṅga advise him: Viśukra derides female opponents, while Viṣaṅga urges investigation and recalls earlier divine victories. Bhaṇḍa rejects the warning, boasts of his forces and named sons, and orders Kuṭilākṣa to fortify the city and send an advance force. Outcome: He dismisses caution and orders fortification and attack.. [Chapter 21, verses 1–101](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362959.html)

17. **lal-event-016** — Bhaṇḍa sends Durmada; Kuṭilākṣa deploys Tālajaṅgha, Tālabhuja, Tālagrīva and Tālaketu at city gates. Sampatkarī fights Durmada on Raṇakolāhala. After Durmada strikes a crown jewel, her arrows pierce his chest and kill him; survivors flee. Outcome: Sampatkarī kills Durmada and survivors flee.. [Chapter 22, verses 19b–66](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362960.html)

18. **lal-event-017** — Kuraṇḍa, Durmada’s elder brother, seeks revenge. The cavalry goddess asks Sampatkarī to yield the fight, then binds his army with multiplying nooses. Kuraṇḍa cuts her bowstring; she kills him with her goad, whose Pūtanās consume his forces. Survivors retreat. Outcome: The cavalry goddess kills Kuraṇḍa after the noose/bowstring exchange.. [Chapter 22, verses 67–108](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362960.html)

19. **lal-event-018** — Five generals deploy Sarpiṇī, whose destroyed snakes multiply while she survives. Lalitā’s palate-born Nakulī rides Garuḍa, creates mongooses from her teeth and dissolves Sarpiṇī with Gāruḍa. Enemy arrows rout her mongooses; Akṣīṇanākula renews the force. Nakulī kills Karaṅka and four colleagues. Śyāmalā makes her a satellite deity; she spares surviving messengers. Outcome: Nakulī defeats regenerative snake magic and the five generals; survivors are released.. [Chapter 23, verses 1–99](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362961.html)

20. **lal-event-019** — Kīkasā’s seven sons attack with a Sun-granted gaze that burns and immobilizes seen enemies and weapons. Lalitā summons Tiraskaraṇikā, Daṇḍanāthā’s guard. From Tamolipta she uses Mohana arrows and Andha to blind them; lost sight ends immobilization. She kills all seven, and the Śaktis destroy their army. Outcome: Tiraskaraṇikā’s blinding restores the Śaktis’ weapons; the generals die.. [Chapter 24, verses 1–99](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362962.html)

21. **lal-event-021** — After defeats, Bhaṇḍa orders Viṣaṅga and fifteen generals to attack Lalitā’s rear secretly, aiming to destroy the source of her powers. [Chapter 25, verses 1–65](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362963.html)

22. **lal-event-022** — Kuṭilākṣa prevents the vanguard returning while Viṣaṅga breaks Lalitā’s fan. Nityās obtain her permission to intervene. 23. **lal-event-023** — The Nityās kill the invading generals. Kāmeśvarī breaks Viṣaṅga’s armour; he escapes near dawn, destined for Daṇḍanāthā’s later attack. 24. **lal-event-024** — Daṇḍanāthā routs Kuṭilākṣa’s night force. She and Mantriṇī acknowledge inadequate rear protection and propose a guarded fire enclosure. Lalitā approves defence before offence. Jvālāmālinī becomes the enclosure; Stambhinī guards its opening, with the chariots and cavalry deliberately positioned. Outcome: A fortified camp closes the route for another ordinary ambush.. 25. **lal-event-025** — Bhaṇḍa sends his sons with two hundred army divisions to capture Lalitā alive. 26. **lal-event-026** — Bālā persists; Lalitā embraces her, grants permission, armour and weapons, and a swan-drawn palanquin from her bow staff. Mantriṇī and Daṇḍanāthā reluctantly guard her. The single Bālā appears separately to each prince and fights with missiles and countermissiles. Outcome: Bālā enters with maternal equipment and protective escorts.. [Chapter 26, verses 81–107](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362964.html)

27. **lal-event-027** — Bālā’s Nārāyaṇa missile burns the princes’ army. Thirty crescent-tipped arrows sever thirty heads simultaneously. Mantriṇī and Daṇḍanāthā embrace her and report her success; Lalitā rejoices. Outcome: All thirty princes die; their father’s grief initiates the next countermeasure.. [Chapter 26, verses 108–117](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362964.html)

28. **lal-event-028** — Bhaṇḍa grieves his sons and threatens personal revenge; his advisers restrain him. He sends invisible Viśukra to operate Jayavighna. Viśukra inscribes eight tridents and eight presiding powers on a stone slab, consecrates it with mantra and sacrifice, and throws it over the fire wall. Outcome: The consecrated enemy yantra enters the camp.. [Chapter 27, verses 1–42](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362965.html)

29. **lal-event-029** — Jayavighna produces sleep, disengagement and rejection of the queen’s authority; the soldiers abandon weapons. Viśukra returns after midnight to exploit their apathy. Mantriṇī and Daṇḍanāthā remain unaffected and report the unexplained breakdown to Lalitā. Outcome: Unaffected commanders seek a remedy.. [Chapter 27, verses 43–66](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362965.html)

He pulverizes Jayavighna with tusks, restoring readiness, then creates elephant-faced allies under six Vighnanāyakas. Outcome: Obstruction is removed and allied reinforcements emerge.. [Chapter 27, verses 67–83](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362965.html)

Lalitā rewards him with precedence in worship before other deities. Outcome: Second night ends with victory and a ritual precedence boon.. [Chapter 27, verses 84–104](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362965.html)

Duels pair Daṇḍanāthā with Viṣaṅga, Śyāmalā with Viśukra, and supporting commanders with nephews. Outcome: Third day opens with paired battles.. 33. **lal-event-033** — Viśukra’s Tṛṣāstra incapacitates the Śaktis with thirst. Mantriṇī proposes the ocean on Daṇḍanāthā’s chariot; at command Surāsindhu showers restoring drink. Śyāmalā surrounds her army with arrows to exclude asuras from it. Recovered troops prepare to resume. Outcome: Countermeasure restores strength without aiding the opposing army..
