34. **lal-event-034** — Daṇḍanāthā rewards Surāsindhu with sacrificial use after mantra purification and benefits for worshippers. Battle resumes: the nephews die; Śyāmalā destroys Viśukra using Brahmaśiras. Daṇḍanāthā fights Viṣaṅga until midnight, hooks his head with her plough and kills him with an iron-club blow. Outcome: Both Bhaṇḍa brothers die before the fourth day.. [Chapter 28, verses 85–114](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362966.html)

35. **lal-event-035** — Grieving brothers and sons, Bhaṇḍa mobilizes his remaining commanders on Ābhila with Ghātaka sword. Lalitā undertakes the duel herself; Jvālāmālinī enlarges the camp exit. After the fourth morning’s army battle, Lalitā’s arrows multiply as they travel and strike. Outcome: Lalitā and Bhaṇḍa enter a sustained missile exchange.. 36. **lal-event-036** — Bhaṇḍa deploys darkness, heresy, blindness, power-loss, death, memory-loss, fear, disease and lost longevity; Lalitā successively counters each. Acyuta, Ananta and Govinda emerge from her three-name missile, burn diseases and receive a continuing healing charge. Outcome: Nine disabling powers are neutralized.. [Chapter 29, verses 62–73](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362967.html)

37. **lal-event-037** — Mahāsurāstra regenerates named asuras. Lalitā laughs; Durgā manifests with divine equipment and repeats the victories cited from Caṇḍikāsaptaśatī. Bhaṇḍa’s Mūkāstra meets Mahāvāgvādinī. Outcome: Durgā destroys the manifested asuras and returns to Lalitā.. [Chapter 29, verses 74–89](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362967.html)

38. **lal-event-038** — Veda-thieves are repelled from Lalitā’s right thumb nail. Her index-nail tortoise supports drowned Śaktis and drinks the flood; middle-nail boar destroys Hiraṇyākṣas. Ring-finger man-lion protects Prahlāda against eyebrow-born Hiraṇyakaśipus. Little-finger Vāmanas bind Balīndras. Outcome: Successive finger manifestations defeat the generated threats.. [Chapter 29, verses 90–108](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362967.html)

39. **lal-event-039** — Left-thumb Bhārgava Rāma kills Haihaya-generated Arjunas. Rāvaṇa emerges from Bhaṇḍa’s huṃkāra with Kumbhakarṇa and Meghanāda; left-index Kodaṇḍarāma and Lakṣmaṇa defeat them. Left-middle Tālāṅka defeats Dvivida’s monkeys; left-ring Viṣṇu’s Caturvyūha destroys Rājāsura kings. Outcome: Generated epic and royal opponents are destroyed by corresponding manifestations.. [Chapter 29, verses 109–129](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362967.html)

40. **lal-event-040** — Left-little-finger Kalki defeats Kali’s generated forces; the avatar leaders return to Vaikuṇṭha. Śāmbhava breaks Mahāmoha. Nārāyaṇa destroys the armies, Pāśupata the commanders, and Mahākāmeśvara kills Bhaṇḍa and burns Śūnyaka, including its women, children and stores. Lalitā brings exhausted troops back at sunset. Outcome: Bhaṇḍa and capital are destroyed; the fourth day closes.. [Chapter 29, verses 130–148](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362967.html)

41. **lal-event-041** — Lalitā’s healing gaze refreshes her army. Gods praise her cosmic forms, recall Kāma’s failed mission and present grief-stricken Rati, asking his revival to relieve her widowhood and enable Śiva’s marriage. Outcome: Rati’s condition elicits the goddess’s compassion.. [Chapter 30, verses 1–44](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362969.html)

42. **lal-event-042** — Lalitā restores Kāma from her glance. Śyāmalā adorns Rati; sages renew their marriage. Kāma offers service. Lalitā grants effective arrows, multiplied Kāmas and immunity from Śiva’s burning, directs punishment of devotee-tormentors through illicit desire, and fulfilment for respectful worshippers. Outcome: Restored Kāma receives protection and a mission.. [Chapter 30, verses 45–64](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362969.html)

43. **lal-event-043** — Kāma’s emanations spread; with Rati, Vasanta, moon and breeze he shoots Śiva, then Pārvatī. Mutual longing grows. Her father prescribes penance: winter immersion and summer fires under the sun. Śiva is pleased; formal marriage and residence at Kailāsa follow. Outcome: Śiva and Gaurī marry after her austerities.. [Chapter 30, verses 65–97](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/lalitopakhyana-lalita-mahatmya/d/doc362969.html)

His father trains him; he leads the gods, kills Tāraka and marries Indra’s daughter Devasenā. Kāma returns to serve Lalitā in Śrīpura. Outcome: Tāraka’s defeat closes the origin-to-restoration chain; Kāma returns to Lalitā.. ## Kāmanda’s previous life and merit-first rebirth

ID: `gup-story-kamanda`. Scope: Upāsanā7.8–8.31. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Death, judgment and royal rebirth close the prior-life arc at8.31;8.32 returns to present frame.

Source narration order preserved; cosmic and past-life retrospections do not assert universal historical chronology.

17. **gup-event-017** — Bhṛgu says Somakānta was Kāmanda, late-born only son of wealthy Vaiśya Cidrūpa and charitable Sulocanā at Kolhāra near Vindhya. Parents doted on him and married him to devout Kuṭumbinī; seven sons and five daughters followed. Father died; mother burned with him and went to heaven. Kāmanda squandered inherited wealth with companions, rejected wife’s warning and sold the house; wife went with children to her father. Outcome: Family separates after squandered wealth.. [Upāsanā 7.8–18](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

18. **gup-event-018** — Kāmanda becomes thief, adulterer, gambler, liar and violent oppressor. Citizens obtain royal leave to exile him. In forest he kills weak people and animals but flees stronger foes, gathers thieves and builds a large stone-and-wood house. His frightened father-in-law sends wife and children there; he lives amid family and robbers supported by killings. Outcome: Robber household established in forest.. [Upāsanā 7.19–30](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

19. **gup-event-019** — Kāmanda seizes solitary learned Brahmin Guṇavardhana. Victim pleads he is innocent and newly remarried after his first wife’s death for lawful household and ancestral obligations. Saving life would make Kāmanda a father to him; even robbers protect suppliants. Killing would bring a thousand kalpas of hell, while relatives enjoying the loot would not share his sin. Outcome: Guṇavardhana appeals for life; no literal father-son kinship established.. [Upāsanā 7.31–41](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

20. **gup-event-020** — Kāmanda rejects the appeal, calls it wasted instruction, refuses release and beheads Guṇavardhana with a sharp sword. Bhṛgu declines to count innumerable killings. Old age and illness bring rejection by household, descendants and friends. Through one trusted Brahmin he summons forest sages and offers gifts; they refuse a fallen donor, describing sinful association, then leave to bathe clothed and recite purifying verses. Outcome: Killing is followed in old age by rejection of his proposed gifts.. [Upāsanā 8.1–18](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

21. **gup-event-021** — Illness, family neglect and Brahmin rejection lead Kāmanda to remorse. Brahmins identify an ancient Gaṇeśa image in a ruined forest shrine; he spends his wealth rebuilding a four-gated, four-towered decorated temple with groves and reservoirs; wife, sons, friends and kin take the remaining portion of wealth (hṛtam, not a gift). After death Yama’s messengers torture and bring him before Yama and Citragupta. Offered good or evil fruits first, he chooses merit and is reborn as Saurāṣṭra’s king Somakānta, rewarded for temple construction. Bhṛgu explains this by compassion for a suppliant and austerity power. Outcome: Merit-first choice explains royal birth; evil fruits remain.. [Upāsanā 8.19–31](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Brahmā’s humbling, initiation and creative boon

ID: `gup-story-brahma-penance`. Scope: Upāsanā14–15. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Brahmā receives requested/affirmed boon, worships, gives dakṣiṇā, and accomplishes creation at15.40.

Source narration order preserved; cosmic and past-life retrospections do not assert universal historical chronology.

36. **gup-event-036** — Somakānta asks how Brahmā created after cosmic vision. Bhṛgu explains Brahmā’s pride in knowledge and power: thousands of grotesque obstacles surround, strike, bind, mock, praise and manipulate him until he abandons pride and faints. Remembering Gaṇeśa he pleads for grace; heavenly voice orders austerity and the obstacles vanish. Outcome: Humbling obstacles end when voice orders penance.. [Upāsanā 14.1–18](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

37. **gup-event-037** — Without mantra or location Brahmā wanders through water, steadily meditating on jewelled red elephant-faced deity with serpentine sacred cord, belly serpent and forehead moon. A second heavenly voice repeats the instruction to see a banyan, again puzzling him. Outcome: Banyan instruction prepares dream initiation.. [Upāsanā 14.19–26](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

38. **gup-event-038** — In a dream Brahmā finds the surviving banyan amid dissolution waters. On a leaf appears four-armed, single-tusked elephant-faced child with crown, earrings and red dress. Child sprinkles him with trunk water, sits on his lap, rebukes his ignorance and teaches one-syllable mantra: ten lakh recitations by puraścaraṇa will bring personal appearance and creative power. Waking joyful, Brahmā bathes, stands one-footed on lotus, restrains food/senses and practices1,000 divine years; fire from his mouth afflicts beings and steadfast devotion moves Gaṇeśa to appear. Outcome: Mantra and long austerity yield deity’s appearance.. [Upāsanā 15.1–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

39. **gup-event-039** — Radiant axe/lotus-bearing Gaṇeśa overwhelms Brahmā’s sight and memory. He reassures Brahmā he is the dream initiator, adopts gentle form and offers boon. Brahmā requests firm devotion, creation ability, removal of obstacles, completion of tasks when remembered, pure knowledge and final stable liberation. Deity affirms, commands manifold creation, says remembrance destroys obstacles and explicitly grants devotion and knowledge. Outcome: Divine affirmation enables fearless creation.. [Upāsanā 15.18–32](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

40. **gup-event-040** — For worship, Brahmā’s thought brings required articles through Gaṇeśa’s favour. At dakṣiṇā two adorned maidens appear, later named Siddhi and Buddhi; he offers them, performs lamp offering, flowers, thousand-name praise and circumambulation. Gaṇeśa accepts both and disappears; Brahmā resumes broad creation by grace. This passage does not explicitly call the maidens biological daughters or narrate a marriage. Outcome: Accepted offering and accomplished creation close penance episode.. [Upāsanā 15.33–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Madhu–Kaiṭabha origin, failed wrestling, initiation, conditional defeat and divine return

ID: `gup-story-madhu-kaitabha`. Scope: Upāsanā16–18. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Opponent conditional grant, thigh/discus deaths, divine celebration, report and return completed by18.45.

Source narration order preserved; cosmic and past-life retrospections do not assert universal historical chronology.

41. **gup-event-041** — Somakānta asks the next creation account. Brahmā produces seven mental sons and asks creation help; they choose austerity and reach supreme Brahman. A second seven also refuse creation despite knowledge; Sanaka and others are mentioned. Brahmā creates Brahmins and Fire from mouth, other classes from arms/thighs/feet, Moon from heart, Sun from eyes, Wind and life from ears, space from navel, heaven from head, Earth from feet and directions from ears, then waters, mountains and plants. Outcome: Creation established before new opponent episode.. [Upāsanā 16.1–11](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

42. **gup-event-042** — Madhu and Kaiṭabha emerge from sleeping Viṣṇu’s ears, insult gods/sages/scripture and threaten to eat Brahmā, shaking Earth and Śeṣa with roars. Knowing through Gaṇeśa’s grace that Viṣṇu will kill them, Brahmā praises eye-resident sleep-goddess, addressed as Pārvatī and as creation/protection/dissolution power. He requests awakening, knowledge for Viṣṇu and delusion of opponents, explaining they worshipped him in previous birth and received various boons making them unkillable by him; his conciliatory praise failed. Outcome: Brahmā requests divine awakening and counterstrategy.. [Upāsanā 16.12–28](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

43. **gup-event-043** — While Viṣṇu wakes opponents conquer heaven and Indra/Yama/Kubera residences; gods flee. Freed from Nidrā, Viṣṇu reassures gods, carries conch/discus/mace and blows Pāñcajanya. Afraid but eager to fight, opponents claim conquests of earth, lower worlds and21heavens and demand wrestling. Viṣṇu accepts, sets weapons aside and fights body-to-body for5,000 years without victory. Outcome: Long accepted wrestling fails to defeat opponents.. [Upāsanā 17.1–18](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

44. **gup-event-044** — Viṣṇu takes a Gandharva form and plays vīṇā in forest, arresting listeners’ tasks. Hearing on Kailāsa, Śiva sends Nikumbha and Puṣpadanta to fetch musician; he honours him. Music pleases Śiva, Skanda, Gaṇeśvara, Pārvatī, gods and sages. Śiva embraces revealed Viṣṇu and offers wishes. Viṣṇu explains earwax-born foes, Brahmā’s appeal, awakening and failed wrestling, and asks a killing method. Outcome: Śiva offers aid; Viṣṇu asks opponent counterstrategy.. [Upāsanā 17.19–35](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

45. **gup-event-045** — Śiva says Viṣṇu entered battle without Vināyaka worship, losing strength. He orders worship: Gaṇeśa will delude foes and subject them, and by Śiva’s favour Viṣṇu will kill them. Asked the method, he distinguishes one-/six-syllable forms, chooses six-syllable after siddhāri-cakra and ṛṇa/dhana calculations, and promises success through observance. Exact technical calculation procedure is not given in these verses. Outcome: Six-syllable initiation sends Viṣṇu to practice.. [Upāsanā 17.36–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

46. **gup-event-046** — Asked by Somakānta, Bhṛgu describes Viṣṇu’s six-syllable practice at future Siddhakṣetra: bāṇa-astra directional sealing, purification, life installation, internal/external letter placements, breathing, mudrās, mental and16offerings, then recitation. After100years Gaṇeśa appears and offers wishes, saying earlier worship would have assured victory. Viṣṇu praises cosmic deity and requests Madhu–Kaiṭabha’s death by himself, victory/fame over other foes and unfailing devotion. Deity affirms requests, strength, renown and freedom from obstacles, then vanishes. Outcome: Personal boon supports renewed battle.. [Upāsanā 18.1–19](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

47. **gup-event-047** — Viṣṇu builds a crystal temple with gold spire and four gates, installs Gaṇḍakī-stone image; gods/sages call it Siddhivināyaka and place Siddhakṣetra because he obtained success there. Returning to foes, he rejects their mocking diminishment with examples of small fire/lamp power; they attack his chest and wrestling resumes. Outcome: Renewed wrestling sets up conditional grant.. [Upāsanā 18.20–28](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

48. **gup-event-048** — Viṣṇu praises opponents’ endurance and proposes giving a boon; māyā-deluded foes instead offer him boons, pleased with wrestling. He chooses that they become killable by him. Seeing water everywhere, they accept death from him, praise death with his remembrance as liberating and require a place not water-covered. He holds them on his thighs and cuts both heads with razor-edged discus; gods celebrate with flowers. Outcome: Conditional grant permits thigh-supported decapitation.. [Upāsanā 18.29–37](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

49. **gup-event-049** — Gandharvas dance and apsarases sing. Viṣṇu reports to Brahmā his Śiva initiation, Gaṇeśa boon and resulting victory; gods/sages praise Gaṇeśa and the triad and return home. Regular hearing of this greatness is promised freedom from fear and fulfilment. Verse printed14 between40 and42 is treated as41 by sequence and colophon total45, with transcription variant exposed. Outcome: Celebration, report and return close opponent arc.. [Upāsanā 18.38–45](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Bhīma restores ancestral worship and obtains Rukmāṅgada

ID: `gup-story-bhima`. Scope: Upāsanā19.1–39 and27.1–23. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Initial childlessness, diagnosis, initiation, devotional practice, divine promise and actual named son birth close27.23 before education/hunt episode.

Source narration order preserved; cosmic and past-life retrospections do not assert universal historical chronology.

50. **gup-event-050** — Vyāsa requests more after Siddhakṣetra. Brahmā narrates Bhīma of Vidarbha’s Kauṇḍinya, a powerful donor supported by tributary kings and vast army. His devout wife Cāruhāsinī is childless; he deems all fortune futile without a son, appoints learned ministers Manorañjana and Sumantu to keep kingdom for his return or divide it if he never returns, gives gifts and leaves with wife. At one gavyūti he sends citizens and unwilling ministers home, assuring their protection. Outcome: King entrusts kingdom and seeks aid.. [Upāsanā 19.1–22](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

51. **gup-event-051** — Bhīma and wife find lake and peaceful hermitage of Viśvāmitra with studying disciples. Sage promises a virtuous famous son and asks identity. Bhīma recounts failed austerities, giving and vows, trusts blessing, and asks prior fault/remedy. Viśvāmitra diagnoses proud abandonment of ancestral Gaṇeśa worship: deity’s anger prevents offspring. He begins why Gaṇeśa became their family deity. Outcome: Ancestral exemplum begins before remedy.. [Upāsanā 19.23–39](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

73. **gup-event-073** — Vyāsa asks Bhīma’s remedy. Viśvāmitra teaches one-syllable mantra and directs observance in Dakṣa’s temple, promising wishes, four aims and return home. Bhīma and Cāruhāsinī return honoured by citizens/ministers. King practices fasting, mantra and constant Gaṇeśa awareness in Dakṣa shrine; seeing god everywhere people think him possessed. Gaṇeśa takes his hand, calls him liberated and offers wish; king says he knows only divine feet. Deity promises virtuous golden-bodied son and commands deity/Brahmin worship. Shortly son is born and named Rukmāṅgada amid gifts. Outcome: Initiation and devotion yield promised son’s birth.. [Upāsanā 27.1–23 (printed duplicate2/missing4)](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Ballāla persecution, rescue and temple establishment

ID: `gup-story-ballala`. Scope: Upāsanā22. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Complete22 body read: persecution, curse, surrender, divine release/healing, requested devotion/site boon and actual temple installation55;23 opens future family aftermath.

Source narration order preserved; cosmic and past-life retrospections do not assert universal historical chronology.

60. **gup-event-060** — Bhīma asks Viśvāmitra why healing and sight came to Dakṣa despite Mudgala’s long austerity, and who he was before. Sage narrates Vaiśya Kalyāṇa of Pallī in Sindhu, devout donor, and wife Indumatī, whose son Ballāla grows devoted. With other boys he bathes and installs a beautiful stone as Gaṇeśa, worships with dūrvā/leaves, meditation, name-recitation, song, dance, forest shelters, flowers, lamps and offerings, and recites Purāṇa/dharma teaching. Outcome: Children’s intense worship established.. [Upāsanā 22.1–20](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

61. **gup-event-061** — Boys forget hunger/thirst; parents blame Ballāla for daily absence and wasting, threaten binding/beating and Kalyāṇa’s expulsion through town ruler. Angry father takes a large club, smashes worship shelter, scatters boys, beats unmoving Ballāla bloody, throws image away and binds son to tree with vine nooses. He taunts that god must feed/release him and threatens death if he returns home. Outcome: Devotee remains bound and injured after shrine destruction.. [Upāsanā 22.21–33](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

62. **gup-event-062** — Ballāla laments apparent failure of obstacle-removing deity and invokes unchanging natural duties of Śeṣa, Sun, Moon and Fire. He curses shrine-destroying father to blindness, deafness, hunchback and muteness if his devotion is true, declares faith cannot be bound and surrenders life. Gaṇeśa appears as Brahmin; radiance loosens bonds and body becomes whole without wounds/blood, with pure knowledge. Outcome: Divine appearance releases and heals devotee.. [Upāsanā 22.34–44](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

63. **gup-event-063** — Ballāla praises deity as universal parent/cause; Gaṇeśa embraces him and confirms shrine destroyer’s hell and bodily curse including bleeding, followed by father expelling him with mother in next embodiment. Ballāla asks firm devotion and deity’s lasting protection at this site. Grant names Ballālavināyaka, affirms steadfast mind/devotion and promises wishes to Pallī pilgrims on Bhādrapada bright fourth. God vanishes; Ballāla installs image and builds temple with Brahmins. Hearing this account is said to remove sins and fulfil wishes. Outcome: Devotion, site manifestation and temple installation granted; family aftermath continues23.. [Upāsanā 22.45–57](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Rukmāṅgada education, curse and pilgrimage

ID: `gup-story-rukmangada`. Scope: Upāsanā27.24–29 and35. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Education/hunt, coercive demand/refusal/curse, pilgrimage instruction, restored body, proxy bath-merit transfer and collective ascent completed35.45;36 opens Mukundā’s subsequent account.

Source narration order preserved; cosmic and past-life retrospections do not assert universal historical chronology.

74. **gup-event-074** — Bhīma entrusts growing Rukmāṅgada to Kapila for education; son grasps teaching on hearing and becomes learned/strong, compared with Gajakarṇa. Father consecrates him and gives priestly gifts; son increases Gaṇeśa devotion and recites father-taught one-syllable mantra daily. Hunting one day he kills game, becomes exhausted and reaches a sage’s peaceful hermitage. Outcome: Education, accession and hunt open new opponent episode.. [Upāsanā 27.24–30](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

75. **gup-event-075** — Exhausted Rukmāṅgada honours sage Vācaknavi and wife Mukundā. While sage bathes, prince addresses her as mother and requests cold water to live. Attracted, she demands affection; he refuses adultery through Vināyaka’s grace and rejects her water. She claims consensual relations exempt punishment and women had freedom in early ages, threatens destruction/exile, seizes and forcibly kisses him. He pushes her away and insists refusal; angry she curses him to leprosy and he leaves distressed. Outcome: Refusal of coercive sexual demand brings Mukundā’s curse.. [Upāsanā 28.1–18](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

76. **gup-event-076** — Prince sees white diseased body replacing golden beauty, questions Gaṇeśa why a devotee suffers and recalls deity’s protective manifestations. Maintaining exclusive refuge, he refuses to show his appearance and resolves fasting unto death beneath banyan. Servants search unsuccessfully until night and go home. Outcome: Prince withdraws to fasting; servants cannot find him.. [Upāsanā 28.19–27](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

77. **gup-event-077** — Rukmāṅgada sees Nārada in sky, honours and asks refuge, recounting hunt, water request, Mukundā’s unwanted kiss/demand, refusal and leprosy, and fears Bhīma’s grief. Nārada says he witnessed an old leprous Śūdra pilgrim at Vidarbha’s Kadamba city bathe in Gaṇeśakuṇḍa before Cintāmaṇi image, gain divine body and ascend by Gaṇeśa attendants’ vehicle to a non-returning abode. He prescribes bath, deity worship and Brahmin giving; prince is joyful. Outcome: Witnessed healing supplies remedy.. [Upāsanā 29.1–19](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

78. **gup-event-078** — Before sage departs prince asks who installed image and first attained success, praising wandering sages’ aid. Nārada accepts question and promises complete account. Outcome: Nested Indra–Ahalyā episode opens.. [Upāsanā 29.20–26](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

92. **gup-event-092** — Vyāsa asks prince’s next step. Army finds diseased Rukmāṅgada after searching mountains/woods/rivers; he recounts curse and Nārada’s prescription, invites them to bathe, give and worship then return. At sight of Gaṇeśatīrtha he already gains former golden divine body, before the subsequently narrated bath; he recognises Nārada’s true instruction. Outcome: Arrival and sight heal before ritual bath.. [Upāsanā 35.1–16](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

93. **gup-event-093** — After bath, Brahmin gifts and Vināyaka worship, prince and witnesses see radiant aerial vehicle with Gaṇeśa attendants, apsarases and kinnaras. He asks identity; messengers explain deity’s command to bring surrendered devotee Rukmāṅgada to himself after fulfilled pilgrimage. Prince marvels at favour and honours them, comparing his limited mind with transcendent divine cause. Outcome: Divine invitation to ascent offered.. [Upāsanā 35.17–31](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

94. **gup-event-094** — Prince refuses ascent without father Bhīma and mother Cāruhāsinī, noting her devotion. Messengers prescribe bathing for them and transfer merit, then they too can ascend. Prince makes kuśa proxies, recites symbolic bath mantra and bathes for all village people. He returns by vehicle to Kauṇḍinya with retinue, transfers merit to parents and all inhabitants; additional vehicles arrive and everyone, down to children and outcastes, ascends to Vināyaka. Devoted hearing of this greatness is promised same destination. Outcome: Proxy merit transfer allows collective ascent and closes prince’s pilgrimage arc.. [Upāsanā 35.32–47 (printed missing34)](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Indra impersonation, curse, initiation, Cintāmaṇi boon and restoration

ID: `gup-story-indra`. Scope: Upāsanā30–34. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Impersonation, discovery, curses, collective intercession, initiated bodily restoration, personal austerity/boon, temple installation and return to office completed34.39;34.41–43 returns to prince frame. Ahalyā foot-touch relief remains future promise within this bounded account.

Source narration order preserved; cosmic and past-life retrospections do not assert universal historical chronology.

79. **gup-event-079** — Nārada says Indra honoured him in Amarāvatī and requested wonder. He described Gautama’s hermitage and Ahalyā’s beauty, claiming his own distraction; comparisons name Sāvitrī, Śacī, Lakṣmī, Girijā, Urvaśī, Menakā, Rambhā, Tilottamā, Anasūyā, Arundhatī, Sun’s wives Chāyā/Saṃjñā and Kaśyapa’s Aditi. He then vanished; this is Nārada’s narrated prior speech. Outcome: Beauty report excites Indra’s desire.. [Upāsanā 30.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

80. **gup-event-080** — Indra, inflamed by Nārada’s report, assumes Gautama’s form and enters hermitage during sage’s bath. Ahalyā questions early return and daytime desire; impersonator invents seeing a bathing apsaras, threatens death, curse or renunciation unless served. Believing voice, appearance and manner are her husband, she follows his command and intercourse occurs. Outcome: Ahalyā is deceived by assumed form and fabricated explanation.. [Upāsanā 30.10–21](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

81. **gup-event-081** — Divine fragrance alarms Ahalyā; fearing scandal and husband’s response she demands stranger identify himself or face curse. Indra reveals ornaments/crown and identity as Śacī’s husband, then asks continued intimacy. Angry she accuses him of breaking chastity by deceit and anticipates Gautama’s curse. Number labels26/30 appear as56/32; sequence and final total32 retained without renumbered quotation. Outcome: Victim confronts revealed deceiver.. [Upāsanā 30.22–32 (printed56/32 anomalies)](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

82. **gup-event-082** — Gautama returns and asks why wife neither greets nor serves as usual. Trembling Ahalyā prostrates and explains mistaken obedience to his assumed form, fragrance-triggered suspicion and revelation under threat, then asks forgiveness. Sage, furious, accuses her of failing recognition and curses her to stone; he adds that Daśarathi Rāma’s foot-touch during forest wandering will restore her form. She immediately becomes stone; restoration remains future here. Outcome: Stone transformation realized; foot-touch relief promised.. [Upāsanā 31.1–15](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

83. **gup-event-083** — Indra hears curse, considers hiding underwater and takes cat form. Gautama identifies him in meditation and says he will not burn deity to ashes but curses him, as Śacī’s husband, to a thousand bhagas. Transformation occurs immediately. Indra regrets disregarded elders, blaming Nārada’s words and desire, vows karmic expiation as animal and hides in lotus bud as indragopaka insect. Outcome: Thousand-mark curse manifests and offender hides.. [Upāsanā 31.16–28](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

84. **gup-event-084** — Nārada tells assembled gods led by Bṛhaspati of both curses. Concerned for Indra, Śacī and cosmic government, they seek Gautama together and praise sage-power, recounting Gautama’s same-day grain aid, Vālakhilya-created second Indra later made bird ruler at Brahmā’s plea, unnamed sage’s ocean-drinking, Gādhi’s son creating another world and Cyavana stopping Indra’s arm. They beg compassion for surrendered gods. Outcome: Collective intercession begins.. [Upāsanā 32.1–18](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

85. **gup-event-085** — Gautama welcomes gods and asks their request. They ask pardon and Indra’s return to office, citing Śiva burning Kāma while sage spared offender’s life. Gautama says unrepentant fallen deceiver lacks expiation but honours their collective plea: transmit Vināyaka’s six-syllable mantra; initiation will give divine body, convert all bhagas to eyes and restore office. Gods worship, circumambulate, receive leave and go to Indra. Outcome: Conditional mantra remedy offered through intercessors.. [Upāsanā 32.19–36](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

86. **gup-event-086** — Gods with Nārada call Indra from lotus, advise confessing rather than hiding fault, and offer Gautama’s remedy, recalling Brahmā’s emission after seeing a toe at Girijā–Śiva wedding and Śiva’s remedy. Indra emerges foul and wounded, bathes and sips ritually; Bṛhaspati teaches six-syllable Gaṇeśa mantra. Initiation immediately yields divine body and1,000eyes; gods rejoice, shower flowers and sages bless. Outcome: Mantra initiation realizes bodily conversion.. [Upāsanā 33.1–14](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

87. **gup-event-087** — Indra thanks gods and sages, confesses wrongful act following Nārada and asks how they won Gautama’s favour. They explain intercession and command Amarāvatī return. He refuses to return without Gaṇeśa’s favour, sends helpers home and remains to practice, acknowledging their restoration of him. Outcome: Indra postpones return pending deity’s favour.. [Upāsanā 33.15–24 (printed duplicate18)](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

88. **gup-event-088** — Indra practices six-syllable mantra beneath kadamba, nose-tip gaze, restraint and air-only diet for1,000years; ant hills and bushes cover motionless body. Brilliant four-armed Gaṇeśa appears and frightened practitioner mistakes him for obstacle. God reassures and identifies himself as meditated transcendent creator, pleased with austerity and ready to grant; Indra recognises and bows. Outcome: Deity appears and offers boon after1,000years.. [Upāsanā 34.1–14](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

89. **gup-event-089** — Indra praises unknowable Gaṇeśa and recalls impermanent office earned by100sacrifices. He says Brahmā earlier taught this mantra and warned forgetting it would cause loss of office and distress; forgetting preceded Ahalyā’s violation. Bṛhaspati’s renewed instruction now produced1,000eyes and sight of deity. Outcome: Earlier warning and repeated initiation explicitly distinguished.. [Upāsanā 34.15–24](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

90. **gup-event-090** — Indra asks constant nonforgetfulness and feet-directed mind, renaming Kadamba as Cintāmaṇipura, lake as Cintāmaṇitīrtha and bath/giving benefits of four aims and siddhis. Gaṇeśa affirms all requests and adds stable office, constant remembrance and divine appearance to accomplish tasks whenever crisis brings remembrance; site bath and thought-fulfilment promises are reiterated. Outcome: Devotion, site efficacy and protected office granted.. [Upāsanā 34.25–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

91. **gup-event-091** — Indra brings heavenly Gaṅgā for deity’s ablution and worship; after divine disappearance he installs crystal image and vast jewelled temple, bows/circumambulates and returns to office. Gaṅgā is said to continue daily ablution by his command. Nārada ends site history and instructs Rukmāṅgada to bathe for all-fault freedom, blesses him and departs. Outcome: Indra returns; narrator hands remedy back to prince.. [Upāsanā 34.35–43](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Brahmā’s restored creation, meditation-born Vakratunda and Dambha’s surrender

ID: `mud-story-brahma-dambha`. Scope: Mudgala1.40–45; final aftermath1.45.35–39, new Vāmana transition40. Newly completed this continuation: False.

All six chapter bodies directly read and major transitions extracted, including pride/obstacles, sonhood promise/fulfilment, Dambha’s body-tremor origin, austerity/request/grant/consecration, named conquests and reversals, mental-son nurture, envoy exchange, two guru dialogues, surrender and assigned duties/aftermath.

Complete bounded author-level story; independent Sanskrit/edition review pending. No biological womb birth inferred for meditation son or Dambha. No final fight or killing inferred. Siddhi/Buddhi narrative daughterhood and own-limb māyā teaching retained locally.

1. **mud-event-brahma-pride** — Dakṣa asks how Brahmā worshipped Vakratunda. Mudgala recalls Gaṇeśa creating five divine lords and empowering them through austerity/boon. Beginning creation without remembering or worshipping Gaṇeśa, Brahmā prides himself on indispensability: without his creation, Viṣṇu, Rudra, Śakti and sun lack their functions. Many-eyed/many-faced Vighna hosts batter and toy with him. He relinquishes pride, takes refuge, remembers and hymns Gaṇeśa; the hosts vanish. Outcome: Humiliation and recollection remove the immediate obstruction.. [Mudgala1.40.1–20](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **mud-event-brahma-vak-tapas** — Freed of obstacles, Brahmā propitiates Vakratunda with six-syllable mantra, feeding on air, standing on toe-tip, holding unwavering mental focus. After a divine thousand of austerity, disturbing beings, Vakratunda comes lion-mounted, three-eyed, elephant-faced, four-armed, with noose/goad/boon/fearlessness, Siddhi/Buddhi, Cintāmaṇi, Śeṣa at navel and red body. Brahmā fears the radiance and requests gentleness. Vakratunda softens it, explains the form ordinarily belongs to Svānanda and manifested through Brahmā’s devotion, then offers wishes. Outcome: Gentle manifestation prompts Brahmā’s hymn and boon request.. [Mudgala1.40.21–35](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **mud-event-brahma-son-boon** — Brahmā praises Vakratunda’s Brahman, lion/elephant/four-arm form and Siddhi/Buddhi, requests unwavering devotion, unobstructed creative capacity, and the deity dwelling at home as son through māyā, freeing parental bondage and allowing continuous nurture/worship. Vakratunda grants all: creative power, obstacles removed by remembrance, desired objects, and rescuing sonhood through māyā/aṃśa; hymn grants all and Brahmanhood. He vanishes; Brahmā creates the whole animate/inanimate three worlds properly and rests remembering Vakratunda. Outcome: Creation restored; meditation-son promise awaits fulfilment.. [Mudgala1.41.1–22](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **mud-event-dambha-origin** — Wind shakes Brahmā and Dambha manifests from his body-tremor as a great four-armed armed man. Asked identity, he claims birth from Brahmā’s tremor and sonhood, requests name, place and livelihood. Brahmā laughingly names him Dambha because of his boast, permits movement everywhere and enjoyments. Dambha bows and circles him, reaches earth, resents perceived disrespect and vows to remove Brahmā’s pride. He seeks Kavi, receives a mantra, and worships Brahmā foodless on one foot for ten thousand years, disturbing creation. Pleased Brahmā arrives with gods, astonished at his wooden-stillness and offers a boon. Outcome: Dambha’s retaliation motive leads to intense worship of his origin deity.. [Mudgala1.41.23–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **mud-event-dambha-boon-coronation** — Offered wishes, Dambha hymns Brahmā as threefold Brahmā/Viṣṇu/Śiva and all creation. He asks three-world rule, no death from five-element beings, no equal in battle, undivided sovereignty with health. Brahmā grants all requested and any contemplated object’s success, then disappears. Dambha establishes beautiful Śobhana city on earth. Śukra summons demons, comes with Brahman-knowing sages and has Brahmins consecrate him at auspicious time. The festival ends with chiefs remaining in the city. Outcome: Brahmā’s grant supports Dambha’s demon kingship.. [Mudgala1.41.41–59](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

6. **mud-event-dambha-conquest** — Suhuṇḍa, Huṇḍa, Rukmaketu, Madyapa, Sakopa, Krodhāsura, Kāmāsura, Rāsabhāsura and other chiefs urge conquest as Dambha’s servants. He agrees, summons Śukra and accepts his counsel, then marches with fourfold forces, defeats terrestrial kings and Pātāla Nāgas, and goes to heaven. Indra’s vajra burns and crushes demons and makes them flee; Dambha’s discus fells Indra, arrows harm gods. Recovering Indra flees to live; Dambha sits on Airāvata and Indra’s throne. Outcome: Dambha conquers three worlds; Indra abandons his office.. [Mudgala1.42.1–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

7. **mud-event-dambha-defeats-siva** — Indra and gods take refuge in Brahmā, then Viṣṇu and Śiva. Śiva angrily challenges Dambha; forty days of destructive battle follow. Dambha’s arrows fell divine chiefs. Śiva’s trident fells Dambha, who rises immediately and hurls discus, felling Śiva for half a prahara. Regaining consciousness, Śiva flees with gods to the forest. Dambha installs demons in Satyaloka, Vaikuṇṭha, Kailāsa, Amarāvatī and other divine cities, then returns to Śobhana ruling three worlds. Outcome: Dambha supplants divine offices after reversible defeats on both sides.. [Mudgala1.42.18–30](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

8. **mud-event-dambha-ritual-suppression** — Dambha orders karma destroyed. Demons bind and beat Brahmins, break sacrificial spaces and trees, destroy tīrthas and temples. Some Brahmins die or abandon duties, others flee forests; sacrificial exclamations and social dharma cease. Gods, dependent on ritual food, appeal to Brahmā as creator/provider and giver of fearlessness: without karma and food they face starvation and death. Outcome: Suppression of sacrifice provokes divine appeal for rescue.. [Mudgala1.42.31–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

9. **mud-event-mental-son-manifestation** — Unable to devise rescue, Brahmā advises one-syllable worship of Vakratunda. Gods/sages worship; he meditates on the previously seen Siddhi/Buddhi-bearing form. After a hundred years he sees the speaking form in his heart, then it emerges with Siddhi and Buddhi. Vakratunda says he arose from Brahmā’s meditation, is dhyānaja, and fulfils the former requested sonhood. Brahmā bows and worships all three. The narrative calls him mental son, Siddhi/Buddhi daughters, and describes Vakratunda as with wife/wives (sabhāryaka) while all prepare a hymn. Outcome: Promised sonhood is fulfilled by meditation-origin, not womb birth.. [Mudgala1.43.1–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

10. **mud-event-child-nurture-envoy** — Brahmā with gods/sages hymns the all-form Brahman deity and marvels that the meditated Siddhi/Buddhi-bearing Brahman came out as his son. Brahmā and Brahmins perform birth rites; on day eleven name him Siddhibuddhipati. He plays as a child, Sāvitrī nurses him, and his deeds please mother and father. At two years, with Siddhi/Buddhi, he asks why father dwells sorrowfully in forest. Brahmā tells Dambha’s story. The child promises to destroy Dambha and restore offices, goes lion-mounted with Siddhi/Buddhi near the city, and sends Indra to demand own-dharma, restored sacrificial food and social order. Dambha spares Indra as envoy, otherwise threatens imprisonment, and sets battle on fifth day. Indra reports and is praised. Outcome: A new child manifestation offers rescue and attempts conciliation.. [Mudgala1.43.18–48](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

11. **mud-event-sukra-siddhi-buddhi** — Dambha summons Kavi/Śukra, prostrates, receives a gold seat and asks who Siddhi/Buddhi and their lord are. Śukra explains Siddhi as alluring māyā and people’s pursuit of worldly/otherworldly/Brahman fulfilment, left at Gaṇeśa’s side; Buddhi as knowledge/mind with five citta states and innumerable variants, at his right. Both māyās were formed from the deity’s own limb for play; Svānanda is Brahman and his abode. Brahmā requested sonhood to remove parental bondage; this deity came accordingly. He warns Dambha to surrender because this form will restore dharma and destroy him. Outcome: Guru explains the opponent’s identity, powers and manifestation purpose.. [Mudgala1.44.1–27](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

12. **mud-event-dambha-impartiality-query** — Dambha asks why impartial Gaṇeśa, from whom dharma/adharma arise, protects one and destroys the other. Kāvya explains an ordered world with separate places: greed-driven excess adharma strengthens Asuras and weakens gods, prompting Gaṇeśa to restore dharma; excessive dharma threatens demon root-destruction, so he grants demons boons to restore adharma’s assigned place. Neither party is self/other to him; greed-driven imbalance draws intervention. Dambha meditates privately, concludes that seeking siddhi or welfare while abandoning their lord causes failure, resolves surrender and sleeps fearlessly with Gaṇeśa in his heart. Outcome: Doctrinal answer changes Dambha’s decision before battle.. [Mudgala1.44.28–49](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

13. **mud-event-dambha-surrender** — Next morning Dambha abandons Asura disposition and tells assembled demons before Śukra that guru taught him to surrender to Vakratunda, Brahman everywhere, neither god-only nor demon-only lord. Angry demons abandon him and return home. Joyful Dambha and Kāvya go outside to Gaṇeśa, prostrate, worship and hymn his Brahman, yoga, impartial, elephant-faced/Siddhi-Buddhi-lord forms. He falls again; Gaṇeśa raises him. Outcome: Dambha surrenders with his guru; army declines to accompany him.. [Mudgala1.45.1–18](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

14. **mud-event-dambha-conditional-livelihood** — Gaṇeśa promises Dambha’s hymn joy, family/wealth/grain, relief of mental/physical affliction and troubles; regular once/twice/thrice-daily recitation pleases him. He came to kill but spares the surrendered devotee. Dambha requests firm devotion, instructions for livelihood and a stable place. Siddhibuddhipati grants unwavering devotion and own-station residence without fear for a long time; gods are not higher nor Dambha lower, all pleasing when faithful to own duty. Dambha must destroy undertakings begun without remembrance, make ritual actions fruitless without first worship, and protect beloved devotees free of Dambha disposition; acts lacking initial remembrance/worship become his karma-share. Outcome: Survival, devotion and conditional obstruction/protection duties replace battle.. [Mudgala1.45.19–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

15. **mud-event-dambha-aftermath** — Gaṇeśa disappears after assigning Dambha’s duties. Gods/sages acclaim victory and return radiant and fearless to their own stations; demons dwell untroubled in Pātāla cavities. Mudgala concludes Dambha’s pacification and Brahmā-sonhood and promises attentive listeners/tellers freedom from Dambha fear, Brahman, desired objects, prosperity and freedom from obstacles. He explicitly transitions to Vāmana. Outcome: Divine offices restored and demons peacefully resident; next arc is Vāmana.. [Mudgala1.45.35–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Vāmana and Bali: omitted worship, three steps, refuge and repaired sacrifice

ID: `mud-story-vamana-bali`. Scope: Mudgala1.46–47, final resolution47.60–66. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Both chapter bodies directly read; Kaśyapa/Aditi origin, planned100 sacrifices, Vighna heart-entry, Viṣṇu promised birth, initial failure, six-syllable austerity/boon/site, three steps/debt, Bali’s refuge, Vighna exit/Viṣṇu repentance, head gift, joint petition and repaired sacrifice/future office all extracted.

This witness’s distinctive sequence retained, without importing mainstream details (Śukra spout obstruction, eye loss, Prahlāda intervention, Manu-specific future office) absent here. Future Indra and Pātāla are decree/promise, not all enacted scenes.

1. **mud-event-bali-ritual-plan** — Dakṣa asks Vāmana’s story. Mudgala introduces Kaśyapa, his senior virtuous wife Aditi, and gods including Indra born to them; opposing demons are also sage’s sons, defeated by Viṣṇu’s varied forms. They seek Kāvya, who advises100 Aśvamedhas with concealed hostility, making Bali Indra and demons celestial enjoyers. Bali, born of Virocana, is Viṣṇu-devoted and dislikes adharma. Yet he begins the first sacrifice remembering Viṣṇu and omitting Gaṇeśa. Vighna decides to consume its ritual fruits, enters Indra’s heart; Indra fears100 sacrifices will replace him and asks Viṣṇu to destroy them. Vighna then enters Viṣṇu’s heart as time-form. Outcome: Neglected initial worship creates obstruction through the divine opponents.. [Mudgala1.46.1–21](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **mud-event-vamana-birth-austerity** — Viṣṇu tells Indra that Aditi and Kaśyapa previously worshipped him intensely for100 years and requested his sonhood; he agreed and will fulfil it now to stop Bali’s sacrifice. Forgetting Bali’s devotion, Viṣṇu is born Vāmana to Kaśyapa in Aditi, but cannot displace mighty Bali. He asks father Kaśyapa for means; receives Vakratunda’s six-syllable mantra, goes to Vidarbha and worships foodless for10 years, meditating Gaṇeśa. Vakratunda appears through mantra/austerity, lion-mounted, four-armed, one-tusked, elephant-faced, red, with Siddhi/Buddhi and noose/goad. Vāmana rises, bows and begins hymn. Outcome: Vāmana seeks Gaṇeśa’s aid after initial inability to move Bali.. [Mudgala1.46.22–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **mud-event-vamana-hymn-boon** — Vāmana hymns Vakratunda as obstacle remover/giver, indwelling lord, and eminent forms including Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Śambhu, Indra, sun, moon, Yama, Varuṇa, Vāyu, Kubera, Agni, Nirṛti, Śeṣa, Śukra, Sanatkumāra and Guha. He dances with tears and horripilation, losing body-awareness in exclusive devotion. Pleased deity offers wishes, promises hymn siddhi, desired objects and final Svānanda liberation. Vāmana requests firm devotion, Bali brought within his power, the deity’s appearance whenever remembered, and freedom from obstruction. Gaṇeśa agrees and disappears; Vāmana installs him, establishing Adoṣa field in Vidarbha. Outcome: All requested granted, with deity installed at Adoṣa.. [Mudgala1.46.35–58](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **mud-event-three-step-demand** — After worshipping Vakratunda and bowing to Kaśyapa, Vāmana reaches Bali’s sacrifice. Sages marvel at radiant short Vedic student; Bali rises, bows, worships and offers a gift. Śukra understands purpose and the initial worship omission producing a Viṣṇu-form obstacle. Vāmana asks land for three steps; Bali grants with devotion. Remembering Gaṇeśa, Vāmana becomes Virāṭ, covers heaven with one step and earth with second, demands place for third. Grieving Bali cannot answer; angry Viṣṇu binds him as debtor to a Brahmin and tells him to go to Naraka. Bali realizes his Gaṇeśa omission has brought this treatment even from his chosen Viṣṇu, and turns to Gaṇeśa for rescue. Outcome: Bali is bound for unfulfilled third step, then seeks Gaṇeśa.. [Mudgala1.47.1–15](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **mud-event-bali-hymn-protection** — Bali hymns Gaṇeśa as obstacle lord, all-form/impartial Brahman, Siddhi-Buddhi lord and māyā-remover; confesses own pride/envy and repeated offences, asks protection from falling into Naraka, and explains refuge gives gods freedom from illusion/obstacles. A sky-voice promises surrendered Bali no fear of Naraka through remembrance. His hymn frees readers/hearers of that fear, grants four aims and increases devotion; joyful Bali keeps meditating. Outcome: Bali receives explicit protection from Naraka before debt is discharged.. [Mudgala1.47.16–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

6. **mud-event-vishnu-repentance-third-step** — Vighna leaves Viṣṇu, immediately restoring understanding. Vāmana realizes he harmed his full devotee Bali and a Brahmin debt could send him to Naraka; he seeks Gaṇeśa and remembers him. Gaṇeśa appears immediately, receives worship from Viṣṇu and gods/sages. Gaining insight from the sight, Bali offers his body and asks the third foot placed on his head. Viṣṇu agrees, discharging the Brahmin debt, and begins hymn with assembly. Outcome: Both understand; third-step head gift discharges debt.. [Mudgala1.47.35–45](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

7. **mud-event-bali-restored-future-office** — Vāmana hymns Gaṇeśa’s supremacy/māyā, begs forgiveness and sacrificial fruit for Bali, acknowledging Bali’s belief in Viṣṇu’s superiority led to omitted worship. Gaṇeśa says he had earlier promised Naraka through Viṣṇu’s hands, but now Bali has taken bodily/mental/verbal refuge and Viṣṇu also petitions. He orders finishing sacrifice with him first; Bali thereby gains all sacrifices’ fruit, but current fruitlessness lets him experience offence. Bali will be Indra after current Indra retires; for now establish him in Pātāla. All approve; Bali completes the final sacrifice placing Gaṇeśa first. Deity vanishes, gods return home; hearing the arc grants wishes. Outcome: Sacrifice repaired; Pātāla placement ordered and future Indra office promised.. [Mudgala1.47.46–66](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Durgama, Śatākṣī and Śākambharī

ID: `dbx-story-durgama`. Scope: Devī Bhāgavata7.28.1–83, drought through Vedas’ return. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Bounded narrative chronology checked against whole cited English body; no exhaustive Sanskrit atomic completeness or other recension equivalence claimed.

Translation narrator statements and attributed dialogue distinguished. No remembered details added; unknown spouse/mother/guru fields remain unstated.

1. **dbx-event-durgama-tapas** — Vyāsa answers Janamejaya: Ruru’s son Durgama, of Hiraṇyākṣa’s family, plans to obtain Vedas so gods lose offerings. He worships Brahmā in the Himalaya for1,000years on air. Brahmā offers a boon; Durgama requests all Vedic mantras of gods/Brahmins and conquering strength; Brahmā agrees. Outcome: Brahmins forget Vedas and Vedic rites cease.. [Devī Bhāgavata 7.28.1–45](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57285.html)

2. **dbx-event-durgama-drought** — Without Vedic rites gods weaken. Durgama attacks Amarāvatī; gods flee into Sumeru caves. Rain ceases for100years, drying water and killing people and animals. Brahmins fast and pray to the Mother in the Himalaya. Outcome: The Goddess answers the petition.. [Devī Bhāgavata 7.28.1–45](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57285.html)

3. **dbx-event-sataksi-food** — The Goddess appears with innumerable eyes and four arms holding bow, arrows, lotus and food. She weeps for nine nights, restoring water; many-eyed Śatākṣī distributes vegetables, roots and fruits until crops return, and grass for animals, receiving the designation Śākambharī. Outcome: Famine relief precedes the battle.. [Devī Bhāgavata 7.28.1–45](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57285.html); [Devī Bhāgavata 7.28.46–68](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57285.html)

4. **dbx-event-durgama-confront** — Messengers tell Durgama of the rescue; he surrounds the defenders with1,000akṣauhiṇīs. A luminous enclosure protects gods and Brahmins while the Goddess fights outside. Arrow clouds darken the sky; frictional fire illuminates it. Fourteen named Śaktis,10,000Guhya Kālikās, groups of32and64and innumerable others emerge and fight. Outcome: After the Śaktis destroy100akṣauhiṇīs, instruments sound and Durgama first confronts them; ten days of fighting destroy all his troops.. [Devī Bhāgavata 7.28.46–68](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57285.html)

5. **dbx-event-durgama-defeat** — On day11 red-clothed Durgama advances in his chariot, overcomes Śaktis and faces the Goddess for two praharas. Her15arrows strike four horses, charioteer, two eyes, two arms, flag, and five strike his heart. He vomits blood and dies; his luminosity merges into the Goddess. Outcome: Durgama dies; this is no surrender.. [Devī Bhāgavata 7.28.46–68](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57285.html)

6. **dbx-event-durgama-return** — Gods including Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva praise the victor. She hands the Vedas to the Brahmins and instructs that they be guarded as parts of her body. She connects Durgā with killing Durgama and names Durgā/Śatākṣī remembrance as liberating from Māyā; the many-eye naming explanation occurred earlier. She enjoins both Suras and Asuras to serve her alone and read her deeds, then disappears. Vyāsa directs secrecy for this mystery and promises daily devoted hearing fulfills wishes and ends in honor in Devī Loka. Outcome: Vedic possession restored; story closes.. [Devī Bhāgavata 7.28.69–83](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57285.html); [Devī Bhāgavata 7.28.1–45](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57285.html)

## Aruṇa and Bhrāmarī

ID: `dbx-story-bhramari`. Scope: Devī Bhāgavata10.13.33–127; opening six-Manu account excluded from this bounded arc. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Bounded narrative chronology checked against whole cited English body; no exhaustive Sanskrit atomic completeness or other recension equivalence claimed.

Translation narrator statements and attributed dialogue distinguished. No remembered details added; unknown spouse/mother/guru fields remain unstated.

1. **dbx-event-aruna-austerity** — Aruṇa lives in a nether Daitya city, then undertakes Brahmā worship beside Ganges in Himalaya. Four successive10,000year stages use dry leaves, water drops, air and finally no food; he repeats Gāyatrī continuously. His ascetic heat threatens worlds; gods petition Brahmā. Brahmā appears with Gāyatrī and four Vedas. Outcome: Brahmā offers a boon.. [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.33–59](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html)

2. **dbx-event-aruna-request** — Aruṇa first requests immortality. Brahmā refuses an impossible boon and says Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva too are mortal. Aruṇa revises the request to the specified war/weapon/sex/limb-category death exclusions and strength to conquer gods. Brahmā assents. Outcome: A qualified boon, not universal immortality, is granted.. [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.33–59](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html)

3. **dbx-event-aruna-conquest** — Aruṇa gathers nether Daityas, sends a messenger to Indra and conquers heaven, taking forms/offices of sun, moon, Yama, Agni and others through ascetic power. Gods seek counsel with Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva at Kailāsa. Outcome: Gods cannot readily defeat him.. [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.60–77](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html); [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.33–59](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html)

4. **dbx-event-aruna-gayatri-loss** — A heavenly voice directs worship of the world Queen and identifies cessation of Gāyatrī repetition as the condition making Aruṇa killable. Indra sends Bṛhaspati; gods perform Devī sacrifice and Māyā-bīja worship. Bṛhaspati approaches as a muni and argues that sharing the gods’ Goddess worship undermines Aruṇa’s hostility. Aruṇa abandons Gāyatrī through pride and loses strength; Bṛhaspati reports success. Outcome: Gods continue worship; a long interval of unspecified duration follows.. [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.60–77](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html); [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.78–109](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html)

5. **dbx-event-bhramari-appears** — After continued worship Bhrāmarī appears with diverse garments and ornaments, bees in her fists and garland, and boon/fearlessness gestures. Bee sound is associated with Hrīm. Gods offer an extended hymn, then describe Aruṇa’s boon and injuries. Hymnic names are attributed praise, not separate narrated births. Outcome: Bhrāmarī responds to their crisis.. [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.78–109](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html)

6. **dbx-event-bhramari-defeat** — Bhrāmarī releases bees/hornets from every side. Their multitude covers the world and attacks the Daityas’ chests. The text denies the foes time or capacity to fight, wield weapons or speak; principal Daityas perish immediately. Bees return to the Goddess; gods rejoice with praise, music and dance. Outcome: Aruṇa’s forces perish; no negotiated surrender.. [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.110–127](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html); [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.78–109](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html)

7. **dbx-event-bhramari-boons** — The Goddess grants separate boons to the gods, whose individual contents are not specified here, and grants their express request for unshakeable devotion. She disappears; Nārāyaṇa concludes with liberation promised for hearing the account. Outcome: Narrative closes.. [Devī Bhāgavata 10.13.110–127](https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/devi-bhagavata-purana/d/doc57377.html)

## Naimiṣa audience, Vyāsa-trained Sūta and the Mudgala–Dakṣa frame

1. **rmd2-event-invocation** — The opening invokes Gaṇeśa as complete yoga, giver of siddhis, heart-resident of yogins, the human/elephant form with attributed human and attribute-free elephant aspects; salutations include Śiva, Viṣṇu, Sūrya, Śakti, Brahmā, Śeṣa, Nara and Nārāyaṇa and the gods, yogins and sages. Outcome: Invocations establish the local teaching frame.. [Mudgala 1.1.1–7](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **rmd2-event-naimisha** — Fearful of Kali faults and instructed by Brahmā, Śaunaka and other sages reside at Naimiṣa with Vedic study, five sacrifices and daily/occasional duties. Sūta arrives, marvels at their conduct, bows, praises the sight of their feet as liberating, and accepts their offered seat and reciprocal welcome. Outcome: Sūta is seated as narrator.. [Mudgala 1.1.8–24](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

Requested to recite, Sūta credits his teacher and tells many accounts and doctrines associated with the four human aims; the listeners rejoice. Outcome: Many narratives have been heard before the present question.. [Mudgala 1.1.25–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **rmd2-event-supremacy-question** — Hearing differing accounts that call Śiva, Viṣṇu, Sūrya, Śakti, Gaṇeśa, Virāj, Brahman, yoga, knowledge, action, self, breath, food, mind, intellect, Sāṃkhya or Svānanda supreme leaves the sages uncertain. Śaunaka asks for one scripturally accepted conclusion; Sūta introduces Mudgala’s teaching as that essence. Outcome: The Mudgala–Dakṣa frame is introduced.. [Mudgala 1.1.41–50](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **rmd2-event-daksha-aftermath-frame** — Sūta briefly previews the ruined sacrifice: Rudra’s forces defeat gods, Vīrabhadra beheads Dakṣa, Brahmā conciliates Śiva and Śiva revives Dakṣa through yoga. Distressed and curse-confused Dakṣa receives the Gaṇeśa devotee Mudgala, addresses him as Āṅgirasa and asks help; Mudgala tells him to remember Vighnarāja. Outcome: The damaged Dakṣa seeks instruction; chapters2–4 retrospectively explain this outcome.. [Mudgala 1.1.51–59](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

Pārvatī says the compressed teaching is not understood and requests an easier detailed account of māyā-bound and transcendent Brahman; Śiva begins. Outcome: The question leads directly into creation teaching.. [Mudgala 1.5.35–47](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

Recognition lets them bow and hymn: formless trunk and formed body below neck, four arms explained through avyakta/sama/sat/asat principles, belly containing all, and feet through māyā/māyika. They ask him to remain in their heart, specify what duty to perform, and give intense devotion. Outcome: Prakṛti/Puruṣa ask direction and devotion.. He names Prakṛti as source of creation, grants unbound devotion and gifts to her desireful/desireless devotees. Puruṣa receives so’ham identity, unbound devotion, grant-giving and yogic Brahmanhood-bestowal; he must implant efficacy-seed in Prakṛti for world creation. Creation, maintenance and destruction proceed at due times; they are called universal mother/father. Gaṇeśa disappears and they obey. Outcome: Cosmic duties and devotional grants enable subsequent creation.. [Mudgala 1.6.43–62](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

Outcome: The tattvas receive the requested compound boon.. [Mudgala 1.8.1–12](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Veda teacher-boon and causal/subtle/gross eight-envelope creation

ID: `rmd2-story-eight-envelope-creation`. Scope: Mudgala 1.9.1–46. Newly completed this continuation: False.

1. **rmd2-event-pradhana-vedas** — Boon-enlightened Guṇeśa turns to creation. Vedas emerge from his breath; he creates the equal-element causal sleep-state world called Pradhāna, tamasic and ignorant of duty. It performs1,000divine-year tapas, receives Gaṇeśa’s appearance, worships/praises and gets yogic capacity. Vedas independently perform severe tapas and worship: Ṛg uses Brahmaṇaspati-sūkta, Yajus Gaṇeśa-śānti, Sāman eight-name praise, Atharvan Atharvaśīrṣa. Gaṇeśa grants universal teacher-standing, accepted/praised worldwide. Outcome: Pradhāna and Vedas receive differentiated powers.. 2. **rmd2-event-dream-waking-creation** — Asked what Pradhāna does, Śiva says it creates subtle sāttvic Hiraṇyagarbha/dream world, enclosed by subtle principles. Lacking knowledge, Hiraṇyagarbha performs difficult tapas and receives the desired, unspecified boon/capacity. It creates gross rājasic waking world; this also lacks knowledge, performs tapas, receives appearance, worships and gains a boon/capacity. Gross world becomes many three-state worlds; innumerable eggs emerge from Guṇeśa’s pores. The enclosing/enclosed unity is called Prakṛti/Brahma-bindu. Outcome: Causal→subtle→gross creation proceeds through distinct tapas/boons.. [Mudgala 1.9.15–28](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **rmd2-event-eight-envelope** — Gross waking creates space, then wind, fire, water and earth with sound/touch/form/taste/smell. Mutual entering gives earth5qualities, water4,fire3,wind2,space1. Prakṛti has eight envelope forms empowered by reflected Guṇeśa; Virāṭ arises in the egg. Earth’s displayed extent is50crore and successive envelopes are tenfold:water/fire/wind/space/waking-ego/dream-Mahat/sleep-māyā. Final order encloses Virāṭ by earth, earth by water, water by fire, fire by wind, wind by space, space by ego, ego by Mahat, Mahat by māyā. Countless such eggs surround Guṇeśa. Outcome: The eight-envelope egg and its cosmic inhabitant are established.. [Mudgala 1.9.29–46](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Gṛtsamada parentage, exclusion, curses, recognition and Varada site

ID: `gup2-story-grtsamada`. Scope: Upāsanā36–37; child-promise continuation38 separate. Newly completed this continuation: False.

36–37 complete source bodies: paternity revelation, social exclusion, mother transformation, personal recognition/knowledge/devotion grant and actual temple installation37.45–47 close this bounded recognition/site arc; future son continues separately.

Source narration order, including attributed mistaken father-identifications, does not turn reported belief into biological parentage.

1. **gup2-event-001** — Vyāsa asks about Mukundā after hearing Rukmāṅgada’s ascent. She remains consumed by desire, abandons food/water and sleeps exhausted in the forest. Indra knows her longing, assumes Rukmāṅgada’s form and has intercourse with her; she returns home believing the prince was her partner. Indra vanishes. She conceives and bears a beautiful son in the ninth month. Outcome: Impersonation yields pregnancy; her belief is explicitly mistaken.. [Upāsanā 36.1–11](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

2. **gup2-event-002** — Birth resounds through earth, sky and directions and startles birds. Vācaknavi leaves his daily rites and, unaware of Mukundā’s conduct, joyfully performs birth rites and donations. After ten days he names the child Gṛtsamada with astrologer Brahmin approval; in the fifth year initiates him and performs four Vedic vows. The boy learns from a single utterance. Vācaknavi, called father in this social/ritual setting, teaches the Vedic Gaṇānāṃ tvā mantra, prescribing steady Gaṇeśa contemplation for success and fame. Outcome: Naming, initiation, learning and mantra instruction completed.. [Upāsanā 36.12–21 (17 printed7)](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

3. **gup2-event-003** — Gṛtsamada practices mantra/meditation. King Magadha of Magadha, generous, martial and learned, with two unnamed learned ministers and devoted curse/grace-capable wife Ambikā, hosts his father’s śrāddha. Invited sages include Vasiṣṭha and Atri, and Gṛtsamada. His boast in śāstra discussion provokes Atri’s rebuke: ascetic merit earns respect, but alleged birth from prince Rukmāṅgada excludes equal sage-honour. Gṛtsamada flares with rage, others flee, and he threatens to burn the sages by curse if he proves not Rukmāṅgada’s son. Outcome: He leaves to interrogate his mother; alleged parentage is attributed speech.. [Upāsanā 36.22–32](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

4. **gup2-event-004** — Gṛtsamada demands his father’s identity and threatens to burn Mukundā if she refuses. Frightened, she admits longing for the hunting prince while husband Vācaknavi was practicing, and believes Rukmāṅgada his father. Ashamed and angry, the son curses her to be a thorny forest plant with countless fruits shunned by all living creatures. Outcome: Mother receives curse; her father-identification remains attributed mistaken belief.. [Upāsanā 36.33–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

5. **gup2-event-005** — Mukundā counters that her son disregarded motherhood: he will father a terrible mighty daitya frightening three worlds. She immediately abandons her body and becomes a forest badarī avoided even by birds. Heavenly speech reveals Gṛtsamada was born from Indra. He leaves for austerity. Hearing this account promises freedom from distress and fulfilment. Outcome: Plant transformation realized; true paternity revealed; future-son promise remains future.. [Upāsanā 36.41–45](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

6. **gup2-event-006** — Gṛtsamada reaches flower-rich Puṣpaka forest, bows to resident sages and stays with permission. Bathing, he fixes mind on Gaṇeśa, stands on a toe, restrains senses/breath/self, looks toward nose-tip and takes air. After1,000 divine years, opened eyes emit fire distressing gods who fear whose office he seeks. He then subsists on one fallen leaf, immobile, for a further15,000 as stated (the later clause gives number without repeating years). Outcome: Long austerity and emitted fire draw divine attention.. [Upāsanā 37.1–8](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

7. **gup2-event-007** — Compassionate Gaṇeśa appears quickly, radiant, moon-browed, lion-mounted, ten-armed with serpent sacred cord and lotus garland, accompanied by Siddhi/Buddhi. His radiance overwhelms Gṛtsamada, who faints, resumes inward contemplation and fears his practice wasted; he begs rescue and recalls exclusion from sage honours. Gaṇeśa identifies himself as arriving for grace, praises toe-supported austerity and asks desired boon. The sage bows joyfully. Outcome: Overwhelming vision becomes understood as grace, enabling request.. [Upāsanā 37.9–23](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

8. **gup2-event-008** — Weeping and dancing, Gṛtsamada calls birth/austerity fulfilled. By deity’s command he asks Brahman-knowledge and knowledge, firm devotion and unforgetting remembrance, supremacy among devotees, exclusive devotional standing able to draw three worlds, worldwide honour by gods/humans. He asks Puṣpaka forest/city fame as Gaṇeśapura, continued divine residence and fulfilment of devotees’ wishes. Outcome: Personal knowledge/devotion/honour and site requests articulated.. [Upāsanā 37.24–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

9. **gup2-event-009** — Gaṇeśa affirms all requested things and grants rare Brahmin standing. Because he recited Gaṇānāṃ tvā, Gṛtsamada becomes that Vedic mantra’s ṛṣi; he gains supreme honour before Brahmā and Vasiṣṭha’s sages. Remembering him first then Gaṇeśa at undertakings yields success; ritual without deity/ṛṣi/metre knowledge is fruitless. A mighty famous son will terrify gods, be undefeatable by all except Rudra and remain Gaṇeśa-devoted. Site names are Puṣpaka in divine/Kṛta era as printed devayuga, Maṇipūra in Tretā, Bhānaka in Dvāpara and Bhadraka in Kali; bathing/giving there grants wishes. Outcome: Personal and ritual standing granted; future son specified with Rudra exception.. [Upāsanā 37.35–43 (35 printed36)](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

10. **gup2-event-010** — Gaṇeśa vanishes after giving boons. Gṛtsamada installs his image, builds a fine temple, establishes enduring Varada name and worships devotedly. Site becomes a siddhi-place/Puṣpaka through divine favour. Listening to boon-account promises all wishes and firm Gaṇeśa devotion freeing transmigration. Outcome: Actual temple, named cult image and worship close recognition/site arc.. [Upāsanā 37.44–48](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Vakratunda field, installation and benefits

ID: `mud2-story-field`. Scope: 1.48.1–24. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud2-event-field-layout** — Śaunaka asks location and manifestation date. Sūta places Vakratunda at the eastern limit, installed by gods/sages/Gaṇas, manifest on Bhādra bright fourth; midday worship gives immense siddhis, beginning/end less. Images lie in each island/region’s east; square field ten yojanas, deity central with Siddhi left/Buddhi right, lion in front, eight siddhis in eight directions, eight Gaṇas and Śiva/Viṣṇu/Sun/Śakti in four directions. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.48.1–10](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **mud2-event-field-assembly** — Brahmā and deities occupy east/behind, Gaṅgā and rivers south, Kāśī and fields west, Vasiṣṭha and sages north. Śeṣa/Nāga kings, Gandharvas, mountains, Siddhas, Apsarases, Vidyādharas and Kinnaras serve joyfully and exchange Vakratunda stories; bathing in the ocean they create tīrthas bearing their names. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.48.11–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **mud2-event-field-benefits** — Gaṇeśa tīrtha bathing fulfils life; pilgrimage gives desired ends; dying in the field gives Brahman state. Distant remembrance gives enduring liberation by śukla-gati; devotees anywhere attain Brahman and purify people by sight. Sūta limits his account: even Śeṣa, Brahmā, Viṣṇu, Śiva, Vedas, Skanda and yogins cannot exhaust it, and he repeats Mudgala’s account to Dakṣa. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.48.18–24](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)
