ID: `mud2-story-visvamitra`. Scope: 1.48.25–1.54.32. Newly completed this continuation: False.

All1.48.25–1.54.32 directly read and extracted in source order, including family background, failed weapons/rank quest, reform and final boons. Embedded Kāśī50.8–52.16 and cosmic53 account separately complete; exact frame transitions retained.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud2-event-teaching-frame** — Śaunaka asks what followed Mudgala’s discourse to Dakṣa. Dakṣa asks the detailed Dhundhirāja incarnation and how Vakratunda is born from Śaṅkara’s body. Mudgala introduces an old Gaṇeśa account Yājñavalkya taught Kauśika, illuminating Brahminhood. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.48.25–30](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **mud2-event-rcika-marriage** — Bhārgava sage Ṛcīka grows old through long austerity, sees deer with mates/descendants, desires household life and asks king Gādhi’s daughter. Gādhi demands a thousand dark-eared horses as his established marriage-price. Ṛcīka gets them from Varuṇa and gives them; frightened Gādhi duly marries his daughter to him. Her mother laments, provides goods/clothes and sends her to the forest, where she diligently serves Ṛcīka. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.48.31–43](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **mud2-event-rcika-city** — Pleased by his wife’s good conduct, Ṛcīka uses his own austerity to create a richly adorned forest city with four social classes, servants and wealth, and adopts a youthful form. He enjoys marriage properly, then judges its pleasures small and becomes unattached. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.48.44–47](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **mud2-event-caru-exchange** — The queen visits with wealth/clothes and finds a magnificent city rather than the hermitage; a sweeper identifies Ṛcīka’s city and summons her daughter. Seeing the prosperous sage, she tells her daughter to ask him for sons for both women. Ṛcīka prepares two carus/puroḍāśas with Brahmin/Kṣatriya potency and specifies each eater, then leaves for midday rites. The mother orders exchanging their portions; daughter complies, mother returns home, both become pregnant. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.49.1–15](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **mud2-event-family-births** — Ṛcīka sees fierce radiance in his wife and asks about Kṣatriya potency. She admits the exchange; he predicts her brother will be an ascetic, her son a fierce bowman. Afraid, she asks a Brahmin son and threatens her life otherwise. He shifts that quality to her grandson, informs Viṣṇu of the pregnancy and effects it through tapas. Jamadagni is born to him; Rāma, best bearer of weapons, becomes Jamadagni’s son. Gādhi’s queen bears Viśvāmitra in ninth month; Gādhi installs his grown son in the kingdom and leaves for austerity. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.49.16–23](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

6. **mud2-event-nandini-conflict** — King Viśvāmitra hunts, grows hungry/thirsty and reaches Vasiṣṭha’s hermitage. Vasiṣṭha feeds king and army by the cow’s power. Viśvāmitra requests Nandinī; refused, he orders her seizure. She destroys the messengers; when men whip her, she creates a great army which destroys the king’s whole army. He returns defeated. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.49.24–30](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

7. **mud2-event-weapons-defeated** — Viśvāmitra leaves rule to ministers and propitiates Śaṅkara in forest. At100 years Śiva grants complete missile knowledge. He returns, attacks Vasiṣṭha and hermitage with fire missile and other weapons. Vasiṣṭha’s Brahmadaṇḍa destroys every missile and defeats him; he flees. Brahmins comfort Vasiṣṭha. Viśvāmitra recognizes Brahmin power’s superiority and his destroyed weapons. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.49.31–37](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

8. **mud2-event-brahminhood-refused** — Viśvāmitra abandons kingdom, fasts and repeats Gāyatrī by puraścaraṇa for Brahminhood, scorching the world. At10,000 years Brahmā offers a boon; his Brahminhood request is answered with further austerity. Brahmā comes ten times offering another boon, which he refuses. His tapas subjects gods and awes Brahmins into assent to his claimed Brahmarṣi rank; Vasiṣṭha still calls him Rājarṣi. Furious, he repeatedly attempts Vasiṣṭha’s death without success and remains distressed. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.49.38–47](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

9. **mud2-event-yaj-arrives** — Yājñavalkya, explicitly arisen from Brahmā’s limb, visits. Viśvāmitra bows, worships and says birth, study and austerity bear fruit through seeing him; he asks the visit’s purpose and offers obedience as his servant. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.49.48–51](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

10. **mud2-event-yaj-counsel-frame** — Yājñavalkya came only because he knew Viśvāmitra’s grief, promises to remove it by reasoned knowledge, and hears the denied boon and Vasiṣṭha’s Rājarṣi address. He promises Brahmarṣi rank after relinquishing pride/envy and offers the Kāśī–Viśveśvara story: their inseparability even at dissolution produces pride and a separating obstacle. Viśvāmitra asks its nature. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.50.1–12](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

11. **mud2-event-visva-humility-method** — Yājñavalkya applies the example: worship Vighnarāja and abandon pride to become Brahmin. Viśvāmitra asks where pride resides, how to abandon it and how to worship/understand Gaṇeśa. The teacher identifies certainty that one’s own tapas must confer Brahminhood as pride. Practise sincerely but accept the deity’s grant or continued Rājarṣi status, knowing embodied dependence. Abandon competitiveness, think oneself below all Brahmins/ascetics and inwardly honor them. He reproaches Viśvāmitra’s envy: destruction of Vasiṣṭha’s100 sons as by a Rākṣasa, alternate creation for Triśaṅku and attempts on Vasiṣṭha’s life. Without reform even a thousand yugas of tapas ends in death. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.52.17–35](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

12. **mud2-event-narayana-frame** — Yājñavalkya recounts former Vakratunda deeds. Viśvāmitra, astonished, requests experiential teaching. Yājñavalkya promises his own past knowledge-giving story: visiting Vaikuṇṭha, he honored Nārāyaṇa and asked the essence and support of all beings, despite his intuition that all rests on Nārāyaṇa. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.52.36–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

13. **mud2-event-visva-reformed-tapas** — Yājñavalkya says he bowed to Janārdana, returned to forest, progressively mastered fivefold mental activity and attained Gaṇeśa by peace-yoga and grace. He urges Viśvāmitra’s refuge, offering Gāyatrī japa merit to Gaṇeśa and friendship to all so his name becomes meaningful. He departs honored, repeating Gaṇeśa’s name-mantra. Viśvāmitra thereafter abandons pride/envy, repeats Gāyatrī as offering to Gaṇeśa and meditates on him in tapas. Gāyatrī is called Veda-mother, Praṇava father, and Gaṇanātha’s Oṃ-shaped head/saguṇa identity explains the offering. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.54.1–10](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

14. **mud2-event-visva-rank-granted** — Soon Brahmā comes and grants Viśvāmitra Brahminhood. Vasiṣṭha with Brahmins honors and declares him Brahmin; sages rank him among seven Ṛṣis and chief Gaṇeśa devotees. He worships regularly and hymns Gaṇeśa. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.54.11–14](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

15. **mud2-event-visva-devotion-boon** — Viśvāmitra hymns Gaṇeśa as guṇa lord/transcendent, knowledge-lord, elephant form, Brahman lord, first-worshipped, siddhi giver, māyā wielder/deluder, Buddhi lord, lion-mounted and also mouse-vehicle/bannered Heramba. Gaṇeśa appears lion-mounted with immeasurable solar brilliance; devotee falls tearful. He promises hymn hearers/reciters honor, desired ends, enjoyment/liberation, and invites boons. Viśvāmitra calls lineage, birth, tapas, fame and parents blessed by sight, declines māyā-objects and requests devotion and living among Gāṇapatyas to destroy māyā-delusion. Gaṇeśa says so be it, vanishes; Viśvāmitra becomes Gāṇapatya. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.54.15–32](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Kāśī, Divodāsa and Śiva’s restored residence

ID: `mud2-story-kashi`. Scope: 1.50.8–1.52.16 (embedded example; frame50.1–12 separately recorded). Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud2-event-divodasa-rule** — After60 rainless years and terrestrial devastation, Brahmā approaches Sūrya-line king Divodāsa, who left rule and performs devotional tapas for liberation. Brahmā asks him to take Kāśī to save life, promising rainfall if he rules. He first refuses, asking liberation; then compassionately accepts on condition earthly rule excludes gods, while gods/heaven, humans/earth and Nāgas/Rākṣasas/Pātāla keep their domains. He will worship Śambhu as a deity; Brahmā agrees and grants Kāśī. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.50.13–24](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **mud2-event-siva-leaves-kashi** — Brahmā takes Śiva with gods/sages to Mandara to grant Marīci a boon, warning the ascetic will die otherwise. Marīci asks liberation; suffering from tapas he relinquishes life, obtains Śiva’s likeness and reaches Kailāsa. Brahmā then asks Śiva to remain at Mandara, explaining Divodāsa’s condition for drought relief and asking pardon. Śiva agrees with a sigh and stays with Pārvatī/Gaṇas. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.50.25–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **mud2-event-kashi-siva-tapas** — Śiva laments that Avimukta was taken from him and his Viśvanātha title is powerless. Recognizing obstacle and removal of pride, he resolves refuge in Vighnarāja and meditates on Gaṇeśa at the mountain for Kāśī. Personified Kāśī hears his departure, loses pride and meditates on Vighneśa for Śiva. Divodāsa’s righteous reign restores rain, joy and sinless observance of social duties. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.50.35–45](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **mud2-event-divodasa-pilgrimage** — Healthy people, fruitful trees, abundant cow milk, sacrifices and svāhā/svadhā please gods/ancestors. Divodāsa’s daily circuit worships Dhundhi first, then Viśveśvara, Jagadambā, Kāśī, Maṇikarṇī, Bhāgīrathī, Mādhava, Jaigīṣavya cave, Kālabhairava and Daṇḍapāṇi. He makes people perform this and punishes the unrighteous;80,000 years pass without even slight sin entering. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.50.46–52](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **mud2-event-divodasa-replaces-gods** — Gods resent loss of earthly sovereignty and scheme against the king. Indra orders Agni to withdraw outward fire; people seek Divodāsa, who assumes fire-form by tapas everywhere on earth. As other gods withdraw he supplies their functions through forms including Vāyu, unknown to the people. Gods ask Brahmā what to do; he sends them to request restored places. They praise Divodāsa, he praises them and restores their offices, remaining in one human body. Agni and others resume their places. The transcription misnumbers sequential63 as33. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.50.53–67](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

6. **mud2-event-failed-divine-plots** — Śiva grieves that the righteous tapas-powered king cannot be displaced. Gods/sages offer to lure citizens by siddhis so their sin falls on king, then Śiva can take Kāśī. Their many forms and siddhis fail for a year because citizens remain righteous. Śiva’s further dispatched Bhairavas, Ādityas, Vasus, sages, Yoginīs and Gaṇas fail. Brahmā is sent and performs ten Aśvamedhas, then becomes quiet. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.51.1–12](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

7. **mud2-event-siva-boon** — Śiva meditates on Vighnarāja for one year, asking freedom from obstacles and separation. Seeing complete humility, Vakratunda manifests lion-mounted, four-armed, noose/goad/boon/fearlessness, red elephant face, great belly, Siddhi/Buddhi, Śeṣa at navel and Cintāmaṇi. Śiva worships/hymns creator/preserver/destroyer and causal/transcendent forms, seeks inseparable Kāśī, and recalls an earlier promise to appear in son-relation on remembrance. Vakratunda promises hymn recitation/hearing gives siddhis, enjoyment/liberation and all other requests, restoring Avimukta meaning. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.51.13–32](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

8. **mud2-event-dhundhi-and-buddha** — Vakratunda creates a beautiful all-knowing radiant Brahmin from his body and directs him to delude Kāśī citizens and Divodāsa and restore rule to Śiva. People will call him Dhundhi the astrologer and honor him, his descendants likewise. The Brahmin bows, circles and goes remembering Gaṇeśa. Vakratunda commands Viṣṇu to enter Kāśī in Buddhist form and corrupt the already-deluded city. Viṣṇu obeys; both accomplish the ordered delusion and corruption. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.51.33–41](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

9. **mud2-event-kashi-boon** — Kāśī meditates for a thousand years; lion-mounted adorned Gaṇeśa arrives. She rises, bows, worships and hymns infinite/formless/formful, Siddhi/Buddhi lord, protector and obstacle-giver to nondevotees. Afflicted separation makes her Avimukta name empty; she asks Śiva’s sight. He promises return, meaningful Avimukta name, hymn that removes separation, desired ends and Brahman state to faithful reciters/hearers. He disappears; she keeps meditating, awaiting the time. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.51.42–61](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

10. **mud2-event-kashi-restored** — Dhundhi astrologer instantly deludes the city; Viṣṇu as Buddha then corrupts it. Divodāsa is instructed by Dhundhi and gives up rule through the Buddhist figure, awaiting Śiva. Both summon Śiva, who leads worshipped Gaṇeśa into Kāśī as first resident, then enters with gods/sages. Kāśī bows to both; gods/sages take places and Śiva grants Divodāsa liberation. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.52.1–6](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

11. **mud2-event-dhundhiraja-installation** — Gaṇeśa tells Śiva he restored Kāśī and will return to his own abode, visible again on Śiva’s remembrance; he disappears with Dhundhi Brahmin. Śiva creates and installs his image with gods/Brahmins, worships and announces: worship Dhundhi before pleasing Śiva to receive enjoyment/liberation, otherwise not; Vakratunda gives Kāśī-residence fruit. Naming the discovered Vedic root Dhundhirāja, Śiva credits him with four human aims and devotes daily service. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.52.7–16](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Nārāyaṇa’s cosmic principles withdrawal-test and Vināyaka teaching

ID: `mud2-story-cosmic-dispute`. Scope: 1.53.1–49, embedded in Yājñavalkya’s account. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud2-event-ganesa-support** — Nārāyaṇa says the world rests on Gaṇeśa: he gives inner buddhi and outer siddhi, is worshipped first, abides at beginning/middle/end, is Brahman-form lord of Siddhi/Buddhi while the others are guṇa-form. His Svānanda realm transcends oppositions and is inaccessible to sages/gods. Knowing him releases bonds. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.53.1–5](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **mud2-event-tattva-dispute** — Envious principles dispute who is highest and claim to create/preserve/destroy the supported world freely. Guṇādhīśa comes in Brahmin guise to end dispute and give knowledge. They wonder how he entered their Brahman paths; he says yoga made the path and asks the quarrel. Asked which principle is greatest, he proposes each leave the world in turn; the one whose departure breaks it is lord. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.53.6–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **mud2-event-gross-subtle-withdrawal** — Earth withdraws, then water, tejas, wind, space and tanmātras, without destroying world. Five elements/tanmātras stand powerless and grieve. Five knowledge-senses, action-senses, Indra-form Brahman and illuminating deity-hosts next leave; the world remains as before and the withdrawn principles/deities lose radiance. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.53.18–26](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **mud2-event-higher-withdrawal** — Anna-Brahman, prāṇa-form, mind-form, vijñāna, ānanda, nāda, bindu, so’ham, bodha, videha, asat, sat, sama-svavedya and neti-svavedya successively withdraw and world remains. Finally Svānanda-form withdraws and world dissolves. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.53.27–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **mud2-event-cosmic-restoration** — Astonished principles praise Svānanda and ask him, their common support, to become Vināyaka, leader of all principles, and recreate the world as before. Vināyaka recreates it; Brahman-form principles reenter their own places and rejoice. Nārāyaṇa explains their Jagadīśvara titles in this framework. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.53.35–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

6. **mud2-event-vinayaka-yoga-teaching** — Nārāyaṇa distinguishes Vināyaka as Nijānanda in saṃyoga and Nirānanda in ayoga, and yoga-form when that duality is dissolved, lord of Brahmans. Knowing him gives peace, so he urges worship. Vakratunda destroys the crooked deluding māyā by his trunk through devotion. Ga denotes mind/speech world, ṇa what exceeds it, and Gaṇeśa their lord/yoga-form; attaining him gives Brahman state. The Vedānta secret should be guarded for siddhi. Nārāyaṇa ceases, repeatedly saluting Gaṇeśa. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.53.41–49](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Brahmā’s flood, child Gaṇeśa and creation commission

ID: `mud2-story-kh2-creation`. Scope: 2.1.21–55. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

2. **mud2-event-brahma-flood** — With Gaṇeśa’s boon Brahmā resolves creation, enters Janārdana through yogamāyā, hides in him and after a year emerges from his navel-lotus, becoming Viṣṇu’s son under the boon recalled here; the exact grant agency is established separately at1.13.33–37. Meditating on Heramba, his own body releases water filling ten directions; submerged and bewildered, he remembers Vakratunda for rescue. On remembrance he sees a banyan and thumb-sized sleeping Gaṇeśa on a leaf, three-eyed, four-armed, elephant-trunked, large-bellied, Śeṣa at navel, moon/crown/adornments/clothes. The child sprays Brahmā with trunk-water; delighted Brahmā begins a hymn. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.1.21–31](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud2-event-brahma-cosmic-belly** — Brahmā hymns leaf-sleeping Gaṇanātha/Heramba as four-armed Śeṣa-navel, elephant-faced Lambodara/Ekadanta, thumb-sized, with Siddhi/Buddhi, creation/preservation/end and māyā-delusion power, and asks rescue from creation-obstructing flood. He sees full animate/inanimate world with lords in the mouth, is carried by varied winds into the great belly, sees many cosmic eggs, and exits by a hair-pore to see the child again. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.1.32–43](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud2-event-brahma-creation-instruction** — Gaṇeśa promises Brahmā’s hymn grants all aims, enjoyment/liberation and whichever thought desired to honored reciters/hearers. He diagnoses forgotten initial remembrance/worship after navel emergence as cause of obstacle, removed now by remembrance/sight. Begin every future act with remembrance/bowing and reproduce the world seen in his belly. Child disappears; Brahmā returns to lotus, performs mantra/worship and meditation; Gaṇeśa appears in heart, receives mental worship and Siddhi/Buddhi as dakṣiṇā when they arrive there, then disappears. Brahmā bows and resolves creation. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.1.44–55](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Avidyā, Kumāras, Rudra and scripture/sacrifice creation

ID: `mud2-story-creation-successions`. Scope: 2.2.1–40. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud2-event-avidya-creation** — Remembering Gaṇeśa, Brahmā emits Avidyā from body, divided fivefold, red/black/white and deluding the threefold world. Tamas obscures through ignorance/body-interest; Moha produces desires/varied enjoyment-actions; Mahāmoha wrong action/sin for bodily pleasure; Tāmisra produces sickness/poverty and mental/physical affliction; Andhatāmisra produces karmic hell-torments. Avidyā remembers Gaṇeśa and pursues her purpose; Brahmā condemns her and resolves further creation. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.2.1–11](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud2-event-kumara-creation** — Four sons arise from Brahmā’s body: Sanaka, Sanandana, Sanātana and Sanatkumāra. Vidyā/jñāna is their mother-form, formless bodha/self nature. Their paths: Sanaka sahaja, Sanandana sama-yoga, Sanātana jñāna-yoga, Sanatkumāra karma-yoga. At Brahmā’s order to create remembering Gaṇeśa they create many yoga paths; displeased Brahmā tells them to stop yoga-creation and they comply. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.2.12–19](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud2-event-rudra-creation** — Angry at knowledge-creation, Brahmā emits Śambhu as forehead-born son: five faces, three eyes, ten arms, half-female/male, crescent, trident, matted hair and tiger/elephant skins. Crying child asks father for place, names and task. Brahmā names Rudra for crying, Śiva as all-happiness ground, recalls boon giving son-form, assigns eight abodes earth/water/fire/wind/space/moon/sun/consecrated sacrificer and eleven names, and orders creation after Gaṇeśa meditation. Śiva creates countless immortal Rudras. Asked to create birth/death beings, he refuses and stays quiet, called Sthāṇu, celibate/upward-seeded and worshipping Gaṇeśa. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.2.20–33](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud2-event-scripture-sacrifice-creation** — Brahmā emits Vedas with śāstras and Purāṇas from mouths and tells them to create. They propitiate Gaṇeśa by tapas, receive boons and create beings and varied rites. Brahmā rejoices, begins sacrifice and thinks of Viṣṇu to obtain him as son. Four-armed Yajña-form Janārdana emerges from sacrifice, asks father’s order and, remembering Gaṇeśa as told, creates many karma-born sacrifices granting manifold fruits like wish-trees. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.2.34–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Nārada’s mutual curses, protected rebirths and final devotion

ID: `mud2-story-narada`. Scope: 2.2.41–2.3.57. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Whole2.2.41–2.3.57 read and extracted through explicitly closing verse3.57; forecast rebirths separated from summary fulfilment, father-truth constraint and final devotion/yoga grants retained.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud2-event-narada-mutual-curses** — Ten body-born sons appear: Marīci, Bhṛgu, Atri, Pulastya, Pulaha, Kratu, Dakṣa, Aṅgiras, Vasiṣṭha, Nārada. Ordered to create, they agree and go to forest tapas. Nārada refuses creation, choosing Gaṇanāyaka worship because world is māyā/mithyā; Brahmā insists create first, worship in old age. Nārada questions labour for delusive aims. Brahmā angrily curses Śūdra birth and woman-enjoying Gandharva life; Nārada curses Brahmā to be unworshipped for needless cursing. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.2.41–49](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud2-event-narada-curse-mitigation** — Nārada propitiates Gaṇeśa by one-syllable mantra for100 divine years and requests curse removal after hymn. Gaṇeśa says he cannot falsify father’s curse because his prior boon makes Brahmā truthful. Instead he will teach Nārada as Gandharva so māyā cannot hurt him amid women; then Śūdra birth with renouncer company/knowledge, forest life and Vaiṣṇava path; after that body falls, he again becomes Brahmā’s son Nārada, worships Śambhu and learns Gaṇeśa from Nārāyaṇa to become a loved Gāṇapatya/yoga lord. Gaṇeśa disappears; narrator says Nārada undergoes curse, returns to heaven and is reborn as Śiva-devotee, then Nārāyaṇa teaches him Gāṇapatya devotion. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.2.50–61](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud2-event-narada-questions** — Dakṣa asks how Nārāyaṇa taught Nārada. Nārada sees Śaṅkara devotedly worshipping a jeweled Cintāmaṇi/Gaṇeśa image on Kailāsa, tearful and immersed in meditation, then approaches Viṣṇu at Vaikuṇṭha. He asks why Śiva, whom he regards as immutable unmanifest Brahman, worships another, and why Viṣṇu also chants Gaṇeśa’s name. Nārāyaṇa answers Gaṇeśa is yoga-peace, Siddhi/Buddhi lord in Svānanda, sole Vināyaka; the other deities gain siddhi-giving power by devotion and worship for yoga-peace. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.3.1–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud2-event-siva-yoga-teaching-reported** — Nārāyaṇa recounts asking Śiva the same question after his daily worship. Śiva explains Buddhi as fivefold mental activity/Prakṛti enjoying fivefold, Siddhi as manifold delusion for whose attainment mind strives, including four aims and Brahman state. Brahman-attainer has no fivefold mind, is yogin non-different from Gaṇeśa. Ga denotes saṃprajñāta, ṇa asaṃprajñāta; Gaṇeśa is their yoga-peace lord. Śiva names himself unmanifest/neti, Viṣṇu sama, and distinguishes bhava-pratyaya’s fivefold/māyā Gaṇeśa and upāya-pratyaya’s māyā-free, while direct Gaṇeśa transcends either and is attained by peace. He urges fivefold-mind abandonment to become Cintāmaṇi; Nārāyaṇa concludes Gaṇeśa grants their offices and Brahman state and is their family deity. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.3.18–36](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

5. **mud2-event-narada-tenyear-hymn** — Nārada bows to Viṣṇu, practices forest yoga, mental Gaṇeśa meditation and mantra; after ten years Gaṇeśa appears. He bows and worships, hymns Veda-transcendent Brahman, beyond/within neither mind/speech category, Siddhi/Buddhi lord, remover of devotee obstacles and giver to nondevotees, delusion/yoga/Vināyaka/Cintāmaṇi/Ekadanta, moon-bearing, mouse-vehicle/bannered, creator/preserver/destroyer. He declares seeing Gaṇeśa removed differentiated death-delusion, attained peace-yoga/no difference, and requests supreme devotion and loving Gāṇapatya status. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.3.37–52](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

6. **mud2-event-narada-final-boon** — Gaṇeśa grants permanent unwavering devotion, no departure from yoga, honor by yogins/all; Nārada’s hymn gives peace-yoga and bhukti/mukti to readers/hearers. He merges into Nārada’s heart, where the sage always sees him. Mudgala explicitly closes Nārada account and promises good destiny to its hearers/readers. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.3.53–57](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Five-deity rivalry and restored Gaṇeśa knowledge

ID: `mud3-story-five-dispute`. Scope: 1.12–17. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Whole1.12–17 narrative read and significant actors/grants/dialogue/powers/transitions extracted through Pārvatī’s attained peace and Mudgala’s closing counsel.17.14 finer etymology remains withheld and prevents whole17 COMPLETE; no major-transition or narrative-action gap.

Bounded narrative spine completeness is distinct from whole chapter semantic extraction;17 remains PARTIAL. Source-local Gaṇeśa Gītāsāra is distinct from similarly titled works elsewhere.

1. **mud3-event-five-deity-frame** — Śaunaka asks Sūta what five empowered deities did. Sūta follows Mudgala’s answer to Dakṣa, recounting Pārvatī’s question to Śiva: she asks Gaṇeśa/devotee accounts after the five-deity boons, preferring them to wasted speech. Śiva joyfully embraces and answers her. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.12.1–11](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **mud3-event-ganesa-priority** — Śiva praises Pārvatī’s Gaṇeśa devotion, calls devotees Brahman-formed and liberating by sight, and says seekers/deity devotees omitting Gaṇeśa fail and fall to hell; the other gods likewise consign Gaṇeśa-disregarding or reviling devotees to hell. He calls Gaṇeśa everyone’s mother/father and first-worshipped Jyeṣṭharāja, requests constant Gaṇapatya company/dwelling in every direction, and praises Gaṇeśa/devotee accounts for worldly/otherworldly happiness, devotion and Brahman attainment by speaker/questioner/listener. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.12.12–28](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **mud3-event-brahma-visnu-pride** — Gaṇeśa’s boons create five independent unbroken worlds: Śiva/Kailāsa, Viṣṇu/Vaikuṇṭha, Sun/Sauraloka, Śakti/Śaktiloka, Brahmā/Satyaloka. Initially mutual benefactors, they grow envious and claim unique supremacy. Viṣṇu creates body-water, sleeps; algae/creeper then earth develop. For play he creates a fragrant hundred-yojana navel-lotus. Visiting Brahmā wakes/honors him but accuses deluding creation and claims all depends on his own prior creation. Viṣṇu denies deceit, claims supreme preservation enables Brahmā and has no equal. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.12.29–48](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **mud3-event-mutual-belly-test** — Brahmā invites Viṣṇu to inspect his belly. Viṣṇu enters by yogamāyā, sees cosmic creation including navel-lotus Viṣṇu, finds no end, exits and acknowledges Brahmā as guru/lord, then invites him inside. Brahmā enters by yoga, sees cosmic worlds and another Brahmā, finds no end. Viṣṇu closes exits by yogamāyā; Brahmā pierces a lotus-stalk doorway by yoga and exits angrily. Viṣṇu explains play/testing, asks pardon; Brahmā reciprocally apologizes for ignorant anger and acknowledges Viṣṇu’s greatness. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.13.1–22](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **mud3-event-siva-linga-son-boon** — Brahmā/Viṣṇu regard themselves supreme and remember Śiva, Śakti and Sun to test them. Śiva comes first and, challenged, claims universal destruction and becomes a boundless luminous liṅga extending all directions. Brahmā proposes upward/downward searches. Before descent Viṣṇu asks a boon; Brahmā offers and Viṣṇu asks him to become his son. Brahmā acknowledges being caught by the offered promise and grants future yoga-created body from Viṣṇu’s navel lotus at creation. Both search Śiva’s limits in opposite directions, find none, return weary and hymn him. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.13.23–41](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

6. **mud3-event-siva-linga-hymn** — The pair hymns Śaṅkara/Rudra/time/three-eyed, Umā lord, bull-mounted first deity, asking revealed nature for fearless worship. Śiva reveals Viṣṇu left, Brahmā right and full world in liṅga. They bow and ask withdrawal; he resumes former bull-bannered form, offers desired boons pleased by hymn, and promises reciters all wishes, his favor and his world at end. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.13.42–51](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

7. **mud3-event-siva-son-promise** — Viṣṇu directs Brahmā to request Śiva as son so he becomes Viṣṇu’s grandson. Brahmā asks; Viṣṇu repeats; Śiva grants partial manifestation as Rudra from Brahmā’s forehead. Invited to inspect their powers with knowledge-sight, Śiva finds no end of either, calls the three supreme lords. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.14.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

8. **mud3-event-sun-shakti-tests** — Sun comes at remembrance, shows boundless radiant animate/inanimate form; three lose radiance and physical sight, use knowledge-sight yet find no limit. He becomes gentle, views their powers likewise without end and calls them equal. Śakti arrives, shows gross/subtle complete-world form; four take refuge finding no end. Gentle Śakti views them and declares five equal. Under Gaṇeśa’s māyā they forget prior knowledge, ask how scripture’s one lord fits five, search above/below/ten directions without result, then meditate inwardly, attain unity and peace. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.14.10–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

9. **mud3-event-inner-manifestation** — Gaṇarāja shows his form in their hearts: four arms, three eyes, trunk, one tusk, fan-ears, crooked trunk/great belly, red vermilion body, Śeṣa navel, crown/ornaments, Siddhi/Buddhi, Cintāmaṇi jewel at heart. First manifested in Śaṅkara’s heart, he is asked who he is and whether the supreme Brahman lord attained through peace-yoga. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.14.35–41](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

10. **mud3-event-memory-restored-hymn** — Vakratunda says he is earlier boon-giving Gaṇeśa, forgotten by his own māyā; removes Śiva’s delusion-net and restores memory, then vanishes before hymn. Śiva’s renewed meditation fails to see him; all five compare similar experiences, abandon pride and jointly hymn Vakratunda’s form, māyā and transcendence, asking appearance. He manifests before them, raises prostrate gods, promises hymn readers honor, desired ends and final sāyujya. He warns no pride: they are dependent fractional manifestations created for play, and offers secret knowledge to remove delusion and make them like him. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.15.1–22](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

11. **mud3-event-gitasara-brahman-mappings** — Five deities request knowledge. Gaṇeśa describes fourfold deha as tvam-pada and fourfold so’ham/dehī as tat-pada; union as asi-pada with self-arising distinction, Sāṃkhya as self-bliss/other-arising, Svānanda beyond both as nondifference/union. He maps five forms: Śakti as asat; Sun as immortal/self support; Viṣṇu as Śakti/Sun delight; Śiva as fourth neti beyond three; their nondifference Svānanda. Brahmā is māyā reflection of five, creating all as Prapitāmaha. Terms retain source’s doctrinal vocabulary, not imported school equivalences. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.16.1–13](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

12. **mud3-event-gitasara-peace** — Gaṇeśa contrasts ayoga beyond Svānanda with no entry/vision and self-experienced vision in Svānanda; yoga beyond saṃyoga/ayoga gives complete peace through mental-activity restraint. Five mental grounds are kṣipta,mūḍha,vikṣipta,ekāgra,nirodhaka, with Cintāmaṇi there; restraining them attains him as peace in heart. Buddhi is mind, Siddhi delusion, their lord plays through Brahman distinctions. Give up worry/pride in Gaṇeśa-identity samādhi to become his form without delusion. He ceases, gods abandon difference and instantly gain peace. Śiva calls the teaching21 verses; faithful reading/hearing gives Brahman likeness, worldly enjoyment then yoga, and others’ sins vanish by sight of such a person. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.16.14–26](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

13. **mud3-event-vak-bindu-explanation** — Gods ask how guṇa-forms are Brahman. Gaṇeśa says they are Brahman in own states, guṇa in distinction, created for play. Reflection gives fivefoldness; māyā differentiates Brahmā/Viṣṇu/Śiva as rajas/sattva/tamas, Sun as their ahaṃkāra and Śakti as delusion to accomplish three guṇas. He is here an aṃśa Vakratunda, Bindu-Brahman expressing distinction, with fourth Guṇapa/Buddhi right and five-deity play/Siddhi left. Devotees’ crooked obstacles are destroyed with trunk, explaining Vakratunda name. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.17.1–13](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

14. **mud3-event-remember-condition** — Gaṇeśa explains Bindu-form supports gods’ acts/play; forgetting him brings māyā-delusion, so constant inward remembrance and first worship in divine/ancestral rites bring siddhi. He vanishes. Śiva tells Pārvatī this is his own heart meditation, that Brahmā creates, Viṣṇu preserves through manifestations at Gaṇeśa’s order, he destroys, she gives delight and Sun drives actions by time. Her Himācala-daughter birth caused forgotten knowledge; she must recover it and see heart Gaṇeśa as Mahāmāyā. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.17.15–23](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

15. **mud3-event-eight-forms-and-image** — Śiva lists eight Gaṇarāja avatāras and Brahman correspondences: Vakratunda tvam; Ekadanta tat; Mahodara asi; Gajānana Sāṃkhya; Lambodara Brahman in Śakti; Vikaṭa sat/Sun; Vighnarāja sama/Viṣṇu; Dhūmravarṇa unmanifest neti/Śambhu. They are kalā forms governing eight Brahmans, supported by self-experienced Brahman; ayoga has no play, Svānanda does; full yoga transcends both. Śiva calls Gaṇeśa family deity, daily worships Cintāmaṇi/Vināyaka as light of five mental activities and shows jeweled elephant-faced household image and secluded meditation place to astonished Pārvatī. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.17.24–38](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

16. **mud3-event-shakti-marriage-emanations** — Pārvatī asks how universal deluding Śakti became wife of partial/tamasic Śiva. He recounts seeing her power in five-deity rivalry; after Gaṇeśa instruction he propitiated her100 years. She appeared, invited boon, accepted hymn and request to be his wife, granted and vanished. Her left limb emitted Lakṣmī beloved of Nārāyaṇa, right Sāvitrī beloved of Brahmā, heart Saṃjñā wife of Sun. Śiva urges recovered Gaṇeśa yoga; Pārvatī privately practices, gains peace quickly through former impressions and returns to him. Mudgala tells Dakṣa wholehearted Gaṇeśa worship will remove obstacles/grief and make him honored yoga lord. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.17.39–56](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Mudgala’s food test, heavenly offer and reasoned refusal

ID: `mud3-story-heaven-refusal`. Scope: 1.18.1–64;1.19.1–20. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud3-event-mudgala-autobiography-frame** — Śaunaka asks further Gaṇeśa accounts. Sūta resumes Dakṣa asking Mudgala why Brahman-formed Gaṇeśa incarnates and for the eight forms’ stories. Dakṣa praises sages who purify by sight, touch and speech. Mudgala praises patient, equal-minded and helpful questioners, contrasts sinners/nāstikas/dāmbhikas lacking desire for sacred narrative, and first offers his own earlier experience before the avatāra accounts. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.18.1–21](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

2. **mud3-event-durvasas-test** — Mudgala recounts śiloñcha gleaning and Dvādaśī fast-breaking after a fortnight, gathering a droṇa of food and performing five yajñas. Gods fear his tapas seeks their Indra/Brahmā/Viṣṇu/Śiva offices and send angry Durvāsas to test him. At fast-breaking the sage arrives naked, like a madman, asks alms and is not recognized. Mudgala gives all food; Durvāsas eats the droṇa, smears leftovers on his body and leaves. Mudgala rejoices in feeding the guest and continues fasting. The same guest consumes all food at six fast-breakings; after three months of fasting Mudgala remains unmoved, attributed to prior merit. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.18.22–34](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

3. **mud3-event-heaven-invitation** — Pleased with pure-hearted tapas, Durvāsas reveals his yoga form and declares Mudgala pure, entitled to enjoy the conquered, imperishable heavenly worlds; he returns to his own hermitage. At once a heavenly vimāna arrives. Its ornamented messenger descends and receives Mudgala’s hospitality, says Indra with Bṛhaspati has sent him and awaits Mudgala, and invites him aboard to heavenly enjoyments. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.18.35–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

4. **mud3-event-heaven-three-defects** — Before accepting, Mudgala asks heavenly virtues and defects. The messenger distinguishes earthly karma-ground from heavenly enjoyment-ground: desired pleasures, youth, no fatigue/sweat/heat/cold, apsaras and desired-form movement, with enjoyment proportionate to prior karma. He admits three defects: no fresh karmic fruit there and worry about expiry; familiar gods/friends expel one without affection when merit is consumed, creating fear of falling; and envy/heart-torment on seeing those with greater merit enjoying more. Despite those defects he again invites Mudgala. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.18.43–64](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

5. **mud3-event-heaven-refusal** — Mudgala asks for a defect-free world. Messenger says desireless karma gains Brahmaloka, Vaikuṇṭha, Śiva/Solar/Śakti worlds, yet they too have defects; Brahman-state alone is faultless and attained on earth, the karma-ground, with dharma/artha/kāma/mokṣa. Mudgala rejects heaven’s heart-torment and fall, choosing Brahman. Messenger calls him unfortunate, ignorant and foolish for refusing what tapas/yajña/dāna earns. Mudgala answers bodily heaven cannot liberate a body-bound person; asks him to convey salutation and refusal to Indra with Bṛhaspati, bows, enters his fire-hall. Messenger returns as he came. Outcome: None. [Mudgala1.19.1–20](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam1.html)

## Kardama king’s prior-birth household, Varada vow, kingship and liberation

ID: `gup4-story-kardama`. Scope: 51.49–92. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Whole bounded body read/extracted with ended practice and outcome.

Source-order nested narration is retained. Former births, divine precedents and prospective efficacy remain distinct from enacted results. Kardama king is distinct from the Mudgala sage.

7. **gup4-event-007** — Śiva tells Skanda of righteous king Kardama, who rules to the ocean limits and has gods attending his court. When Bhṛgu visits, the king rises, honours him like a guru, seats and feeds him. Kardama asks his former-birth identity and the merit behind his unmatched, untroubled kingdom, divine honour, Kubera-like wealth and desired objects appearing at home. He wants to repeat that meritorious practice. Outcome: Kardama asks which past practice caused his kingship and how to repeat it.. [Upāsanā 51.49–57](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

8. **gup4-event-008** — Bhṛgu uses focused contemplation to recount Kardama’s earlier life as a poor, pure kṣatriya. His work does not support the household, and harsh words from his wife and children drive him into the forest without consulting them. He finds Saubhari teaching distress-removing knowledge to disciples among sages, bows, and is welcomed and seated. He asks relief from the verbal wounds of family and friends, heat, cold, hunger and thirst, while admitting he has not become detached from his harsh loved ones. Outcome: Kardama’s former self asks Saubhari for relief; relatives remain unnamed.. [Upāsanā 51.58–66](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

9. **gup4-event-009** — Compassionate Saubhari considers a remedy and recommends the Varada Gaṇeśa vow, which removes distress and sin and gives the four goals and unsurpassed siddhi. He cites earlier practice by Brahmins, kṣatriyas, vaiśyas and brahmarṣis. The kṣatriya asks Gaṇeśa’s identity, form, nature, acts and origin. Saubhari calls him eternal, stainless, sorrowless knowledge-Brahman, without beginning, middle or end, boundless and all-pervading, the source of Oṃ, Vedas and the world. Outcome: Saubhari provides the remedy and Gaṇeśa’s doctrinal identity.. [Upāsanā 51.67–73](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

10. **gup4-event-010** — Saubhari cites Brahmā’s full hundred years of arduous tapas for creation. Brahmā worships the pleased deity with varied services, divine gems and fruits, and gives the two mentally created maidens Siddhi and Buddhi. Gaṇeśa grants the one-syllable vidyā; the booned Brahmā then creates the world. Viṣṇu previously pleased the deity with the six-letter mantra, made an image and practiced the stated vow and rules without defect for one year. He received a boon and preserved the world. Saubhari urges the kṣatriya to worship this universal cause and leave distress. Outcome: Nested divine precedents supply exact austerities, mental-maiden gift and mantra/role grants.. [Upāsanā 51.74–80](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

11. **gup4-event-011** — The kṣatriya asks the time and procedure, promising practice for relief. Saubhari prescribes Śrāvaṇa’s non-dark fourth through Bhādra fourth, daily sixteen-service worship of an earthen image and Brahmin feeding, promising all wishes. Bhṛgu says Kardama’s former self performs it at Saubhari’s hermitage. At completion, Gaṇeśa’s favour transforms his home into a divine household with servants, cattle and Vedic sound; his adorned wife and children await him. After leaving the teacher he does not recognize the grand house and searches for his former home, until his wife’s messengers bring him in. He recognizes Varada’s power. Bhṛgu attributes his present kingship to the vow. Outcome: The past vow transforms his household and is said to cause later kingship.. [Upāsanā 51.81–89](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

12. **gup4-event-012** — Kardama joyfully repeats Bhṛgu’s prescribed practice, gains knowledge and dispassion, enjoys desired pleasures, places his sons in his office and reaches Gaṇeśa’s abode without return. Śiva urges Skanda to practice and cites Damayantī, Nala, Candraṅgada, gods, asuras, Gandharvas, sages and people as previous practitioners who gained wishes and a divine destination. Himavat closes his embedded history, promises Pārvatī that the vow will obtain Śiva, and explicitly commands her not to publicize it. Outcome: Kardama’s succession and no-return destination close his account; Himavat adds a prospective promise and secrecy command.. [Upāsanā 51.90–96](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

13. **gup4-event-013** — Pārvatī asks why Nala performed the vow. Himavat describes Niṣadha’s king as Veda-knowing, brave, generous, wealthy and pure, skilled with chariot, sword, arrows, bow, quiver and armour, and able to travel the three worlds. His innumerable cavalry, elephants, chariots, bowmen and weapon/fire-weapon fighters frighten Indra and direction-lords. His wife Damayantī is beautiful, virtuous and adorned; Brahmā fashioned her from selected essence and gave her the beauty of women in the three worlds. Minister Padmahasta is valiant, intelligent like Bṛhaspati, skilled in policy like Aṅgiras. Outcome: The king, wife, minister and capacities are introduced; comparison sages are not teachers.. [Upāsanā 52.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

14. **gup4-event-014** — Padmahasta is compared to Meru in height and the ocean in depth. A court with Apsaras dancers, praisers and brahmarṣis receives Gautama. Nala honours and seats him, calling his own birth, kingship, parents, lineage and life fruitful through the visit. Gautama says he wanted to see the king’s wealth, reports praise by Brahmā, Indra, Hari and Śiva, then asks leave. Nala pleads that he stay to remove uncertainty; the sage agrees. The king asks which merit or tapas produced his extraordinary wealth and who he was in a former birth. Outcome: Nala asks Gautama to explain the merit behind his wealth.. [Upāsanā 52.10–20](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

15. **gup4-event-015** — Gautama says Nala was formerly a poor, pure, knowledgeable kṣatriya in Pippala city beyond Gauṇḍadeśa. Harsh words from his wife, children and friends lead him into the forest at night without consulting them. Wandering among woods, animals and lakes, he finds Kaushika’s hermitage resounding with Vedic recitation and bows. The compassionate sage raises him and blesses him with Gaṇeśa’s auspiciousness. The joyful former self asks for a method granting wishes, ending poverty, and giving enjoyment and liberation. The printed numbering repeats 25; the body is uninterrupted. Outcome: Nala’s former self seeks a remedy from Kaushika; duplicate verse numbering leaves no body gap.. [Upāsanā 52.21–28 (24 printed25)](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

16. **gup4-event-016** — Kaushika teaches a month-long Gaṇeśa vow: make a pleasing earthen image, perform the previously stated procedure and daily worship, and hear the story, thereby gaining success. The kṣatriya first asks Gaṇeśa’s identity so he can practice with knowledge. Kaushika describes the ineffable Brahman whose differentiated forms appear in the world, the world’s creator, father, mother and teacher, meditated on by Brahmā, Indra, Śiva and Viṣṇu. Outcome: Guru instruction and deity knowledge precede the vow.. [Upāsanā 52.29–35](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

17. **gup4-event-017** — With Kaushika’s permission the former self returns home and begins the bright Śrāvaṇa-fourth vow, making the taught image. He meditates on Gaṇeśa while standing, speaking, silent, moving, sleeping and eating. The vow gives success and wealth of elephants, chariots, horses, cattle and servants. He donates for divine pleasure and builds a costly Gaṇeśa temple, enjoys pleasures, then dies and is born as Nala in Niṣadha. Gautama attributes Nala’s steady wealth and three-world honour to this and asks leave. Outcome: Actual wealth, donations, temple building, death and Nala’s rebirth are narrated.. [Upāsanā 52.36–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

18. **gup4-event-018** — After Gautama leaves, Nala trusts his account, makes a fine image, practices the vow, hears Gaṇeśa’s story and worships daily with devotion, obtaining all wishes. Himavat concludes that this is Nala’s practice after Gautama’s instruction about his former-birth vow, whose full glory cannot be narrated. Outcome: Nala’s current-life repeated vow fulfils his wishes and closes the account.. [Upāsanā 52.43–46](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

Prabhā’s son receives rites, initiation and a princess’s marriage; the first wife is reduced to service and kicked down. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 32.11–24; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

3. **gkr4-event-kirti-refuge** — Kīrti despairs and considers Hari’s aid to Draupadī. Devala teaches Gajānana worship. She worships the Mandāra image with sixteen services and a hymn framed by five-deity non-difference. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 32.25–44; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

4. **gkr4-event-dream** — Unable to obtain dūrvā, her friends bring śamī. At year’s end a dream gives unsolicited marital and son grants, predicts fourth-year poison death and Gṛtsamada’s revival, and promises hymn-reading fruits. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 33.1–16; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

5. **gkr4-event-son-and-poison** — After completing her vow, Kīrti waits. Prabhā’s illness changes the king’s preference; Kīrti bears the promised son. Jealous Prabhā gives poison, physicians fail, she is expelled and the child dies as his mother takes him into the forest. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 33.17–38; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

6. **gkr4-event-revival** — Gṛtsamada hears the grieving mother’s refuge request. He orders transfer of the śamī-worship merit into the child’s hand, and the boy rises. Kīrti reports the messengers’ battle and asks how the sage knew and why the leaf has this power. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 33.39–53; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

7. **gkr4-event-teaching** — Gṛtsamada describes the leaf’s superior devotional merit and introduces Nārada’s conversation with Indra before continuing the frame. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 33.54–60; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

8. **gkr5-event-kirti-easy-name38** — The revived child cannot pronounce the six-syllable mantra already taught. Kīrti asks an easy, effective formula leading to rule. Gṛtsamada gives his own four-syllable Dhundhirāja name, explains śamī worship and its royal/enemy-destroying benefit, and answers her request for the deity’s identity and history by beginning Durāsada and Divodāsa accounts. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 38.1–20; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

9. **gkr5-event-kirti-shrine** — The sage departs. Mother and son go to the Kāśī festival, offer roadside leaves/flowers, remain fasting and receive the ten-armed vision and boon invitation. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 48.24–40; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

10. **gkr5-event-kirti-request** — After a hymn and revival recap, Kīrti asks her son’s devotion, fame, detached rule, long life, virtues, strength and victory. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 48.41–52; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

11. **gkr5-event-kirti-grant** — All requests granted, with thousand-year life/thousand sacrifices, end-remembrance/form promise; own axe given and Parśubāhu name assigned. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 48.53–57; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

12. **gkr5-event-kirti-coronation** — Homecoming, parental embrace/report and coronation rites; father takes forest-dwelling initiation and son begins rule. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 49.1–14; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

13. **gkr5-event-kirti-rule-outcome** — A thousand-year righteous reign, constant leaf worship, wives/sons and generosity; succession followed by heaven for many kalpas in Dhundhi’s form. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 49.15–18; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

## Sāvitrī river curse, goddess śamī tapas and restored gods with river aṃśas

ID: `gkr4-story-river-curse-release`. Scope: Krīḍā36.1–41;37.1–33. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire stated chapter bodies read; narration, significant dialogue, all named actors/family, worship/requests/grants, weapons/powers/counters and aftermath extracted.

Bounded scene completion only; larger Vināyaka cycle incomplete. Independent print/Sanskrit expert audit pending.

1. **gkr4-event-neglect-curse** — At Mahābala, the hurried sacrifice neglects Gaṇeśa and Sāvitrī and seats Gāyatrī. Sāvitrī’s curse becomes river forms at the petitioners’ request, including Śiva/Veṇyā and Kṛṣṇa/Kṛṣṇā. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 36.1–15; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

2. **gkr4-event-refuge** — Brahmā and the goddesses acknowledge the neglected worship and plan devotion. The women go to the Rāma-established Vakratunda image in Karnāṭaka; his victory is recalled briefly. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 36.16–29; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

3. **gkr4-event-tapas-vision** — Varied austerities initially fail. Following a sky instruction, all offer śamī leaves and receive the four-armed, TWO-TUSK, mouse-mounted vision with Siddhi and Buddhi. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 36.30–41; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

4. **gkr4-event-restoration** — The goddess hymn requests original forms and constant remembrance. Gaṇeśa preserves Sāvitrī’s curse through river portions while restoring gods’ forms and offices. They apologize and worship. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 37.1–17; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

5. **gkr4-event-install-aftermath** — Gods install the stone Heramba temple at Supattana; Indra takes the earlier Mandāra image. Divine offices and household joys return. Brahmā performs twelve YEARS of tapas and repeats the sacrifice. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 37.18–27; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

6. **gkr4-event-frame-return** — Gṛtsamada applies the plant account to the child’s revival and asks to leave. Kīrti bows and dismisses him; devotional hearing and morning remembrance fruits close the account, before another question in38. Outcome: None. [Krīḍā 37.28–33; https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam2.html)

## Brahmā’s body creation, Manu and Kardama’s creation mission

ID: `mud4-story-creation-manu-kardama`. Scope: 2.4.1–23;2.5.39–63;2.6.1–15. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Whole bounded creation-mission/grant/offspring passages read and significant source-local creation origins, practices, family, request/grant/fulfillment and Yajña aftermath extracted. Later Dakṣa daughter genealogy is a separate spine.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud4-event-creation-body-orders** — Mudgala resumes Brahmā’s creation: Brahmins from mouth, Kṣatriyas arms, Vaiśyas thighs, Śūdras feet; renunciation from head, forest-stage heart, brahmacarya navel and household-state lower legs. Dharma chest and Adharma back set god/demon courses; gods/Gandharvas/Apsarases/Yakṣas/Asuras/Yātudhānas, snakes/birds/beasts/humans arise from his body. They act yet do not multiply. These are source-local cosmological body-emergences, not universal biological descent claims. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.4.1–7](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud4-event-nine-sargas** — Mudgala classifies nine creations: first mahat, second tanmātra/bhūta, third vaikārika/indriya (three prākṛta); fourth stationary beings, fifth tiryak, sixth upward-flow gods, seventh downward-flow humans, eighth bhautika beings, ninth kaumāra (six vaikṛta). The first three are described as buddhi-pūrva, guiding creatures through principles. Brahmā is distressed that his diverse creatures do not multiply. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.4.8–16](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud4-event-manu-satarupa-kardama-origin** — A couple emerges from Brahmā: Śatarūpā from left limb and Svāyambhuva Manu from right, first knowledgeable in Kṣatriya dharma. They ask orders; he directs sexual procreation through tapas. They bow, go to forest and practice intense Gaṇeśa meditation. Kardama emerges from Brahmā’s shadow, is ordered to create, then goes to forest desiring progeny and meditates on white-form Viṣṇu with prescribed tapas. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.4.17–23](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud4-event-manu-eight-syllable-tapas** — Svāyambhuva Manu with wife goes to forest, performs intense tapas by eight-syllable procedure1000 DIVINE years and gains Gaṇeśa’s appearance. He bows, worships and hymns elephant/Human-elephant/Oṃ form, four arms, mouse vehicle, Svānanda, Siddhi/Buddhi and yoga, saying vision inspires praise and blesses father/tapas/knowledge/lineage/learning/vows. Gaṇeśa raises him, offers a boon and promises his hymn readers/hearers all ends, progeny/prosperity, sorrow removal, wealth/grain, four aims/Brahman and firm devotion. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.5.39–58](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

5. **mud4-event-manu-request-grant** — Manu requests firm Gaṇeśa devotion eliminating delusion, capacity to create, excellent dharma maintenance, lasting protection of subjects, success in every wish and continual dwelling among Gaṇeśa devotees. Gaṇeśa assents to Manu, vanishes and goes to his own realm. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.5.59–63](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

6. **mud4-event-manu-family-kardama-kapila-yajna** — Manu and Śatarūpā bow to Brahmā and depart at his order; Manu rules dharmically, armed and fond of Gaṇapatyas. Through sexual union they produce sons Priyavrata/Uttānapāda and daughters Ākūti/Devahūti/Prasūti. He gives Prasūti to Dakṣa, Ākūti to Ruci and Devahūti to Kardama. Kardama’s100-year Viṣṇu tapas gains appearance; he asks Viṣṇu as son, receives grant, and Viṣṇu as Kapila becomes his son in Devahūti. Ruci’s mānasa twin Yajña/Dakṣiṇā is born in Ākūti; Yajña Viṣṇu fathers12 Yāma gods in Dakṣiṇā. With wife/sages he makes a jeweled four-armed Gaṇeśa image, constantly worships by yoga; Gaṇeśa gives marvelous yoga. Yajña becomes foremost Gaṇapatya yogin, chants Heramba and teaches disciples Gaṇapatya yoga. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.6.1–15](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Madhu/Kaiṭabha defeat Viṣṇu; Gaṇeśa’s remembered counter and their cutting

ID: `mud4-story-madhu-kaitabha`. Scope: 2.4.24–65. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud4-event-madhu-kaitabha-battle** — During this interval Madhu and Kaiṭabha arise from Viṣṇu’s ear dirt, conquer three worlds by arm strength and scatter terrified gods. They approach to eat Brahmā; Brahmā hymns Nidrā and she releases sleeping/unmanifest Viṣṇu, who rises. Viṣṇu wrestles them5000 years without defeating them; the powerful pair crushes him. He conceals himself, flees, takes Śaṅkara’s refuge, bows, narrates the battle and asks a victory method. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.4.24–30](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud4-event-visnu-six-syllable-tapas** — Śiva says Viṣṇu forgot Gaṇeśa and should worship for siddhi. He gives the six-syllable great Gaṇeśa mantra. Viṣṇu goes to Daṇḍakāraṇya, sits in lotus posture, meditates on Gaṇeśa and performs intense tapas by the six-syllable procedure1000 years. Gaṇeśa comes to grant; Viṣṇu joyfully bows, worships and begins hymn. The chapter does not explicitly label these1000 years divine. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.4.31–36](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud4-event-visnu-hymn-benefits** — Viṣṇu hymns Gaṇeśa as protector, Siddhi/Buddhi lord, remover of devotee obstacles and obstacle-giver to nondevotees, qualified/unqualified cosmic/acosmic Brahman, human-elephant form, cause/beyond cause, Jyeṣṭharāja, yoga giver in yogin hearts, māyā support and deluder of māyā-makers. He says grace gives intellect/praise, sight makes him Brahman-formed/yoga-giver; he dances with tears/raised hair. Gaṇeśa offers wishes pleased by tapas/devotion and promises hymn reciters/hearers all siddhis, four human aims and immediate desired ends through grace. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.4.37–51](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud4-event-madhu-kaitabha-boon-counter** — Viṣṇu requests rare unwavering devotion, incomparable knowledge, universal fame and varied strength, destruction-method for Madhu/Kaiṭabha and strength against other demons. Gaṇeśa assents, sends him to battle promising victory by strength. He reveals Brahmā, pleased by the pair’s tapas, kept their death very secret at boon-granting: when they themselves speak death, kill them by that stated mode. He says taking māyā and residing in their and Viṣṇu’s hearts he will bring them under Viṣṇu’s control; when Viṣṇu remembers him he will know the victory-method. The original demons’ request/tapas details and exact death words are not narrated here. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.4.52–60](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

5. **mud4-event-madhu-kaitabha-ending** — Gaṇeśa disappears. Viṣṇu establishes a great image with good Brahmins, worships richly; the place becomes Siddhikṣetra, renowned for siddhis through observance and served by siddhas. He resides/worships there devotedly with wife and entourage, then bows and hastens to battle. Meditating on Gaṇeśa he cuts the two powerful demons. No named cutting weapon, thighs/dry-place condition or final death dialogue is supplied in this chapter. Outcome: Viṣṇu cuts both demons while meditating on Gaṇeśa; narrator does not give detailed final duel or death speech.. [Mudgala2.4.61–65](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

Led by Dakṣa they hymn him as witness/self/yoga/peace, creation-preservation-destruction, all gods, beings/elements/food, cit/bodha/videha/asat/sat/sama/Svānanda/ayoga/yoga and Cintāmaṇi of mental activities. She grants future daughterhood in many forms and filling the world through offspring, promises her hymn’s readers/hearers all wishes, and vanishes. Mudgala then addresses current Dakṣa: he abandoned Gaṇeśa name/mantra, scorned Satī who became ash, was made sorrowful by Vighneśa, but prior impressions restored memory. Kingship prosperity induces proud blindness/destroys knowledge, so sages avoid it; hear the present account devotedly. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.5.25–38](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Atri’s peace-yoga instruction, Datta’s realization and Vijñāna installation

ID: `mud4-story-datta-peace-vijnana`. Scope: 2.8.1–86. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud4-event-datta-peace-question** — Datta moves through varied avadhūta yoga conduct, sometimes eating/body behavior like cattle/beasts/birds. Reaching sahaja and inward-controller independence yet lacking peace despite manifest/unmanifest Brahman practice, he approaches father Atri, bows and is seated respectfully among yogins. He asks peace-yoga beyond qualified/unqualified/equanimity/turya/sahaja states he has practiced, troubled by independence still remaining. Atri promises eternal peace-yoga learned from Brahmā, says Gaṇapati is their and Śiva/gods’ family deity, origin/support of all; worship him to gain peace as other gods did. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.8.1–15](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud4-event-atri-peace-doctrine** — Atri maps name/form to Śakti/asat, immortal self to Solar/sat, their unity to Viṣṇu/sadasat, distinct fourth/neti/nirmoha/independence to Śiva, and fourfold union to māyā-Svānanda Gaṇeśa. Karma yoga joins acts; knowledge yoga joins knowledge; their nonduality gives sama/ānanda; freely adopting/rejecting these is independent sahaja. Svānanda union joins worlds/Brahmans in nondifference; ayoga excludes union/entry and māyā, described as nirvṛtti. He distinguishes Svānanda-residing Gaṇeśa from mere Svānanda identity, play/union from playless yoga; dissolution of both saṃyoga/ayoga is Gaṇeśa’s peace/full yoga, where with/without māyā distinction is delusion. Terms remain this teacher’s doctrinal maps, not cross-school equivalences. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.8.16–36](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud4-event-atri-mind-siddhi-initiation** — Atri calls Buddhi fivefold mind and Siddhi deluding accomplishment, distinguishing dharma/artha/kāma/mokṣa and Brahman-state siddhis. Reflections in five mental activities and five desired sovereignties are described as Gaṇarāja form and jīva respectively; desire circles in their wealth. With fivefold mind destroyed and sovereignties dissolved Datta will become Gaṇarāja in peace-yoga. Atri urges leaving prior avadhūta path to become chief avadhūta by casting off great mind/sovereignty, offers prescribed Gaṇeśa mantra and ritually gives one-syllable mantra to his son; Datta bows and leaves. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.8.37–45](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud4-event-datta-ganga-vision-hymn** — Datta, explicitly Viṣṇu-form, worships Gaṇeśa on Gaṅgā’s SOUTH bank, relinquishes previous grounds and practices heart meditation/peace. After ONE YEAR he attains peace and becomes Gaṇapatya. Gaṇeśa freely visits his hermitage; Datta rises/bows/hymns family deity as yoga-peace, Siddhi/Buddhi and fivefold mind, sovereignty/delusion-maker/destroyer, Svānanda/saṃyoga, Sāṃkhya/videha/bodha/deha-dehī, ahaṃkāra/mahat/bindu/nāda/Guṇeśa and creator/preserver/destroyer/Sun/Śakti/all beings. He says sight blesses body/parents/lineage/learning/yoga/tapas; dances with tears/raised hair. Gaṇeśa takes him by hand and embraces. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.8.46–65](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

5. **mud4-event-vijnana-ganesa-grants** — Gaṇeśa declares Datta a yogin through his grace, with full unwavering peace and no future distinction; to deepen love he will stay here. Atri’s Gaṇeśa knowledge becomes direct realization here, so he calls it Vijñānakṣetra and himself Vijñānagaṇapa; sight gives peace and devoted residents receive peace-yoga near Datta. Śiva formerly realized knowledge here and was named Vijñāneśvara by him; Datta’s practice near Śiva gives friendship through grace. Datta’s hymn gives users yoga-peace/Brahman, four aims, all siddhis and knowledge. No separate desired-boon request is narrated before this declaration. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.8.66–74](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

6. **mud4-event-datta-self-offering-installation** — Gaṇeśa vanishes; saddened Datta meditates and by grace offers self in yoga-nondifference. Devotee Datta/master Gaṇeśa separation ceases, with servant established in master-form: ātmānivedana devotion through peace-yoga. Vijñāneśvara Śiva appears, embraces his new friend, says he meditated on this same Gaṇeśa here. Placing Datta foremost, Śiva/Brahmins install Gaṇeśa on Gaṅgā south bank and sages name Vijñānagaṇarāja. The Vijñāna field gives travelers peace-yoga/all siddhis; installation is JYEṢṬHA bright Caturthī, annually celebrated then. Gods/yogins/Gandharvas/siddhas/nāgas/people serve; hearing/reading Datta account gives all siddhis. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.8.75–86](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)
