## Nārada instructs Priyavrata to rule before forest yoga

ID: `mud4-story-priyavrata-rule-retirement`. Scope: 2.9.6–44;2.10.1–9. Newly completed this continuation: False.

Whole bounded9.6–10.9 narrative read and family/elder relation, Nārada duties and initiation, Manu retirement, Priyavrata rule/succession/retirement and final yoga outcome extracted. Further outer-island geography separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud4-event-priyavrata-duties** — Sūta resumes Manu offspring at Śaunaka’s request; Dakṣa asks their deeds. Manu’s fully dharmic sons are Priyavrata/Uttānapāda; ELDER Priyavrata sees bodily transience and goes to forest. Distressed Manu asks visiting Nārada to instruct him. Priyavrata rises/bows at Nārada’s arrival. Nārada affirms transience but says human body owes three debts and sustains world through dharmic acts: gods by yajña, ancestors by śrāddha, human debt by progeny. He urges rule first, discharge debts, establish sons in kingdom, then go to forest for yoga. Affectionate son address is teacher speech, not replacing Manu parentage. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.9.6–23](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud4-event-priyavrata-ganesa-initiation** — Priyavrata asks Nārada how one attains Brahman. Nārada teaches Gaṇeśa as yoga/lord of groups: outer/inner groups such as food are Brahman, their lord grants peace. He calls Gaṇeśa family deity, says ancestor Guṇeśa worshipped him to become Brahman; Brahmā/Viṣṇu/Śiva/Sun/Śakti are Guṇeśa sons who worshipped for peace; Brahmā’s sons including his speaking group became liberated/all-knowing yogins by worship. Priyavrata should worship too. He gives him one-syllable mantra and all Gaṇeśa worship instruction. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.9.24–37](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud4-event-manu-retirement-rule** — Brahmā and Manu arrive, sons bow. Brahmā again instructs grandson Priyavrata, who returns with Manu to city. Manu establishes TWO sons in rule, goes to forest and worships Gaṇeśa by yoga. Beyond five mind-grounds, leaving mind he becomes Brahman, attains peace-yoga, goes to Brahmaloka and remains worshipping Gaṇeśa. Priyavrata rules earth dharmically with policy/dāna/renunciation, wins fame; varṇa/āśrama people are content, truthful and devoted to duties. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.9.38–44](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud4-event-priyavrata-sons-retirement** — Priyavrata has10 powerful dharmic sons: Āgnīdhra,Idhmajihva,Yajñabāhu,Vīra,Hiraṇyaretas,Savana,Ghṛtapṛṣṭha,Medhātithi,Vītihotra,Kavi. Kavi/Savana/Vīra do not desire rule; he appoints seven others to Jambū/Plakṣa/Śālmala/Kuśa/Krauñca/Śāka/Puṣkara respectively. He then goes to forest, receives yoga from Nārada, becomes Gaṇapatya and finally Gaṇeśa-formed through yoga; source calls no comparable king past/future. Mother not specified here. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.10.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Sulabha, Sumudrā and Madhusūdana: curses and inadvertent rescue

ID: `gup5-story-trio-rescue`. Scope: 62.1–44. Newly completed this continuation: True.

All bodies and named transitions in this bounded witness-local scope are read and extracted; its stated ending is present.

Source-order embedded narration remains distinct from chronology. Brief reports are complete only as bounded reports in this witness, not exhaustive biographies or other-corpus arcs. Ritual formula claims do not become new narrated births.

10. **gup5-event-010** — Kṛtavīrya’s father accepts the explanation of the dark-fourth meal at moonrise and asks why dūrvā is especially dear to Gaṇeśa. Brahmā introduces Sulabha of southern Jāmbā city, a wealthy, generous and self-controlled kṣatriya versed in Vedas/śāstras and devoted to Vikaṭa. Wife Sumudrā is beautiful, faithful, god/Brahmin/guest-devoted and follows her husband’s mind. Purified after bathing, they sit together to hear Purāṇa. Poor Brahmin Madhusūdana, continually contemplating the supreme deity, arrives for alms in worn clothes; Sulabha joyfully bows. Outcome: Named household and poor visitor introduced before reciprocal curses.. [Upāsanā 62.1–10](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

11. **gup5-event-011** — Deluded, Sulabha suddenly laughs at the poor Brahmin. Angered, Madhusūdana curses him to become a suffering plough-pulling bull. Hearing her husband cursed, Sumudrā angrily curses the Brahmin to the printed cakrivān/feces-eating state; he curses her to become a poor, cruel caṇḍālī eating excrement and urine. Abandoning their rare human bodies, Sulabha becomes the endlessly labouring bull, Madhusūdana a donkey, and Sumudrā the violent, impoverished caṇḍālī. The subsequent actual donkey form is recorded without inventing another interpretation of cakrivān. Outcome: Three actual changed births/forms follow the curses; no numeric curse duration is given.. [Upāsanā 62.11–19 (15 printed55)](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

12. **gup5-event-012** — The wasted Sumudrā/caṇḍālī wanders south of the city and sees Gaṇeśa’s temple with trees, birds and observance practitioners seeking wishes, sons, liberation or wealth. On Bhādra fourth, a city-wide Gaṇeśa festival meets a torrential storm. Driven and beaten from houses, she enters the temple holding fire; some beat her there, but yogins restrain them. She lights grass to warm herself. Wind carries one dūrvā sprout onto Gaṇeśa’s head. The cold-fearing donkey enters, and the bull, released from the plough, also comes by providence. Outcome: Unintended grass-offering occurs during three cursed beings’ storm refuge.. [Upāsanā 62.20–29](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

13. **gup5-event-013** — The bull and donkey eat her grass and fight with hooves and horns near the deity while people sleep. Dūrvā falls from their mouths onto Gaṇeśa’s trunk and feet, pleasing him. She strikes them with a staff and eats the offerings. People awaken at hoof sounds, beat and expel her with staff, fist, elbow and stones. Fearing contact, they bathe the deity with enchanted tīrtha water and worship afresh. They also beat all three and close the temple doors, leaving the trio without escape. Outcome: Further inadvertent offerings and renewed worship arise amid violence.. [Upāsanā 62.30–36](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

14. **gup5-event-014** — The cries and movements of the trapped trio move Vikaṭa. He considers that their presence caused renewed worship, their dūrvā reached him and they performed many circumambulations. He declares one dūrvā offering on Bhādra bright fourth and circumambulation make a person honoured and worthy of his regard. He decides to take the trio to his own abode and sends Gaṇeśa-shaped Gaṇas with a festive vimāna, Gandharvas and Apsarases. The Gaṇas give them divine bodies, place them aboard and swiftly bring them to Gaṇeśa’s abode before astonished onlookers. Outcome: The three cursed beings actually receive divine bodies and Gaṇeśa’s abode.. [Upāsanā 62.37–44](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Janaka’s feeding test and the poor couple’s devotional gift

ID: `gup5-story-janaka-test`. Scope: 65.1–45;66.1–25. Newly completed this continuation: True.

All bodies and named transitions in this bounded witness-local scope are read and extracted; its stated ending is present.

Source-order embedded narration remains distinct from chronology. Brief reports are complete only as bounded reports in this witness, not exhaustive biographies or other-corpus arcs. Ritual formula claims do not become new narrated births.

26. **gup5-event-026** — Kauṇḍinya recounts Nārada visiting Gaṇeśa after many days. The sage bows, praises the fruitful sight and stands with joined hands. Gaṇeśa holds his hand and seats him. Nārada says he came to report an astonishing matter and would depart after bowing. Gaṇeśa asks him to describe everything before returning to his hermitage. Outcome: Nārada receives an explicit invitation to tell the report.. [Upāsanā 65.1–6](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

27. **gup5-event-027** — Nārada reports Janaka of Mithilā as proud, generous and learned in Vedas and Vedāṅgas: he continually feeds and honours Brahmins with ornaments, clothing and gifts, gives wealth to poor, blind and destitute people, and grants petitions. Nārada wonders whether Gaṇeśa’s satisfaction accounts for undiminished wealth. On visiting, Janaka mocks him through pride in Brahman knowledge. Nārada praises Gaṇeśa as giver of the king’s devotional desires, but Janaka claims to be lord, giver, enjoyer, protector, maker, cause and instrument, with none other in three worlds. Angered, Nārada says God alone makes the world, challenges the king’s apparent religious display, and promises evidence soon. Outcome: The king’s attributed claims and Nārada’s challenge motivate the later test.. [Upāsanā 65.7–16](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

28. **gup5-event-028** — Having heard the report, Gaṇeśa honours Nārada with arghya, ornaments, flowers and sandal. With permission Nārada goes to Viṣṇu in Vaikuṇṭha. Gaṇeśa comes to Mithilā to test the king’s devotion, wearing a repulsive old Brahmin form with bleeding wounds, flies and missing teeth. He stumbles, faints and falls amid children; people cover their noses and spit. At the royal gate he asks to be announced as an old, hungry Brahmin guest desiring a meal. Janaka orders his admission, observes his filthy clothes, trembling, bleeding and exhaustion, suspects a divine test, and resolves to satisfy him if merit allows. Outcome: The narrator identifies the disguised deity; Janaka separately conjectures a divine guest.. [Upāsanā 65.17–28](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

29. **gup5-event-029** — The guest says he came after hearing Janaka’s fame and asks food until fully satisfied, promising the merit of one hundred sacrifices. Janaka takes him inside, honours him and serves tasty food. The guest consumes the entire portion in one mouthful, then a mountain-sized portion in one mouthful, then prepared food sufficient for ten thousand. Rice is put to cook in innumerable vessels and he eats every finished batch. Outcome: Successive immense servings fail to satisfy the guest.. [Upāsanā 65.29–35](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

30. **gup5-event-030** — Some advisers conjecture that the guest is a Rākṣasa and gifts to such beings give no merit; others say even the three worlds could not satisfy him and urge offering grain. All palace, city and village grain is gathered and consumed, still without satisfaction. Messengers report none remains; Janaka lowers his head. The guest departs house to house asking food. Residents explain their grain was collected by the king and already eaten by him. He questions how he can leave unsatisfied after hearing Janaka’s unparalleled giving fame. Amid silence he finds the Brahmin home of Virocanā and Triśiras, enters like its owner, and sees no equipment or metal vessels. Outcome: Janaka’s resources are exhausted; the test continues in a poor couple’s home.. [Upāsanā 65.36–45](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

31. **gup5-event-031** — The couple sits on bare earth, with sky as covering, avoiding contact with metals. They eat unasked food, use water for their rites and maintain purity. Amid flies and mosquitoes their Gaṇeśa image is worshipped with flowers and leaves in exclusive devotion. The disguised guest says Janaka did not satisfy him and ostentatious action cannot preserve sattva; he asks anything in their house that could satisfy him. The couple wonders how they, poor people, can feed one whom an emperor could not, comparing a drop with rivers unable to fill the ocean. He says even a small devotional gift greatly satisfies him; abundant gifts without devotion or given for display are fruitless. Outcome: The guest explains why devotional giving differs from abundance without devotion.. [Upāsanā 66.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

32. **gup5-event-032** — The couple swears nothing remains except one dūrvā sprout left from the morning Gaṇeśa worship. At the guest’s request Virocanā offers that single sprout with devotion, imagining rice, pāyasa, varied cooked dishes, accompaniments and lickable or suckable foods upon it. He joyfully eats it; his stomach-fire immediately subsides, he gains complete satisfaction and embraces Triśiras. Outcome: One actually offered sprout accompanied by imagined foods satisfies the guest whom abundant actual food did not satisfy.. [Upāsanā 66.10–16](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

33. **gup5-event-033** — Gaṇeśa abandons the repulsive disguise and appears four-armed, lotus-eyed and trunk-bearing, holding lotus, axe, rosary and tusk, wearing costly crown, earrings, divine clothing and scents. He invites the couple to choose their desired boon. They ask for firm devotion wherever they are born, or liberation from the difficult ocean of transmigration, wanting nothing else. He assents with tathā, joyfully embraces Triśiras again and vanishes. Outcome: The deity explicitly assents and withdraws; no later death or selected alternative is invented.. [Upāsanā 66.17–22](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

34. **gup5-event-034** — Kauṇḍinya tells Aśrayā that this is why he offers dūrvā: the deity who remained unsatisfied by innumerable foods gained full satisfaction through one sprout. He states that hearing the offering’s greatness gives all wishes; devoted hearing or telling brings sons, wealth and enjoyment here and hereafter, Gaṇeśa devotion, and liberation for a desireless person. Outcome: Kauṇḍinya applies the completed test account to his own practice.. [Upāsanā 66.23–25](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Prescribed Sankaṣṭa worship and its completion protocol

ID: `gup5-story-sankasta-procedure`. Scope: 69.1–62;71.1–17. Newly completed this continuation: True.

All bodies and named transitions in this bounded witness-local scope are read and extracted; its stated ending is present.

Source-order embedded narration remains distinct from chronology. Brief reports are complete only as bounded reports in this witness, not exhaustive biographies or other-corpus arcs. Ritual formula claims do not become new narrated births.

47. **gup5-event-047** — Śūrasena asks Indra how Brahmā taught Kṛtavīrya the successful Sankaṣṭa vow. Indra recounts the father approaching Brahmā in Satyaloka and asking what people in distress, anxiety, separation from friends, difficult undertakings, or seeking wealth, sons and good fortune should do. Brahmā prescribes the wish-accomplishing vow: daytime bathing with herbs and white sesame, proper intention and Gaṇeśa meditation, and devotional worship with Āgama mantras on dark fourth at night’s moonrise. The king asks the exact worship method. Outcome: Brahmā answers a general remedy request and specifies time, preparation and ritual authority.. [Upāsanā 69.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

48. **gup5-event-048** — After daily rites at moonrise, prepare a clean cow-dung-coated place with a pavilion and honour Gaṇeśa’s seat with kuṅkuma and rice. Set a kalaśa with five gems and a gold vessel; silver, copper or bamboo is permitted when unavailable, and cloth according to ability. Draw the Āgama-prescribed yantra and install a well-formed gold image. Meditate one-tusked, huge, molten-gold-coloured, large-bellied, wide-eyed Gaṇeśa with fire-like eyes, riding a mouse, fanned by Gaṇas, with Śeṣa as sacred thread. Invoke him to remove distress and remain present until the vow ends. Outcome: The image/mount/thread are meditation instructions; no actual travel or new birth is inferred.. [Upāsanā 69.10–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

49. **gup5-event-053** — The parenthetical ritual alternatives recite Puruṣa as thousand-headed, thousand-eyed and thousand-footed, embracing what has been and will be; Virāj arises, and Puruṣa arises from Virāj. Gods perform sacrifice with Puruṣa as offering, spring as ghee, summer as fuel and autumn as offering-material; gods, Sādhyas and sages worship the primordial sacrificial Puruṣa. From the sacrifice arise the curd-ghee mixture and aerial, forest and village animals, Ṛc/Sāman/metres/Yajus, horses, creatures with teeth on both sides, cattle, goats and sheep. The bodily social formula assigns mouth to Brahmins, arms to Rājanyas, thighs to Vaiśyas and feet to Śūdras. The cosmic formula gives Moon from mind, Sun from eye, wind/breath from ear, Fire from mouth, interspace from navel, heaven from head, earth from feet and directions from ears. The gods bind Puruṣa as sacrificial animal with seven enclosures and twenty-one fuel-sticks; the closing alternative praises ancient Sādhyas in heaven. These are prescribed Vedic recitations, without a new narrated Gaṇeśa birth or universal biological identity assertion. Outcome: All named cosmogonic formula roles remain source-attributed ritual assertions.. [Upāsanā 69.17–37](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

50. **gup5-event-049** — The prescribed offerings proceed through seat, foot-water, gem-and-fruit arghya, sipping water from Gaṅgā and other tīrthas, pañcāmṛta of milk/curd/ghee/sugar/honey, bathing with Narmadā, Candrabhāgā and Gaṅgā-confluence waters, two garments, sacred thread, sandal, ghee/kuṅkuma rice, campaka/mallikā/dūrvā and other flowers, and fragrant incense. The formulas repeatedly ask acceptance and distress-removal, addressing Gaṇeśa as Gaṇādhīśa, Umāputra, Modakapriya, Lambodara, Ibhavaktra, Gaṇādhyakṣa, Vināyaka, Paraśudhārin, Īśaputra, Mūṣakavāhana, Mahākāya and Dhūmraketu. Parenthetical Puruṣasūkta alternatives accompany many services. Outcome: The service formulas supply petitions and source-specific epithets; they are not new narrated biological scenes.. [Upāsanā 69.18–29](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

51. **gup5-event-050** — Continue with lamp, modaka/apūpa/laḍḍū/pāyasa with sugar and ghee-cooked food, coconut/grapes/mango/pomegranate, areca/cardamom/clove/betel leaves, gold dakṣiṇā, and twenty-one dūrvā with a twenty-one-name list. Offer camphor light and campaka/aśoka/bakula/pārijāta flower handfuls. The hymn attributes creation, protection, destruction and universal appearance to the elephant-faced deity; the bowing formula praises obstacle-removal, devotee relief, liberation, knowledge and Vedic foundation. Parenthetical Vedic alternatives include Puruṣa-body social and cosmic emanations. Outcome: Offerings, names and cosmic praise remain explicitly within prescribed recitation.. [Upāsanā 69.30–39](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

52. **gup5-event-051** — After the hymn bow repeatedly and make twenty-one circumambulations according to ability. The prayer says those seeking tasks without worship of Gaṇeśa are ruined. Offer vāyana to the Brahmin teacher and special arghya with fruit, flowers, rice, water and dakṣiṇā. Invoke Heramba for distress-removal and honour Indra and the surrounding world guardians. Prepare ghee-cooked modakas with cooked mung and sesame and other foods/betel as able, then offer twenty-one dūrvā separately using the verse names. This second sequence includes Abhayaprada, Amareśvara, Gajavakra and Sarvavidyāpradāyaka alongside names shared with the earlier list; the distinct printed forms are retained. Outcome: Circumambulation, teacher offering, guardian worship and separate sprout-name sequence extend the prescribed services.. [Upāsanā 69.40–50](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

53. **gup5-event-052** — Request satisfaction with the properly directed worship, fulfillment of inner wishes, removal of obstacles and present enemies, destruction of enemies’ intelligence and prosperity of friends. Bow repeatedly and perform one hundred eight homa offerings. For vow-completion prepare vāyana of modakas or laḍḍū/vaṭaka and other foods with twenty-one fruits, wrap in red cloth and give to the teacher. Hear the sacred story and offer arghya to the tithi, then Gaṇeśa, then Moon seven times. The Moon formula calls him milk-ocean-born, arising in Atri’s lineage, accompanied by Rohiṇī. Ask the deity’s pardon, feed Brahmins, eat their offered remainder silently in seven mouthfuls according to ability and comfort, and perform this procedure in all four months. Outcome: The detailed worship chapter ends with monthly repetition; the broader explanation and udyāpana continue.. [Upāsanā 69.51–62](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

59. **gup5-event-059** — The king asks Brahmā to explain udyāpana for public benefit. Brahmā prescribes vow-completion in the first, fifth or seventh month. Make a flower pavilion decorated with various fabrics and a multicoloured sarvatobhadra arrangement; worship on a kalaśa as before with sandal and flowers. Give coconut arghya to the tithi, Gaṇeśa and Moon. Their formulas ask distress-removal; the Moon formula again gives milk-ocean and Atri-lineage origin and Rohiṇī accompaniment. Offer edible, chewable, lickable, drinkable and suckable foods and many fruits. Outcome: Exact alternative completion months and separate recipient sequence are prescribed.. [Upāsanā 71.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

60. **gup5-event-060** — Choose a teacher and twenty-one officiants. Perform ten thousand homa offerings with Gaṇānāṃ tvā, or use the root mantra for one thousand, half that, or one hundred eight offerings; then give bali, full offering and vasordhārā. Finish homa and feed Brahmins. Give paired garments, kalaśas, dakṣiṇā and seats according to capacity without deceit about means. Honour the fed teacher with clothes/ornaments and give fruit-vāyana, a winnowing basket filled with pāyasa wrapped in red cloth, the gold Gaṇeśa image with dakṣiṇā, an āḍhaka of sesame, and an adorned kapilā cow with calf. Request the Brahmins’ pardon, praying Vighneśa be pleased. Completing udyāpana gives horse-sacrifice merit. Outcome: Completion protocol reaches its stated merit and closing pardon request.. [Upāsanā 71.10–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

## Brahmā’s brief earlier-practitioner reports and transmission rules

ID: `gup5-story-vow-exempla`. Scope: 70.1–25. Newly completed this continuation: True.

All bodies and named transitions in this bounded witness-local scope are read and extracted; its stated ending is present.

Source-order embedded narration remains distinct from chronology. Brief reports are complete only as bounded reports in this witness, not exhaustive biographies or other-corpus arcs. Ritual formula claims do not become new narrated births.

54. **gup5-event-054** — The king asks who first practiced and disclosed the vow and what merit/results it gives. Brahmā says Pārvatī practiced at Śiva’s instruction for four months when Skanda was gone and saw Kārttikeya in the fifth. Agastya desiring to drink the ocean practiced and drank it through Vighneśa’s favour in three months. Damayantī searching for Nala practiced for six months and then saw him. These are brief source reports; no unprovided journeys or battle details are added. Outcome: Three retrospective exempla give specific practice intervals and actual reported outcomes.. [Upāsanā 70.1–5](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

55. **gup5-event-055** — Citralekhā had taken Pradyumna’s son away. Grieving and uncertain where or by whom he was taken, Pradyumna hears Rukmiṇī address him as her son. She recalls Śambara abducting him when he was six days old, many years of grief and comparisons with other women’s growing sons, then Lomaśa’s fortunate visit and instruction in Sankaṣṭa-caturthī. She practiced four times; through its favour Pradyumna returned after killing Śambara in battle. She urges him to practice the same vow so he will learn where his son is. Outcome: Rukmiṇī’s retrospective rescue experience becomes explicit mother-to-son instruction for a new search.. [Upāsanā 70.5–12](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

56. **gup5-event-056** — Brahmā says Pradyumna practices the pleasing vow and hears from Nārada that the son is detained in Bāṇāsura’s city. At Uddhava’s instruction Kṛṣṇa, fearing a battle with Īśvara, properly practices the vow once. He goes to Śoṇitapura, defeats Bāṇāsura in combat, and quickly brings Aniruddha with Uṣā. Outcome: Nārada locates the detained son; one vow, actual combat victory and retrieval follow. No weapons or marriage ceremony are supplied here.. [Upāsanā 70.13–15](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

57. **gup5-event-057** — Brahmā says he too performed the vow desiring creation and produced varied creation through its power. Gods, asuras, humans, sages, Dānavas, Yakṣas, Kinnaras, Uragas and Rākṣasas also practiced to quell obstacles. He instructs practice during distress, hearing the story, silent eating with hands between knees while meditating Gaṇeśa, and sharing the Brahmins’ remainder with relatives. Tasks accomplish in few months. Teach neither non-devotees, atheists nor deceitful people, but a son, pupil or virtuous devotee. Calling the king dear, righteous and a worker for public good, Brahmā says to perform this before other vows; men and women should perform it before intended tasks. Outcome: Reported creation and wider practitioners support instructions, timing and transmission restrictions.. [Upāsanā 70.16–25](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/gaNeshapurANam1.html)

1. **mud5-event-jambu-sons-retirement** — Mudgala says Jambū’s ruler has nine sons Nābhi, Kimpuruṣa, Hari, Ilāvṛta, Hiraṇmaya, Kuru, Ramya, Bhadrāśva, Ketumāla; he gives each an eponymous region. Father is called Jambū ruler here, not named; prior 10.4 identifies him as Āgnīdhra. The SEVEN Priyavrata sons, distinct from these nine grandsons, then enter forest, perform tapas, become Gaṇapatyas/Brahman-knowers and at the end attain Gaṇeśa’s abode without return through yoga-nondifference. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.11.1–4](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html); [Mudgala2.10.1–9](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Mudgala’s world layers, luminary extents, eclipse and solar wheel

ID: `mud5-story-solar-wheel`. Scope: 2.12.1–42. Newly completed this continuation: True.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud5-event-world-layers-winds-distances** — Mudgala describes seven cosmic-egg worlds Bhūr, Bhuvar, Svar, Mahar, Jana, Tapo, Satya. Bhūr is the walking-accessible seven-island region illuminated by Sun/Moon; Bhuvar runs from Bhūr to Sun, Svar from Sun to Dhruva with corresponding Bhūr extent. Eight Vāyu divisions are Āvaha, Pravaha, Annavaha, Saṃvaha, Vivaha, Parāvaha, Parivaha, Vaha. Distance sequence in yojanas: Earth→Sun 100000, Sun→Moon 100000, Moon→stars 100000, stars→planet zone 200000, planet zone→ṛṣi zone 100000, ṛṣi zone→Dhruva 100000. Dhruva is axis of luminary wheel, holding wind-rays and moving stars. These are source cosmography, not modern astronomical measurements. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.12.1–12](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud5-event-luminary-extents-eclipse** — Sun maṇḍala extent 10000 and Moon 12000 yojanas; Rāhu 10000 below Sun, maṇḍala 13000. Darkness-formed Rāhu moves at parvan and rapidly covers Sun/Moon with shadow; called eclipse. Bhārgava’s maṇḍala is 1/16 Moon; Bṛhaspati 1/4 less than Bhārgava; Bhauma/Saura 1/4 less than Bṛhaspati; Saumya 1/4 less than those. Star/nakṣatra maṇḍalas successively 500/400/300/200, smallest star 1 ½ yojanas. Fractions preserved as stated, without calculated replacements. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.12.13–20](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud5-event-planet-speeds-order** — Three distant slow planets named Saura, Aṅgirā, Bhauma lie above four fast planets Sun, Moon, Budha, Bhārgava. Ordering upward: Sun, Moon, stars, Kavi/Bhārgava, Budha, Bhauma, Bṛhaspati, Śanaiścara, seven ṛṣis, Dhruva. Sun travels two ayanas and governs planets. Bhauma is above Soma’s son, referring to Budha; only this explicit patronymic is normalized as parenthood, not an invented Bhārgava/Bṛhaspati family. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.12.21–26](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud5-event-sun-chariot-cities-time** — Sun chariot 9000 yojanas and shaft twice that; axle one lakh anchored at Dhruva, second axle’s wheel on Mānasā mountain. Wheel is zodiac/twelve-spoked; seven horses are metres led by Gāyatrī. Southward ayana is fast, northward slow. On Mānasā are east Indra’s Amarāvatī, south Dharmarāja’s Saṃyamanī, west Varuṇa’s Sukhā, north Kubera’s Vibhā. Sun dawn at Indra city, midday Yama, evening Varuṇa, midnight Kubera; potter-wheel-like movement gives direction-dependent perceived times. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.12.27–36](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

5. **mud5-event-solar-cosmic-support** — Sun rays sustain people and fill three worlds, making day/night. Water, earth and sky and whole god/demon/human world arise, are supported and end in Sun. Enumerated deities, stars, fire, plants, Brahmins/water draw their radiance from him; he distributes all brilliance for play. Prabhākara is all-self/lord, Gaṇeśa portion, root of three worlds and karma-formed deity. This is source cosmological praise/support, not a universal biological genealogy. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.12.37–42](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

1. **mud5-event-moon-nectar-cycle** — Moon three-wheel chariot has 10 jasmine-white horses left/right; star pathways and Dhruva support with waxing/waning. Sun Suṣumna ray replenishes Moon nectar drunk by gods in sequence over half-month. With little fifteenth-part remaining, Pitṛs attend in afternoon and drink for 2 LAVAS; remaining kalā is svadhā nectar issued through rays at amāvāsyā, giving monthly satisfaction. Moon does not perish from nectar drinking; Sun causes waxing/waning. No lava-to-modern-time conversion. Outcome: None. 2. **mud5-event-nine-planet-chariots** — Budha/Soma-son chariot 4 wind-speed LIONS; Bhārgava golden 8 horses; Bhauma 8 RAMS; Bṛhaspati golden 8 REDhorses; Manda iron 8 BLUEvultures; Svarbhānu/Rāhu black lions and Ketu doves, counts unspecified. All 9 planet/star stations bind Dhruva by wind-rays; he revolves and moves them in every direction. No remembered horse substitutions. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.13.38–43](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Śeṣa dissolution,Viṣṇu supports,hells and cosmic-form conclusion

ID: `mud5-story-deep-support-chain`. Scope: 2.15.20–40. Newly completed this continuation: True.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud5-event-sesa-dissolution** — Śeṣa stands 30000 yojanas below Pātāla, Gaṇeśa devotee/portion, Viṣṇu kalāvatāra and called Kālarudra. Thousand-faced, powerful, poison-born flame burns entire world; deathless hence Ananta, he dissolves at Brahmā-day’s end and remains. Vāsuki-led Nāga kings, Prahlāda-led demons and Nāga women serve him; women worship for many desired successes, not a narrated grant of all requests. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.15.20–25](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud5-event-kurma-varaha-support** — 1000 yojanas below Śeṣa is Viṣṇu’s tortoise/Kūrma station, served by Varuṇa and others; 1000 further below is Viṣṇu’s Varāha, served by Earth and others, giving fearlessness to devotees. Whole structure rests on Śeṣa; Śeṣa rests on Kūrma, Kūrma on Varāha. Unsupported Varāha remembers Gaṇeśa in heart and by that power supports all. No battle or Earth-lifting episode supplied here. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.15.26–31](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud5-event-hells-cosmic-conclusion** — Below Varāha are purifying/punishing hells Raurava, Tāmisra, Mahāraurava, Andhatāmisra, Sūcīmukha, Lohadaṇḍa, Taptavāluka, Khaḍgapatraka, Lālāsya, Kṛmibhakṣya and many others, where sinners are cooked. Mudgala says the cosmic sphere cannot be fully expanded here; Dakṣa’s curse-delusion will end by Gaṇeśa worship and he will know truly. All cosmic eggs alike have 8 envelopes and Guṇeśa support; this is Gaṇapati’s egg-form briefly told. Devoted hearing/reading gives all, siddhis, enjoyment/liberation and sin-cover destruction. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.15.32–40](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Ṛṣabha’s lost peace,Śiva instruction,attained peace and Gaṇeśa devotion boon

ID: `mud5-story-rsabha-peace`. Scope: 2.16.9–58. Newly completed this continuation: True.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Source narration order. Embedded narratives have their own spines; parent records their framing transitions without duplicating events.

1. **mud5-event-rsabha-siva-search** — Forest Ṛṣabha performs various tapas and yoga, becomes avadhūta, passes jada/unmatta/piśāca and sahaja states. He follows fourth, unmanifest, neti, delusion-free, independent Śaṅkara Brahman and teaches Śiva wherever he goes. Yoga reveals independence as defect, since Brahman neither depends nor is independent; loses peace, visits Śiva’s abode, receives honor, and is astonished by Gaṇapatya yogins who are Śiva disciples. No austerity duration stated. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.16.9–17](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud5-event-rsabha-santi-request** — Ṛṣabha hymns Śiva as yoga/world-support/Brahman lord, asks full peace-giving śāntiyoga, declares servant and disciple. He explains that sahaja was highest he knew but independence took peace away. Śiva joyfully teaches that Gaṇeśa is peace-yoga, origin/support/end of all, and promises peace through a secret he ATTRIBUTES to Sāmaveda. This witness’s Sāmaveda attribution is not independent quotation verification. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.16.18–25](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud5-event-rsabha-ga-na-teaching** — Śiva teaches ga as all visible/invisible mind-and-speech forms, ṇa as mind/speech-free saṃyoga/ayoga; their Brahmans are principles and nirodha. Both letters are Brahman-forms in Gaṇapati name, and Gaṇeśa their lord. Contemplate mind/speech and their beyond as Gaṇeśa and māyā. He illumines five mind-grounds, hence Cintāmaṇi; worship him as yoga and relinquish grounds for peace. Gaṇeśa transcends saṃyoga/ayoga; abandon māyā-born delusion for happiness. All statements are attributed instruction. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.16.26–36](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud5-event-rsabha-peace-before-appearance** — Śiva stops teaching; Ṛṣabha bows and returns forest. Practicing taught yoga he gradually attains peace. He still never abandons Gaṇapati, so Gaṇeśa comes near him. Ṛṣabha prostrates in joy, with tears/gooseflesh and folded hands, hymns. Actual peace precedes divine appearance and later devotion boon. Outcome: Ṛṣabha attains peace BEFORE Gaṇeśa appears.. [Mudgala2.16.37–41](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

5. **mud5-event-rsabha-ganesa-hymn** — Ṛṣabha hymns Gaṇeśa as Brahman beyond difference/nondifference, formless eternal world-form, infinite, beyond beginning/middle/end, Guṇeśa creator/preserver/destroyer, many-avatar protector of devotees, god/demon forms and worshipped by them, yoga-giver and lord of yogas/Brahmans, all-origin and final survivor, Heramba in nijānanda, Siddhi/Buddhi lord, giver of four aims and Brahman attainment. Vedas and Śiva-led gods cannot fully praise him. Hymn ontology is attributed to Ṛṣabha. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.16.42–50](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

6. **mud5-event-rsabha-devotion-grant** — Ṛṣabha requests firm unwavering devotion, falls holding Gaṇeśa’s foot and is respectfully raised. Narrator calls him Śiva disciple/Śiva-wise and eternal Viṣṇu. Gaṇeśa promises immovable devotion and peace-giving hymn; whatever wished he grants through reciting/hearing it. God disappears; Ṛṣabha remains peaceful yogin and actually attains Gāṇeśatā. No extra immortality/wife/power request, or substitution of Gāṇeśatā for requested devotion. Outcome: Immovable devotion granted; Gaṇeśa disappears; Ṛṣabha attains Gāṇeśatā.. [Mudgala2.16.51–56](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

7. **mud5-event-rsabha-account-phala** — Devoted hearing/reading of Ṛṣabha’s account gives all, Brahman attainment and peace for peaceful minds. Account phala kept separate from hymn grant. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.16.57–58](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

After unspecified time Gaṇeśa comes, but absorbed sage does not notice. Gaṇeśa makes heart-resident Dhuṇḍhi disappear; Pulaha cannot see inner form, looks as if bewildered and sees Gaṇeśa outside. He rises, bows, worships by rule and hymns with joy/gooseflesh. Divine removal of meditation-form, not destruction of the god or killing event. Outcome: None. Vedas, yogins and Śiva/Viṣṇu-led gods cannot praise completely; he bows and asks lasting favor. Source hymn attribution, not universal physical assertion. Outcome: None. Gaṇeśa grants firm unwavering joy-giving devotion, and promises appearance near him whenever he remembers. Hymn gives devotion, enjoyment/liberation and peace-yoga; human reciters/hearers gain all happiness and become like Pulaha. God disappears; Pulaha remains and worships. No fixed appearance-duration or immortality request. Outcome: Gaṇeśa disappears; Pulaha remains worshipping.. [Mudgala2.18.20–26](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

## Bharata’s retirement,fawn attachment,deer rebirth and Gaṇeśa devotion before deer-body abandonment

ID: `mud5-story-bharata-deer-life`. Scope: 2.17.6–24; 2.18.27–37; embeddedPulahateaching/encounterrecordedseparately. Newly completed this continuation: True.

Entire bounded cited Sanskrit arc read; significant named actors, relationships, boons/powers, dialogue and transition checks by author. Independent root review separate.

Complete deer-life chronological arc only. Embedded Brahmā/Pulaha teaching has a separate PARTIAL spine at 17.42–43; this records Bharata hearing/retaining it without claiming full philosophical normalization. Later human rebirth starts 19 and remains queued.

1. **mud5-event-bharata-retirement** — Ṛṣabha’s eldest son Bharata rules righteously, pleasing subjects, then entrusts kingdom to unnamed son and goes forest. At Śālagrāma āśrama he performs fierce tapas, weakens body gradually and meditates on Viṣṇu. No son-name or fixed duration stated. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.17.6–8](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

2. **mud5-event-bharata-fawn-rescue** — While Bharata makes Pitṛ offerings at river, a lion’s sudden roar frightens a pregnant doe. She leaps into river fleeing; fetus falls into water and she dies farther away from distress. Bharata compassionately rescues and carefully raises fawn; fondling/playing becomes attachment and his tapas-related acts gradually depart, as he fails to see attachment fault. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.17.9–14](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

3. **mud5-event-bharata-deer-rebirth** — Fawn goes with other deer into unknown forest. Bharata searches forest after forest, laments and dies in continued grief; at death he sees deer nearby. Because of attachment fault he is born as deer. Earlier tapas grants previous-birth memory, so he fears attachment, abandons deer company, returns former āśrama, practices nonviolence/turns from dualities, eats dry grass, remembers Viṣṇu, bathes in tīrthas and desires saintly company. This is narrated rebirth/memory mechanism, not a deity boon scene. Outcome: Deer rebirth with former-life memory; avoids attachment.. [Mudgala2.17.15–21](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

4. **mud5-event-bharata-pulaha-arrival** — Wandering in forest by earlier merit, deer-bodied Bharata sees Pulaha āśrama. Sages there perform tapas and Gaṇeśa worship; he joyfully dwells near them, devoted to saintly company. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.17.22–24](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

5. **mud5-event-bharata-heard-brahma-teaching** — Deer-bodied Bharata dwelling there hears full knowledge, holds Brahmā’s instruction to Pulaha continually in heart, and joyfully meditates on Gaṇeśa while staying there. Narrator says full knowledge; this records reception, not independent resolution of 17.42–43 dense teaching grammar. Outcome: None. [Mudgala2.18.27–29](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)

6. **mud5-event-bharata-deer-body-ending** — Bharata reflects that after leaving kingdom and remembering Hari, attachment to deer nevertheless made him deer-bodied. He attributes victory over difficult obstacles by Śiva/Viṣṇu-led gods/all beings to Vighnarāja grace. He praises earlier Vaiṣṇava devotion for saṃskāra leading to excellent knowledge, and fortunate meeting Gaṇapatya yogin Pulaha/ongoing saint company, now worshipping Gaṇeśa without obstruction. In time, devoted to Gaṇeśa and meditating on him, he abandons deer body. No new birth narrated within 18 and no express new liberation grant. Outcome: Meditating on Gaṇeśa, Bharata abandons deer body; next life outside bounded arc.. [Mudgala2.18.30–37](https://sanskritdocuments.org/doc_purana/mudgalapurANam2.html)
